# Owlshift — build plan for P0 and P1

Status: draft, 2026-09-27. What to build first, in order: the foundations (P0), then one ticket turned into a verified pull request on demand (P1). The steps after that are described in the [roadmap](roadmap.md).

## Checks to run live before writing code

Seventeen assumptions carry the design, C9 to C17 added for the GitHub App sign-in of OWL-336; each is checked with a real call before the code that depends on it, and the dated result is written down next to the decision it settles.

| # | Assumption | Depends on it | Check | Before |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | `claude -p` runs headless on the user's own subscription login, with no API key, and honours `--model`, `--effort`, `--permission-mode` and structured output inside a worktree | The whole executor | One tiny run in a scratch repository | P0 |
| C2 | `codex exec` runs headless on a ChatGPT subscription login, gives structured output, accepts a reasoning-effort setting, runs under Owlshift's own confinement with its sandbox off (OWL-296), and exits non-zero on failure | Codex reviewers | One tiny run | P5; passed on 2026-10-15 (OWL-297) |
| C3 | GitHub accepts pushes to a custom ref namespace, and creating an existing ref is rejected, so a claim is atomic | Claims | Two concurrent pushes to a test repository | P0 |
| C4 | Linear exposes a comment's last-edit time; an issue can be created directly in Triage with an API key; an app user works with polling only | S2 quiet window, S13, D8 | API calls on the Owlshift workspace | P2 |
| C5 | Optional reading, not a gate: Sortie's and Symphony's `WORKFLOW.md` and their adapter pitfalls | D5, Jira adapter | Read their docs and adapter code | P0 |
| C6 | `owlshift` is free where it matters: crates.io and npm were free on 2026-09-27; the GitHub name `owlshift` is taken, so the repository lives on the maintainer's personal account (`pitchopp/owlshift`), and `owlshift.dev` still needs a registrar check | D3 | Registrar check | P0 |
| C7 | Each provider's terms allow unattended headless use of a personal subscription through its own CLI; each CLI reports a reached usage limit in a detectable way (message, reset time, exit status) | Subscription-first design, S10, S17 | Read the current terms; trigger or capture a limit message for each CLI | P0 for Claude, P5 for Codex; Codex read on 2026-10-15 (OWL-297) |
| C8 | Each harness CLI reports whether it is logged in through a status command, readable without Owlshift touching a credential | `owlshift doctor` (OWL-12) | Run each status command logged in, logged out and on an API key | P0 |
| C9 | A GitHub App's device flow gives a device code to its client ID alone, with no client secret on the user's machine | `owlshift init`'s sign-in (OWL-336) | `scripts/github-app-checks.sh` with no flag: `POST login/device/code` with the client ID only. Required | This design (OWL-336); passed on 2026-10-19 |
| C10 | The token endpoint answers the device flow's polls as the standard says: `authorization_pending` before the code is entered, `slow_down` inside the interval, the token once entered, `access_denied` on Cancel, `expired_token` past the code's life | `init`'s polling and its messages (OWL-336) | The script with no flag (pending, slow_down, token), `--check C10-denied` (required) and `--check C10-expired` (optional, may be recorded 'not observed') | This design (OWL-336); passed on 2026-10-19, C10-expired not observed |
| C11 | Where the app is installed, its user token makes every call of the forge adapter: the default branch, `createPullRequest`, the pull request read with `statusCheckRollup`, a check run by id and an Actions job log by id (both ids from the commit's check-runs answer), reviews and comments; the pull request's author and its "via app" mark, the `X-Accepted-GitHub-Permissions` header and the token's `X-RateLimit-Limit` are recorded | The forge adapter, watch, review intake (OWL-336) | The script with no flag, on a scratch repository with the app installed, a workflow run on the commit and a pushed branch; it closes the pull request it opens. Required | This design (OWL-336); passed on 2026-10-19, the rollup with check runs and the author unverified |
| C12 | A user token finds the app's installations and their repositories (`user/installations`, `user/installations/<id>/repositories`), each with the `app_slug` it belongs to | `doctor`'s app line, `init`'s wait for the installation (OWL-336) | The script with no flag. Required | This design (OWL-336); passed on 2026-10-19 |
| C13 | With user-token expiration on: the token's `expires_in`, a refresh token, whether the refresh grant is accepted with the client ID alone (no client secret), whether it rotates the refresh token, what a reused refresh token and two refreshes at once get. **Its result picks the design's token-lifetime branch**: refresh without the secret, or expiration off | Section 8's token lifetime, D13, the token-failure rule (OWL-336) | `--check C13`. Required | This design (OWL-336); passed on 2026-10-19, refresh without the secret |
| C14 | With user-token expiration off: no `expires_in` comes back, and the token still works after C13's interval | The non-expiring token, if C13 needs it (OWL-336) | `--check C14 --token-file FILE`, then `--check C14 --resume FILE` after C13's interval. Required, with its resume run | This design (OWL-336); the sign-in passed on 2026-10-19, the resume not observed, by the maintainer's choice |
| C15 | What a user token sees when the app is not installed on the repository (C15-none), installed elsewhere only (C15-elsewhere), or requested on an organisation repository and not yet approved (C15-pending); the install link `https://github.com/apps/<slug>/installations/new` opens the app's install page | `init`'s install link, `doctor`'s app line, the pending-request message (OWL-336) | `--check C15-none` and `--check C15-elsewhere` (required); `--check C15-pending` (optional, may be recorded 'not observed') | This design (OWL-336); passed on 2026-10-19, C15-pending not observed |
| C16 | A suspended installation (C16-suspended), and a permission the app raised but the installation has not accepted (C16-permissions), show as refused reads | `doctor`'s per-permission probes (OWL-336) | `--check C16-suspended` and `--check C16-permissions`. Optional, may be recorded 'not observed' | This design (OWL-336); not observed on 2026-10-19 |
| C17 | A revoked authorization refuses the token (401) and its refresh | `doctor`'s token line, "sign in again" (OWL-336) | `--check C17`. Optional, may be recorded 'not observed' | This design (OWL-336); not observed on 2026-10-19 |

### Results

Checks run on 2026-09-28 (OWL-6) with Claude Code 2.1.283 and git 2.54 on macOS. Account identifiers are left out on purpose.

**C1 — passed, with one guardrail finding.** Seven tiny `claude -p` runs in a linked worktree of a scratch repository, each launched with `env -i HOME PATH USER LANG`: no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, no `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, no parent session variables.

- Login: `claude auth status` reports `authMethod: claude.ai` on a Max subscription; the stream's `init` event reports `apiKeySource: none`.
- `--model haiku` and `--model sonnet` reach the named model (`modelUsage`). `--effort` is applied: the same prompt used 0 thinking tokens at `low` and 2,389 at `max`.
- `--permission-mode dontAsk` denied Write and Bash (`permission_denials`, no file created); `acceptEdits` wrote the file inside the worktree. `--json-schema` returns the object in `structured_output`.
- Failure: an unknown model exits with status 1 and `is_error: true`, `api_error_status: 404`, `terminal_reason: "api_error"`, yet `subtype: "success"`. The harness adapter reads `is_error` and the exit status, never `subtype`.
- `--bare` cannot be used: according to `claude --help`, its authentication is strictly `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `apiKeyHelper`, and the subscription login (OAuth, keychain) is never read.
- **Guardrail finding.** By default a headless run loads the user's own configuration: hooks, `CLAUDE.md`, plugins and every MCP server, including claude.ai connectors to Linear, Slack and Gmail. That hands the agent tracker credentials, against the rule that agents get none. Verified mitigation, in one of the seven runs: with `--setting-sources project,local --strict-mcp-config`, the `init` event lists no MCP server, no user hook fires (the default run showed hook events), and the run succeeds on the subscription login (`apiKeySource: none`). Credentials outside Claude Code, such as the `gh` login in the system keyring, stay reachable from the agent's shell: the agent environment closes that path (OWL-22, below). Since OWL-53, below, the adapter loads no settings file at all.
- **Account tools (OWL-42), checked on 2026-09-28 with Claude Code 2.1.283.** Eight tiny runs on `haiku` at effort `low` in a scratch repository, launched with `env -i HOME PATH USER LANG TMPDIR` and the adapter's exact argv: `claude -p --output-format stream-json --verbose`, then `--permission-mode dontAsk --allowedTools 'Edit(./.owlshift/result.json)'` (read-only) or `--permission-mode acceptEdits --allowedTools Bash` (write in worktree), then `--permission-prompts none`, then `--setting-sources project,local --strict-mcp-config --no-session-persistence`, the prompt on standard input.
  - Offered: under both modes the `init` tool list has 29 entries, `RemoteTrigger` among them, next to `CronCreate`, `CronDelete`, `CronList`, `DesignSync`, `EnterWorktree`, `ExitWorktree`, `ListAgents`, `Monitor`, `PushNotification`, `ReportFindings`, `ScheduleWakeup`, `SendMessage`, `Workflow` and the task tools. The OWL-14 fixtures, recorded with the same flags, already listed it.
  - Callable: asked to call it once with its read-only `list` action and nothing else, the model loaded it through `ToolSearch` (`select:RemoteTrigger`) and called `RemoteTrigger {"action":"list"}`, which answered `HTTP 200 {"data":[],"has_more":false}` from the account, with no permission denial, under `acceptEdits` and under `dontAsk` alike: no mode asks before this tool. Every call in the eight runs had the input `{"action":"list"}`, so nothing was created or scheduled, and the second listing, made after the first, still found no trigger.
  - Denied: with `--disallowedTools RemoteTrigger` the tool leaves the `init` list, and the model can neither call it nor load it (`ToolSearch` answers "No matching deferred tools found"). It holds in one flag with the web tools (`--disallowedTools RemoteTrigger WebFetch WebSearch`, followed by `--json-schema`), and against an allow rule for the tool: given `--settings '{"permissions":{"allow":["RemoteTrigger"]}}'`, loaded without a warning, the tool stayed out. The same rule in the project's `.claude/settings.json` was not a test of precedence: the CLI ignored it, saying on standard error "Ignoring 1 permissions.allow entry from .claude/settings.json: this workspace has not been trusted". A worktree the user never opened interactively is untrusted the same way, so its project allow rules do not apply. A trusted worktree's allow rule was not tried here; OWL-46, below, tried it.
  - Decision: the adapter removes `RemoteTrigger` from every launch, in its one `--disallowedTools` flag, whatever the permission level and network access. Its contract test replayed the `--settings` run (`remote_trigger_denied.jsonl`); OWL-46's test and recording, below, now cover it with the other tools listed above.
- **Tools reaching beyond the run (OWL-46), checked on 2026-09-28 with Claude Code 2.1.283.** Tiny runs on `haiku` at effort `low` with OWL-42's argv and environment, in a linked worktree of a scratch repository. Every run that could reach a tool under test also carried, for the probe only, a `--settings` PreToolUse hook that logged each call and blocked every tool but the ones under test; it was first seen blocking a `Read`, and refusals from the permission mode still came through it. The CLI updated itself to 2.1.284 during the session; every result below comes from the 2.1.283 binary, run by its path. The tool definitions in that binary were read too (`strings`) where a run could not show a tool safely.
  - Reaching beyond the run, so denied:
    - `PushNotification`, loaded with `ToolSearch`, not called: it "sends a desktop notification in the user's terminal. If Remote Control is connected, it also pushes to their phone". The binary marks it read-only, so no permission mode asks before it.
    - `DesignSync`, loaded, not called: it reads and updates the user's claude.ai/design projects through the claude.ai login, with methods such as `write_files`, `delete_files` and `create_project`.
    - `Workflow`, loaded, not run: it runs a script that orchestrates subagents in the background. Its schema in these runs has no remote parameter, but the binary carries a remote launch ("Workflow launched in a remote CCR session", on a clone of the pushed branch). A workflow spawns agents, so none was run, and the tool is denied by default.
    - `CronCreate`: a session-only one-shot job (`durable: false`) was accepted under `dontAsk` with no denial ("Session-only (not written to disk, dies when Claude exits)"); `claude -p` exited 8 s later and the job died with it. With `durable: true` the job is written to `.claude/scheduled_tasks.json` (tool description and binary), a schedule meant to outlive the run; not tried. `CronList` and `CronDelete` listed and cancelled a session job with no denial. They also act on that durable file, whose location for a linked worktree (the CLI's project root) was not established without writing one, so they are denied with `CronCreate`.
    - `ListAgents`, called under `dontAsk`: no denial, and it listed five other Claude sessions running on the machine (Claude Desktop sessions, idle or busy); its description adds the account's cloud and Remote Control sessions. `SendMessage`, loaded, not called, sends to any of them.
    - `EnterWorktree`, called under `dontAsk`: no denial; it created a worktree under the scratch main checkout's `.claude/worktrees/`, on a new branch, and moved the session there. `ExitWorktree` then removed that worktree and branch and moved the session back. It acts only on a worktree `EnterWorktree` made, so it goes with it.
    - The `Agent` tool, listed as `Task` in `init`, offers `isolation: "worktree"` or `"remote"`. With `worktree`, under `dontAsk` and with no denial, a subagent ran in a worktree created under the main checkout's `.claude/worktrees/`, removed afterwards because the subagent changed nothing. `remote` "launches the agent in a remote cloud environment (always runs in background; availability is gated)"; not tried, since it would start a cloud session on the account. `--disallowedTools Agent` and `--disallowedTools Task` both remove the tool.
  - Staying inside the run, so kept:
    - `Monitor` has Bash's permission. Under `dontAsk` it ran `echo`, a read-only command that `dontAsk` lets Bash run too, and was refused `touch` and a WebSocket source (`ws://127.0.0.1:9/`, refused before any connection); under `acceptEdits` it ran `echo`. Its process belongs to the run's process tree.
    - `ScheduleWakeup`: a 60 s wakeup was accepted under `dontAsk` with no denial; `claude -p` exited 6 s later and the wakeup died with the process.
  - Trusted worktree. A linked worktree's trust is keyed on its main checkout's path: the untrusted warning names `projects["<main checkout>"]`. A worktree of a checkout the user trusts is therefore trusted, and the allow rules of its `.claude/settings.json` apply. `CLAUDE_CODE_SANDBOXED=1` did not count as trusted, and a trust entry in the user's `~/.claude.json` would change the user's security settings, so the check ran with a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` whose `.claude.json` trusts the scratch main checkout. That configuration has no login: each run stops at "Not logged in" after the `init` event, and no model ran. With the worktree allowing every denied tool and one Bash command, no warning was printed, while the same configuration without the trust entry printed "Ignoring 13 permissions.allow entries from .claude/settings.json: this workspace has not been trusted". With the deny list, none of the ten denied tools such a run offers came back; without it, all ten were listed. `PushNotification` and `RemoteTrigger` are not offered without a login, so this cannot show them; for `RemoteTrigger`, OWL-42's `--settings` result stands. Whether such an allow rule widens a role was left open; OWL-53, below, found that it does.
  - Decision: the adapter removes, next to `RemoteTrigger` and in the same flag, whatever the permission level and network access: `PushNotification`, `DesignSync`, `Workflow`, `CronCreate`, `CronDelete`, `CronList`, `SendMessage`, `ListAgents`, `EnterWorktree`, `ExitWorktree` and `Agent`. Removing `Agent` takes subagents away from every role; the default build role does not use them. The contract test `tools_reaching_beyond_the_run_are_denied_on_every_launch` checks the flag for both levels, with and without network, and replays `tests/fixtures/claude/beyond_run_tools_denied.jsonl`. That run used the write-in-worktree argv with no network and a JSON Schema, plus a probe-only `--settings` holding an allow rule for each denied tool and the blocking hook: none of the tools was in `init`, `ToolSearch` loaded none of the deferred ones ("No matching deferred tools found"), and the model called nothing else.
  - Seen in passing, left to follow-ups: `init` carries a `messaging_socket_path`, through which the user's other sessions can message a run (OWL-52, below); and a monitor's notification made one run continue after its `result` record, with a second `init` and `result`, of which the adapter keeps the last.
- **Messages from the user's other sessions (OWL-52), checked on 2026-09-29 with Claude Code 2.1.283 and 2.1.284.** Tiny runs in a scratch directory under `$TMPDIR`, launched with `env -i HOME PATH USER LANG TMPDIR`. A receiver ran the adapter's argv, write in worktree with network, on `haiku` at effort `low` with `--max-turns 6`, and was asked to run a 60 s Bash loop, then to report any message another session had sent. While it waited, a sender, a second `claude -p` on `sonnet` offered `SendMessage` alone (`--tools SendMessage`), sent it a probe text at `uds:<socket>`; a probe-only `--settings` hook let through that one call and nothing else, and blocked a 2.1.283 sender that tried `"main"` instead. The 2.1.283 sender's model declined twice to use a `uds:` address, which its tool description does not mention, so the recorded refusal pairs a 2.1.283 receiver with a 2.1.284 sender.
  - Open: every run's `init` reports a `messaging_socket_path`, a live socket at `/tmp/cc-socks/<pid>.sock` here (`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` unset), and the run registers under `sessions/<pid>.json` in the configuration folder, where other sessions look for it. Both were gone once the run exited.
  - Reachable: with the argv as it was, `SendMessage` answered "queued there", and the receiver's final answer was the probe text, on both versions. The stream does not echo the text: only a `command_lifecycle` pair follows the Bash result, so the executor cannot tell that it happened. The binary (`strings`) explains the delivery: with no setting, a message is delivered when the sender's permission class, prompting or bypass, matches the receiver's, and `acceptEdits` and `dontAsk` runs prompt, like the user's ordinary sessions.
  - Refused: with `--settings '{"crossSessionInbound":"refuse"}'` added, the socket stays open and `SendMessage` still answers "queued", then the sender gets a warning, "Cross-session message refused … it was not delivered", and the receiver answered NONE, with no `command_lifecycle` event. The same held for a read-only receiver (`dontAsk`, which refused its Bash loop, so it ended within seconds) and for a receiver whose `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` both set `crossSessionInbound` to `accept`. Those files are read although the folder was never trusted: set to `hold` without the flag, a message was held (`peer_message_hold`, cause `repo-setting`) and discarded when the run ended. The CLI's settings schema documents the setting: "'accept' delivers them, 'hold' parks them for your review without letting Claude act, 'refuse' opts this session out". Per the binary, managed settings come first, then `--settings`, then user settings, and project and local settings can only tighten the result; managed settings were not tried. Since OWL-53, below, the adapter reads neither file.
  - Switched off: with `CLAUDE_CODE_HARBOR_KITE=0` in the environment, `init` reports no socket and none is bound. The binary reads the variable as the override of a feature gate (`tengu_harbor_kite`, on by default) and checks it before any setting; it is not documented. `--bare` also skips the inbox, and cannot be used (above).
  - Decision: the adapter passes `--settings '{"crossSessionInbound":"refuse"}'` on every launch, whatever the permission level and network access. It holds whatever the executor's environment turns out to do. The contract test `messages_from_other_sessions_are_refused_on_every_launch` checks the flag for both levels, with and without network, and replays `tests/fixtures/claude/peer_message_refused.jsonl` (receiver) and `peer_message_sender.jsonl` (sender): the sender's one call went to the receiver's socket and was refused, and the probe text is nowhere in the receiver's stream.
  - What stays open: the socket is still bound and registered, so the run can be found and only its messages are refused. Managed settings outrank `--settings`, as they outrank every flag Owlshift passes, and they can also add hooks that put text before the model. The runs went outside the executor's own environment: its agent login folder as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, where the run registers, and the OWL-41 sandbox, which closes the shared temporary folders where the socket was bound. Either may keep the inbox from working at all; not tried. `CLAUDE_CODE_HARBOR_KITE=0` in the agent environment would remove the socket; left to a follow-up.
- **Messages from other sessions, in the executor's own environment (OWL-65), checked on 2026-09-29 with Claude Code 2.1.284, and 2.1.283 where named.** OWL-52's open item, run with the command the executor spawns: a throwaway program called `AgentEnv::from_runner`, `ClaudeHarness::sandbox_needs`, `claude::command` and `AgentEnv::confine`, so the receiver had the agent environment, the agent login folder as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (under a scratch `OWLSHIFT_CONFIG_DIR`), the write-in-worktree argv with network, and the OWL-41 profile built from the run's paths as `Executor::run` builds them: a linked worktree of a scratch repository, its common git folder, a hidden run folder, the harness's install and login folders, and a temporary folder made per run under the system one, 0700. No agent login exists on this machine yet, so the model was a local stand-in, as for OWL-53: the login folder held a made-up claude.ai login, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, passed as a declared and allowed gate variable, named the stand-in, and every other request went to a dead proxy. The stand-in played one Bash call (`sleep 5`), answered it only after 20 s so the run stayed up, then answered DONE. It logged every request the CLI sent, which is the check: a message delivered to the run is in the request that follows. The send was made about 3 s after `init`, while the stand-in still held its first answer, so a second request always followed it. The sender was a second `claude -p`, unconfined, with its own configuration folder and stand-in, calling `ListAgents` and then `SendMessage` to `uds:<path>`, the path from the receiver's `init` or, without one, `/tmp/cc-socks/<pid>.sock`.
  - Bare, without the refuse flag (the control): the socket was `/tmp/cc-socks/<pid>.sock`, and the run registered under `sessions/<pid>.json` in the agent login folder, not in `~/.claude`. `ListAgents` from the sender's folder answered "No reachable agents", yet the message sent to the path was delivered: the probe text was in the receiver's next request.
  - Confined, with the flag or without: `init` reports no `messaging_socket_path`, the process holds no Unix socket (`lsof`), its registration names none, and a send fails with `ENOENT`. The binary binds the socket under `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, which an agent does not inherit, else under `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`, else under `/tmp`, and the sandbox closes `/private/tmp`.
  - Found on the way, left to a follow-up: under the same profile, Claude Code's Bash tool fails before it runs anything, with `EPERM: operation not permitted, open '/private/tmp/claude-<uid>/<working directory>'`. The CLI keeps a folder per project under `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`, else under `/tmp`, so a confined role cannot run a command on macOS. Settled by OWL-100 (below): the executor now sets `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` to a folder of the run's own.
  - Reopened: with `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` naming the run's own temporary folder, as a fix for Bash would set it, Bash ran, the socket was bound at `<run temporary folder>/cc-socks/<pid>.sock` and registered, and the unconfined sender's message was delivered to the confined run. That path was 90 bytes; past 103 the binary falls back to `/tmp/cc-socks-<uid>` (read in the binary, not run). With the flag as shipped, the send still answered "queued" and nothing was delivered.
  - Switched off: with `CLAUDE_CODE_HARBOR_KITE=0` added to that reopened run, without the flag, no socket was bound, nothing was delivered, and Bash still ran. With the adapter's argv as shipped, unconfined, the variable left `init` without a socket on 2.1.283 and 2.1.284; the run registered all the same, with no socket named.
  - Decision: `AgentEnv::from_runner` sets `CLAUDE_CODE_HARBOR_KITE=0` beside `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (`owlshift_adapters::harness::claude::PEER_INBOX_ENV`), and the adapter keeps the refuse flag. On macOS the sandbox keeps the inbox shut today only as a side effect of closing `/tmp`, the same one that stops Bash: a fix for Bash that points `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` at the run's temporary folder reopens the inbox to the user's other sessions unless this variable stays (the reopened run above). Since OWL-100 (below) the executor sets `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` so, and this variable is what keeps the socket unbound. In every configuration tried, confined or not and with the flag or without, the variable removed the socket; the flag is the documented layer, and it refused every message sent to an open socket here, so it is what remains if a release drops the variable, which is not documented. Neither was tried against managed settings (below). A project that declares the name, with the operator allowing it, still gets `0`: the runner sets it after the declared variables. Only `from_runner`, which `owlshift do` uses, sets it; `AgentEnv::new` and `AgentEnv::for_project`, used by the test bench, do not. The contract test `the_agent_environment_switches_the_peer_inbox_off` replays a matched pair recorded on 2.1.283 with the stand-in, unconfined, with the adapter's argv and the agent environment, differing only in the variable: `init` names a socket in `tests/fixtures/claude/peer_inbox_open.jsonl` and none in `peer_inbox_switched_off.jsonl`. `crates/owlshift-runner/tests/peer_inbox.rs` checks that `from_runner` sets the variable to `0`, also when the runner's own environment sets it to `1` and the project declares it, and that the confined command carries it whatever the harness's own command asked for.
  - What stays open: no real model ran, for want of an agent login on this machine; the check is worth repeating once one exists. Managed settings were not tried: the public build reads `CLAUDE_CODE_MANAGED_SETTINGS_PATH` but does not use it (a managed file there set to `refuse` did not stop a delivery), and the real folder, `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode`, needs an administrator, so whether managed settings can turn the variable back on was not established. Linux was not run: bwrap gives the run a private `/tmp`, where the socket would be bound out of the other sessions' reach.
- **Repository settings (OWL-53), checked on 2026-09-29 with Claude Code 2.1.283 and 2.1.284.** Runs in a linked worktree of a scratch repository, with the adapter's read-only argv but for `--setting-sources`, under `env -i` with a scratch `HOME` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, whose `.claude.json` trusted the main checkout or not. The model was a local stand-in for the Messages API (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` on 127.0.0.1, a made-up API key) playing scripted tool calls: Write to the result file, Bash (`touch`), Write to another file. The CLI decides whether a call runs, so a scripted call tests that decision exactly, with no login and no usage; it cannot show what a real model would try. The key runs were repeated on 2.1.284 with the same outcome.
  - Widened, under `project,local`. In a trusted worktree, `permissions.allow` of `Bash` and `Write` in `.claude/settings.json` let the read-only role run both, and the same rule in `.claude/settings.local.json` alone did too. In an untrusted worktree the allow rules were ignored, with OWL-42's warning, but the repository's `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit` and `PreToolUse` hooks ran, and a `PreToolUse` hook printing `permissionDecision: "allow"` let the role run Bash and Write with no denial. Owlshift's runs are the untrusted case, their login folder trusting no directory, and the worktree is the ticket's branch, which agents write: one run could plant settings for the next. The worktree's `CLAUDE.md`, a `.claude/rules` file and a skill reached the model's request; a command and an agent were listed in `init`.
  - Closed, under `--setting-sources ""`. With every vector at once in a trusted worktree (allow rules in both files, hooks in both, one approving every call), the result file was written, Bash and the other Write were denied, no hook ran, and none of the five files above was loaded; the same held untrusted. The binary reads the empty value as no source and always adds the `--settings` and managed sources: an allow rule for Bash given with `--settings` still let it run. The write-in-worktree argv still wrote in the worktree and ran Bash. A made-up claude.ai login in the configuration folder's `.credentials.json`, with no API key and every other request sent to a dead proxy, gave `apiKeySource: none` and a completed run. OWL-52's inbox check, repeated with the stand-in and the new flag, the repository's files set to `accept`: a probe sent with `SendMessage` reached a receiver without the refuse flag and never one with it. The configuration's legacy per-project `allowedTools` in `.claude.json` did not widen the role either.
  - Decision: the adapter passes `--setting-sources ""` on every launch, so no user, project or local settings file applies; `--settings` and managed settings still do. Claude Code therefore no longer loads the worktree's `CLAUDE.md`, rules, skills, commands or agents (its own bundled skills still loaded, until OWL-333, below): the brief carries the project's rules (OWL-61, below). The contract test `repository_settings_cannot_widen_a_role_on_every_launch` checks the flag for both levels, with and without network, and replays `tests/fixtures/claude/repo_settings_ignored.jsonl`, the every-vector run on 2.1.283 (the stand-in's API key shows as `apiKeySource`). `tests/claude_live.rs` now plants hooks in both files and fails if their marker appears; with the stand-in, that planting left the marker under `project,local` and not under the new flag.
  - What stays open: no real model on a subscription login has run the new flag; `claude_live.rs`, run by the maintainer, is that check. Of the settings keys, only permission rules and hooks were observed; the others (`env`, `apiKeyHelper`) live in the same files and are left out with them. OWL-41's finding that `.claude/settings.local.json` widened Claude Code's own sandbox came from the local source; not re-run. Managed settings, set by an administrator, outrank every flag.
- **Contract on Claude Code 2.1.284 (OWL-77), checked on 2026-09-29.** Every recorded fixture was re-recorded on 2.1.284 by `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/claude_record.rs` (`OWLSHIFT_RECORD_CLAUDE=1 cargo test -p owlshift-adapters --test claude_record -- --ignored`), which starts each run from the adapter's own command line, in C1's environment, checks the version, exit status, standard error and the behaviour each contract test relies on, then scrubs and writes the fixture; `usage_limit.jsonl` stays constructed. The OWL-46, OWL-52 and OWL-53 setups were replayed as recorded above: the peer pair ran together, both on 2.1.284, and `repo_settings_ignored.jsonl` again came from the local stand-in, whose control run under `project,local` ran the repository's hooks and all three calls.
  - Held: the contract tests pass unchanged on the new recordings, but for the exact values of `success`, and `tests/claude_live.rs` passed on the installed 2.1.284, a real model on the subscription login running `--setting-sources ""`, which closes OWL-53's first open item. 2.1.284 joins 2.1.283 in `harness/tested.rs`; the adapter did not change.
  - Changed, harmless to the adapter: `init` now lists the user's `agents`, `plugins`, `skills`, `slash_commands`, `terminal_slash_commands` and `memory_paths` (local paths) even under `--setting-sources ""`, and a `capabilities` list; the recorder drops the first six. The skills listed were invocable: OWL-333, below. Every run's `init` reports a `messaging_socket_path`, the read-only ones included. The `result` record gained timing fields (`ttft_ms`, `time_to_request_ms` and others). The success run's rate-limit event was an `allowed_warning` on the seven-day window, which the adapter already reads.
- **The read-only result after a `cd` (OWL-323), checked on 2026-10-17 with Claude Code 2.1.300.** Found on the first Locary run (`owlshift do LOC-1407`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`): design attempt 1 (run `20261016T152140Z`) had its `Write` of `<worktree>/.owlshift/run/result.json` denied by `dontAsk` (`permission_denials`), attempt 2 (`20261016T152333Z`) made the same call and it passed. In attempt 1's stream the last Bash call the mode allowed was `cd "<worktree>/backend"; …`; attempt 2's only `cd` was in a call the mode denied. The model then ended on prose and the result in a fenced block, and the run failed as `NoResult`. Cause: a `path` or `./path` rule is relative to the current directory (Claude Code's permissions page, read that day), and a Bash `cd` moves it, so `Edit(./.owlshift/run/result.json)` followed the shell into `backend/`. Tiny runs on `haiku` at effort `low` with the adapter's read-only argv, under `env -i HOME PATH USER LANG TMPDIR` with `CLAUDE_CODE_HARBOR_KITE=0`, in a scratch folder whose path holds a space, unconfined, on the user's own login; the model ran one Bash command, then wrote one file:
  - Rule as shipped: with no `cd`, the result was written; after `cd sub && pwd`, its `Write` was denied, as in Locary; and after the same `cd`, a `Write` of `sub/.owlshift/run/result.json` was allowed, a file in the worktree a read-only role could write.
  - Not a fix: `Write(./…)` beside the `Edit(./…)` rule, still denied after the `cd`; an absolute `Edit(//<path>)` rule, denied, since `--allowedTools` splits its value on spaces and every macOS worktree lies under `Application Support`.
  - Fixes: the documented `CLAUDE_BASH_MAINTAIN_PROJECT_WORKING_DIR=1`, which brings the shell back after each command, let the result be written after the `cd` and denied `sub/…`. A rule anchored with one slash, `Edit(/.owlshift/run/result.json)`, which a command-line flag anchors at the session's primary working directory (same page), did too: after the `cd` the result was written and `sub/.owlshift/run/result.json` denied, and without it the result was written.
  - Write in worktree is not affected: under `acceptEdits` plus Bash, a `Write` at the worktree's root after `cd sub` was allowed.
  - Decision: the read-only rule is `Edit(/<result path>)`, a flag of the adapter's own, rather than the variable, which would change Build's shell too. A read-only run that still leaves no result is read from its last message (architecture section 6, "Harness contract"). The adapter's unit test pins the rule; that it refuses `sub/…` is recorded since OWL-327 (below).
  - What stays open: the sandbox and the run's own configuration folder with the token login were not rerun, since neither changes how a rule is anchored; native Windows was not run, agent runs being refused there (D9). The last-message fallback takes a final fenced block at its word: a run that wrote no file and ended on a quoted result, such as its prompt's example, would have that result read, still validated against its brief.
- **Contract on Claude Code 2.1.300 (OWL-327), recorded on 2026-10-17.**
  The installed `claude` had updated itself to 2.1.302, and its updater prunes old binaries.
  So the recording ran 2.1.300 through a shim:
  a copy of `~/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.300` in a scratch folder, linked there as `claude`,
  that folder first on `PATH`, then OWL-77's command.
  The recorder keeps `PATH`, so the shim is the `claude` each run starts; its version check passed on every run.
  - Recorded as OWL-77 did, with the OWL-52 peer pair and the OWL-53 stand-in.
    The `peer_inbox_*` pair, made by hand on 2.1.283 with no recorder, stays as it is.
  - Two new fixtures replay OWL-323's scenario, in a folder whose path holds a space:
    a read-only run runs `cd sub && pwd`, then writes the result, then the same name below `sub`.
    - With the adapter's argv (`read_only_after_cd.jsonl`): the result is written, `sub/…` is denied.
    - With the rule edited back to `Edit(./…)` (`read_only_after_cd_dot_rule.jsonl`): the result is denied, `sub/…` is written.
    - Contract tests `the_read_only_result_survives_a_cd` and `a_dot_rule_follows_the_shell_after_a_cd` replay them.
  - Seen while recording: the prompt first named the folder through `/var/folders`, a link to `/private/var/folders`.
    Both writes were then denied, under either rule; with the resolved path, the runs above.
    Likely cause, not probed further: rules are matched on resolved paths, and a write named through a link misses them.
  - Changed, harmless to the adapter: `haiku` resolves to `claude-haiku-5-5`, and `result` gained `safety_stops`.
    The success run's rate-limit event was a plain `allowed`; a constructed line keeps the `allowed_warning` read tested.
  - 2.1.300 joins `harness/tested.rs`. The adapter did not change.
- **Contract on Claude Code 2.1.302 (OWL-338), recorded on 2026-10-19.**
  The installed `claude` had updated itself to 2.1.302 two days before, the day OWL-327 recorded 2.1.300, so doctor flagged it as untested and `tests/claude_live.rs` failed on it.
  The maintainer re-recorded every fixture the recorder writes with `OWLSHIFT_RECORD_CLAUDE=1 cargo test -p owlshift-adapters --test claude_record -- --ignored` on the installed 2.1.302, no shim needed: all eight recordings passed their checks, the two read-only `cd` ones included (`read_only_after_cd.jsonl`: the result written, `sub/…` denied; `read_only_after_cd_dot_rule.jsonl`: the reverse).
  `peer_inbox_*` (2.1.283, by hand) and `usage_limit.jsonl` (constructed) stay as they are.
  - Drift from 2.1.300, found by diffing the fixtures' event kinds and fields:

    A few recordings differ in their count of events and in their thinking events (`beyond_run_tools_denied.jsonl` has none beyond the result's token detail, while four others still carry them): the model's own steps, not a change of the CLI.

    `beyond_run_tools_denied.jsonl` and `peer_message_refused.jsonl`, the two write-in-worktree recordings, now carry the OWL-346 command line: `permissionMode` is `bypassPermissions` where it was `acceptEdits`, and their `tool_result_meta` `reason_type` is `mode` (`rule` in the 2.1.300 `peer_message_refused.jsonl`, absent in the other).
    That is the argv change the OWL-346 entry announced (the recorder takes the new argv at its next run), not a change of the CLI.
    The denials are the same in number and place, the `cd` ones included.
    The exact values of `success` (tokens, cost, duration, rate-limit utilization) moved with the new run; its rate-limit event is again a plain `allowed`.
    On the OWL-346 item that the peer inbox was not rerun in the bypass class: `peer_message_refused.jsonl` is now a recording in that class. It shows the receiver, refuse flag set, ending with `NONE` under `bypassPermissions` while the sender got the `Cross-session message refused` notice, both of which the recorder asserts.
    It is one macOS run on `haiku`: it does not settle the item for other roles, platforms or models, and `peer_inbox_open.jsonl` and `peer_inbox_switched_off.jsonl` were not rerun.
    None of it touches what the adapter reads.
  - 2.1.302 joins `harness/tested.rs`. The adapter did not change.
- **Skills in an agent run (OWL-333), checked on 2026-10-17 with Claude Code 2.1.302.**
  Found on Locary's LOC-1407 (2.1.300): every run's `init` listed 19 skills and offered `Skill` under `--setting-sources ""`; none called it.
  Runs through the executor, confined, on the token for agent runs and an empty `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, on `haiku` at effort `low`,
  one read-only and one write-in-worktree role, each told to invoke a listed skill, then write its result.
  - The premise was wrong in part: the 19 were Claude Code's bundled skills (`dataviz` among them), with 52 slash commands.
    None of the operator's own skills was listed: a confined run's configuration folder is empty, so `~/.claude/skills` is not its folder.
    What OWL-77 saw under the operator's own home was not rerun.
  - Without a new flag, both runs called `Skill` on `dataviz` and got the skill's text: no error, no permission denial.
    An instruction Owlshift did not put in the brief reached the model.
  - With `--disable-slash-commands` ("Disable all skills" in `--help`), both runs' `init` listed no skill and no slash command,
    `Skill` was not offered, and the model's search for it found nothing.
    `--disallowedTools Skill` was not chosen: it would remove the tool and leave the listing.
  - Decision: the adapter passes `--disable-slash-commands` on every launch (`SKILL_ARGS`).
    The contract test `bundled_skills_are_disabled_on_every_launch` pins the flag on both levels, with and without network;
    the fixtures were recorded before it, so the CLI's behaviour under it is checked by the live test
    `bundled_skills_do_not_reach_a_confined_run` in `owlshift-testkit/tests/executor_live.rs`, which passed on both levels.
    No role prompt or brief sends a slash command.
  - Every version in `harness/tested.rs` takes the flag: it is in `--help` on 2.1.284, 2.1.300 and 2.1.302,
    and on 2.1.283 too, run on 2026-10-17 from a scratch folder with `npx -y @anthropic-ai/claude-code@2.1.283 --help`.
  - What stays open: the flag's effect was observed on 2.1.302 only; the older versions were checked for the flag, not run with it.
    The Codex harness already drops skills (OWL-131).
- **`.claude/` files in a write-in-worktree run (OWL-346), checked on 2026-10-18 with Claude Code 2.1.302.**
  Found on Locary's LOC-1406 (2026-10-17/18): a Build committed most of its steps and passed the gate, then parked,
  its `Edit` of `.claude/rules/pricing-catalog.md` refused as a sensitive file, the run having "no approval surface"; `continue` met the same refusal.
  Many projects keep their rules there (OWL-67), so a ticket that changes one with the code could never finish.
  - The ticket's check, unconfined: `claude -p --model haiku --permission-prompts none --no-session-persistence --setting-sources ""`
    in a scratch git repository holding `.claude/rules/x.md` and `plain.md`, each run asked to append a line to both with the Edit tool,
    the files checked on disk after each run.

    | Flags | `.claude/rules/x.md` | `plain.md` |
    | -- | -- | -- |
    | `--permission-mode acceptEdits --allowedTools Bash` (the adapter's) | denied, as a sensitive file, no approval surface | written |
    | the same plus `--allowedTools 'Edit(/.claude/rules/**)'` | still denied | written |
    | `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` | written | written |

  - Then three runs confined as the executor confines a write-in-worktree run: the agent environment of `AgentEnv::from_runner`,
    the OWL-41 profile with the worktree and the common git folder written but for its `hooks`, `config`, `config.worktree` and `info`,
    the run's own temporary and configuration folders, and the token for agent runs on a pipe;
    `haiku` at effort `low`, with network, in a linked worktree of a scratch repository, asked for ten calls by name.
    The control ran the adapter's argv as it was; the second, `bypassPermissions` without `--allowedTools Bash`;
    the third, the same with `--settings '{"crossSessionInbound":"refuse","permissions":{"disableBypassPermissionsMode":"disable"}}'`, standing in for managed settings.
    The maintainer ran the two bypass runs, in their terminal, from a throwaway probe since removed.

    | Call | `acceptEdits` + Bash (control) | `bypassPermissions` | bypass, disabled by settings |
    | -- | -- | -- | -- |
    | Edit `.claude/rules/x.md` | denied by the CLI | written | denied |
    | Write `.claude/settings.json` | denied by the CLI | written | denied |
    | Write `plain.md` | written | written | denied |
    | Write `<git>/hooks/pre-commit` | denied by the CLI | `EPERM` (sandbox), absent | denied |
    | Write `<git>/config.worktree` | denied by the CLI | `EPERM` (sandbox), absent | denied |
    | Write `<git>/refs/heads/probe-ref` | denied by the CLI | written; isolation check: `Refs(["refs/heads/probe-ref"])` | denied |
    | Write a file beside the worktree | denied by the CLI | `EPERM` (sandbox), absent | denied |
    | Bash `printf … > .claude/rules/bash.md` | denied by the CLI | written | denied |
    | Bash `printf … >` a file beside the worktree | "operation not permitted" (sandbox) | "operation not permitted" (sandbox) | denied |
    | WebFetch `https://example.com/` | denied by the CLI | fetched | denied |

    In all three, `init` offered the same twelve tools, none of the removed ones, and no MCP server.
    The third exited 0 with nothing on standard error: only its `init` said `permissionMode: "default"`, and nothing was written.
    A plain Bash redirect was no way around the refusal either, as the control shows.
  - Read in the 2.1.302 binary: asked for `bypassPermissions` by a process running as root, outside a sandbox it recognises
    (`IS_SANDBOX=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_BUBBLEWRAP`), the CLI prints "--dangerously-skip-permissions cannot be used with root/sudo privileges for security reasons"
    and exits 1 before any event; settings with `permissions.disableBypassPermissionsMode` set to `disable` drop the mode to `default`.
  - Decision: write in worktree is `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`, with no allow rule (`claude::permission_mode`); read-only is unchanged.
    Nothing `acceptEdits` refused and bypass lets through was a guardrail of Owlshift's:
    the sandbox refuses a write outside the worktree, or to the git folder's hooks and configuration; the isolation check catches a ref;
    `--setting-sources ""` keeps a settings file or hook an agent writes from acting on this run or a later one (OWL-53);
    and Bash, which `acceptEdits` allowed, already reached every other path the sandbox opens,
    the common git folder's objects, `packed-refs` and other worktrees' metadata included.
    WebFetch now goes through when the brief allows network, which adds no reach: Build runs already had Bash with the network.
    An agent's change to `.claude/` reaches the pull request; architecture section 8 says who checks it.
  - It presumes a confined run, since no tool asks and the sandbox is then the only bound:
    `AgentEnv::from_runner`, which every agent the CLI launches gets, always confines, and `without_confinement` exists for the test bench, whose runs use the fake harness.
    The recorder (`claude_record.rs`) still runs its write-level recordings unconfined, now in bypass mode, on fixed prompts in scratch folders.
  - The guard: `claude_end` fails a run that completed in another mode than the one asked, naming the setting; a run that failed keeps its own reason.
    A write-in-worktree run that the CLI refused to start as root fails with a reason saying so.
    Unit test: `a_run_in_another_permission_mode_than_asked_fails` (`executor/harness.rs`).
  - Rejected: keeping `acceptEdits` and having Build leave the `.claude/` files to a person, named in its delivery report
    (the work lands incomplete, and a person commits the rest of every such ticket);
    keeping the park and saying in the PARKED comment what a person edits before `continue` (a park on ordinary work);
    an allow rule (the ticket's second row); `--permission-mode auto` (a classifier decides each call: not deterministic, and a model call each);
    a `--settings` hook approving `.claude/` paths (a path check in a hook program, for no reach the sandbox does not bound).
  - Tests: the unit test `a_write_run_edits_the_worktree_and_runs_bash` pins the new argv; the contract tests replay output and do not.
    The live test `a_build_edits_claude_files_and_the_runners_guards_still_hold` (`owlshift-testkit/tests/executor_live.rs`) repeats the bypass run through `Executor::run`,
    without network, so the WebFetch row rests on the probe: the two `.claude/` files are edited, on macOS the hook and a file beside the worktree get the sandbox's `EPERM`,
    and the run is quarantined for the ref alone. It passed on 2026-10-18 on macOS with Claude Code 2.1.302, in 19 s.
  - On Linux, checked on 2026-10-19 by OWL-349, in a Linux container, not on a Linux host: the same test passed (`test result: ok. 1 passed`, 22.38 s).
    - **Where.** Docker Desktop on the maintainer's Mac, Linux container (linux/aarch64, kernel 6.12.76-linuxkit), Debian bookworm, bubblewrap 0.8.0, Claude Code 2.1.302 from npm,
      source at `32e1308`; the run user was `owl`, uid 1001, not root, and `init` reported `bypassPermissions`.
      The container ran with `--security-opt seccomp=unconfined --security-opt systempaths=unconfined`, which bwrap needs inside Docker, as for OWL-101 (under OWL-94 below).
    - **How the token got there.** As for OWL-101: the maintainer ran a throwaway script, since removed, that piped the token for agent runs from the macOS Keychain
      into the container's standard input, where a probe stored it in gnome-keyring on a tmpfs; the test read it back as the executor does.
      A dry run with a made-up token ended on API status 401 first.
    - **What Linux changes in the test.** bwrap gives the run a private `/tmp`, a tmpfs, and the test's folder is in `/tmp`:
      the Write of a file beside the worktree succeeded there, into the tmpfs, and the file was absent on the host. So on Linux that row rests on host absence, not on a tool error.
      The hook, a read-only bind, gets `EROFS` ("read-only file system") where Seatbelt says `EPERM`, as a credential-free bwrap probe of the same layout showed;
      on Linux the test accepts either error for a sandbox refusal, and nothing broader.
      The refusal of a write outside the worktree is probed instead in `OWLSHIFT_LIVE_OUTSIDE_DIR`, a folder the user can write on the host outside `/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, `/run/user` and the home,
      which the sandbox sees only through its read-only `/` (`/srv/owl349-outside` in the container): the Write was refused by the sandbox and nothing reached the host.
      The test asks for that eighth call on Linux only, and matches every call on its exact path.
    - **What this settles.** A write-in-worktree run in bypass mode on Linux edits the `.claude/` files and stays bounded by bwrap and the isolation check: the hook and a file outside the worktree refused, the run quarantined for the ref alone.
  - What stays open: a Linux host. The Docker Desktop kernel has no AppArmor restriction on unprivileged user namespaces, so the Ubuntu 24.04 path (the `bwrap` profile) was not exercised,
    and neither x86_64 nor bare metal was checked; one run, on haiku.
    A Linux runner started as root cannot run a write-in-worktree role on Claude Code (the refusal above); `IS_SANDBOX=1` would lift it, and is not set.
    Managed settings themselves were not tried, `--settings` standing in for them.
    The peer inbox was not rerun in the bypass class, where OWL-52 found delivery follows the sender's class: `PEER_INBOX_ENV` binds no socket whatever the mode (OWL-65), and the refuse flag stays.
    The fixtures recorded under `acceptEdits` stay as they are; the recorder takes the new argv at its next run.

**C2 — passed, with three findings for the Codex harness (OWL-297).** Run on 2026-10-15 with codex-cli 0.154.0, on macOS 26.6 with the maintainer's ChatGPT login (Plus), and on Linux (Debian trixie in Docker, bubblewrap) logged out, by the maintainer's choice. About twenty tiny `codex exec --ephemeral --json --ignore-user-config -c model_reasoning_effort=low` runs, in a linked worktree of a scratch repository. Each run started from an environment of `HOME`, `PATH`, `USER`, `LANG`, `SSL_CERT_FILE` and `CODEX_HOME` alone. Each run had a `CODEX_HOME` of its own: no `config.toml`, no `.env`, a marker `AGENTS.md`, one execpolicy rule, and the user's login file read through a link. The confinement was the executor's own profile, built by a throwaway program from `AgentEnv::policy` and `sandbox::wrap`, with the worktree and the repository's git folder moved to read-only: the profile OWL-298 builds. The scrubbed recordings are in `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/fixtures/codex/`, named below in parentheses. Each has a `.meta.json` beside it: command line, platform, confinement, exit status, standard error and `-o` file. `tests/codex_fixtures.rs` checks them on the three CI platforms.

- **Headless on a subscription.** With no API key, `codex exec` ran on the ChatGPT login. Under `--ignore-user-config` the default model is `gpt-6-astra`; plain output names it, the stream does not.
  - The stream is `thread.started` (with `thread_id`), then `turn.started`, then items, then `turn.completed`.
  - Items arrive as `item.started` and `item.completed`. An `agent_message` item carries `text`. A `command_execution` item carries `command`, `aggregated_output`, `exit_code` and `status`.
  - `turn.completed` carries `usage`: `input_tokens`, `cached_input_tokens`, `cache_write_input_tokens`, `output_tokens` and `reasoning_output_tokens`. There is no cost (`success`).
  - Standard error always opens with `Reading additional input from stdin...`, even with standard input from `/dev/null`.
- **Codex's sandbox off, inside Owlshift's.** `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` runs inside the read-only profile on both systems. The answer is the same as the run without the profile (`success`).
  - Left on with `-s read-only` inside `sandbox-exec`, the model announced `ls`, but no `command_execution` item followed, and the run still completed with exit 0 (`nested_sandbox`). A nested Seatbelt fails silently, so the adapter must turn Codex's sandbox off, as section 5 of the architecture says.
  - Under `bwrap`, `-s read-only` starts too. Whether Codex's own sandbox works there shows only at a tool call, which the logged-out run never reached.
- **Writes, with Codex's sandbox off.** Under the profile:
  - A write to the worktree is refused: `zsh:1: operation not permitted`, `exit_code` 1, `status: failed`. So is a write to the git folder (`write_refused`).
  - A write to the run's temporary folder succeeds.
  - `git status` still answers, with two `xcrun` cache errors on macOS, where `/var/folders` is closed.
  - On Linux, a plain shell under the same profile gets `Read-only file system` for the worktree and the git folder.
  - Without the profile, the same five commands all succeeded.
- **Finding 1: `CODEX_HOME` must be writable.** With it read-only, Codex stops before the thread starts: exit 1, nothing on standard output, and `Error: failed to initialize in-process app-server client: Operation not permitted (os error 1)` on standard error (`Read-only file system` on Linux) (`codex_home_read_only`).
  - Even with `--ephemeral`, every run wrote there: `config.toml`, `installation_id`, the SQLite files `state_5`, `logs_2`, `goals_1`, `memories_1` and `queue_1`, `cache/` (the apps server's tools and info), `plugins/cache/`, `skills/`, `shell_snapshots/` and `tmp/arg0/`.
  - Codex's documentation says a ChatGPT login refreshes `auth.json` in place.
  - Decision, with the maintainer on 2026-10-15: the fact is recorded and the security goal of OWL-130 stands. No run's write to `CODEX_HOME`, its `.env` above all, may route the next run; with Codex's sandbox off, what Codex writes the critic's shell can write.
  - Decided with the maintainer on 2026-10-15 (OWL-298), which settles OWL-130's goal for Codex: a `CODEX_HOME` of the run's own, in its temporary folder, holding only a copy of the user's `auth.json`; after the run the runner copies `auth.json` back only if it changed and still parses as a ChatGPT login of the same account, and otherwise drops it with a warning, so a refresh the copy makes does not log the operator out. Built by OWL-299 (architecture section 5, "The Codex harness"). Rejected: the user's `CODEX_HOME` left writable, with the `.env` path closed another way.
- **Finding 2: certificates.** Under the profile on macOS, Codex's certificate check failed with `invalid peer certificate: UnknownIssuer`. The run then retried without end: no event on standard output, and a model refresh on standard error every 4.5 min, until it was killed after 23 min.
  - With `SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/ssl/cert.pem`, the system bundle, the same run connects. That was checked logged out, then on every recorded run.
  - The cause, isolated on 2026-10-15 (OWL-298) with the same Codex logged out, an empty `CODEX_HOME` and the executor's profile: the `mach-lookup` of `com.apple.SecurityServer`, the Keychain server, which the profile closes. With it allowed, the run connects (a 401, logged out, in 15 s); with only `com.apple.securityd.xpc` allowed, or with the Keychain files opened and both services closed, `UnknownIssuer` stays. The server stays closed, and the read-only profile sets `SSL_CERT_FILE` instead (part O).
  - So the read-only profile on macOS must let Codex verify certificates, and a run that cannot connect never ends on its own: the executor's timeout is its only bound.
- **Finding 3: the login is readable from the critic's shell.** It answered `auth-readable` to `test -r "$HOME/.codex/auth.json"` (`write_refused`). Codex needs the file, and its commands run in its own process tree. This is the known limit section 8 states for Claude Code's token.
- **Structured output.** `--output-schema` is sent to the API as a strict schema.
  - A schema with a property missing from `required` fails at the API: an `error` event, then `turn.failed`, whose `message` is the API's error as JSON text (`invalid_json_schema`, "required to include every key in properties"); exit 1 (`invalid_schema`). The critic's schema must therefore list every property as required, with a nullable type where a value is optional.
  - Asked for a plain word against a schema of `{verdict: yes|no}`, the model answered `{"verdict":"no"}` (`output_schema`). An answer that misses its schema was not observed; the runner validates `result.json` anyway.
  - `-o` writes the last message, the same JSON text, to its file.
  - The schema file must be readable inside the profile. From a closed folder, Codex stops before the thread starts: exit 1, `Failed to read output schema file …: Operation not permitted`. It belongs in the run's temporary folder, beside the brief.
- **Effort.** `-c model_reasoning_effort=` was run with `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max` on `gpt-6-astra`, and every level answered. The CLI does not check the value; the API does, per model.
  - The API refused `minimal` for this model (`unsupported_value`: "Supported values are: 'low', 'medium', 'high', 'xhigh', and 'max'"), and `bogus` for any model, listing `none` and `minimal` besides those five (`unsupported_effort`, `unknown_effort`). Each refusal came as `error` and `turn.failed`, exit 1.
  - Owlshift's five levels therefore map one to one on this model.
- **Unknown model.** `-m no-such-model` gave an `item.completed` of type `error` ("Model metadata … not found"), then `error` and `turn.failed` with status 400, "not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account"; exit 1 (`unknown_model`).
- **Failure.** On every run, macOS and Linux, the exit status was non-zero exactly when the stream ended in `turn.failed` or was empty. A failure's text is in `turn.failed.error.message`, often itself the API's error as JSON text. `codex_fixtures.rs` pins this rule on the recordings.
- **Rules.** `--ignore-rules` drops execpolicy `.rules` files, and only those.
  - With a `prefix_rule` forbidding `touch` in `$CODEX_HOME/rules/`, the command was rejected before it ran: no `command_execution` item, and the model reported `Rejected: OWL-297 probe rule` (`rules_rejected`). With the flag, the command ran.
  - The flag does not drop `AGENTS.md`: the markers of the repository's file and of `CODEX_HOME`'s both appeared, with and without it.
  - `-c project_doc_max_bytes=0` was tried with a one-word prompt, which hid the markers in the control run too. Whether it drops the repository's `AGENTS.md` is unsettled. Settled by OWL-131 (OWL-130's entry, below): it drops the repository's and keeps `CODEX_HOME`'s, and the adapter passes it.
  - A `CODEX_HOME` of the run's own leaves the user's `AGENTS.md` out.
- **The user's skills.** Under the profile, the run read 13,725 input tokens against 17,066 without it. It could not scan `~/.agents/skills`, an error on standard error, so the user's skills reach a run that is not confined. The worktree's skills reach a confined run too; the adapter lists none since OWL-131 (OWL-130's entry, below).
- **Linux under `bwrap`, logged out.** Codex starts inside the read-only profile with its sandbox off and reaches the API (`logged_out`): 5 `Reconnecting… n/5` errors on the websocket, an `item.completed` of type `error` for the fallback to HTTPS, 5 more errors, then `error` and `turn.failed` with 401; exit 1. A logged-in run under `bwrap` was not run, by the maintainer's choice on 2026-10-15: no credential went into the container.

**C3 — passed.** On GitHub (`pitchopp/owlshift`, over SSH), 8 runners pushed concurrently to the same ref in each round, each with its own commit, and only under `refs/owlshift/check-c3/`:

- Create, 5 rounds: `git push --porcelain --force-with-lease=<ref>: origin <commit>:<ref>` (empty expected value: the ref must not exist). Each round had exactly one `[new reference]`, and the remote ref then held the winner's commit. All 35 losers were rejected by the server: `[remote rejected] (cannot lock ref '<ref>': reference already exists)`.
- Takeover, 3 rounds: the ref was first set to a stale lease, then all 8 runners pushed with `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<stale-oid>`. Each round had exactly one `(forced update)`. All 21 losers got `[remote rejected] (cannot lock ref '<ref>': is at <winner-oid> but expected <stale-oid>)`, which also names the new holder.
- GitHub accepts refs outside `refs/heads` and `refs/tags`. All 8 test refs were deleted afterwards, and `git ls-remote origin 'refs/owlshift/*'` returns nothing.
- Why this is a compare-and-swap: the push carries the expected old value with the new one (the zero id for "must not exist"). The server compares it with the current value under the ref lock and writes only on a match, so the check and the write are one step. A check made by the client alone would not do: every loser here was rejected by the server, after another runner's write had landed, a case a client-side check cannot see. GitHub's storage backend is not visible from outside, so for GitHub the evidence is the observed behaviour above, not its internals.
- The rejection text differs from a plain bare repository, where the same races (10 create rounds, 5 takeover rounds, one winner each) gave `(reference already exists)` and `(incorrect old value provided)`. The forge adapter decides from the exit status and the per-ref `!` status of `--porcelain`, never from the text.

**C4 — passed.** The comment edit time was run on 2026-09-28 (OWL-13) against the Owlshift workspace with a personal API key, read-only apart from `commentCreate` calls on an issue that does not exist, which write nothing. Triage creation and the app user were run on 2026-10-04 (OWL-141), the last two items below.

- Schema, by introspection: `Comment.editedAt` is "the time the comment was last edited by its author", null when never edited. `Comment.updatedAt` is not an edit time: on every comment of OWL-6 to OWL-30 it differs from `createdAt`, sometimes by being earlier, while `editedAt` is null. The adapter reads `editedAt`. `Issue.priority` is 0 (none), 1 (urgent), 2 (high), 3 (medium) or 4 (low). `Comment.user` is null for bots and integrations, which `botActor` or `externalUser` then names. `Issue.comments` also holds replies and inline comments on the description.
- `issue.comments` answers newest first, with or without `orderBy: createdAt` or `updatedAt`; the adapter sorts oldest first itself.
- Errors: an unknown issue answers HTTP 200 with code `INPUT_ERROR` and "Entity not found: Issue" on a query, "issue not found" on `commentCreate`; a rejected key answers HTTP 401 with `AUTHENTICATION_ERROR`; a malformed issue id on `commentCreate` answers `INVALID_INPUT`, "issueId must be a valid UUID or issue identifier (e.g., 'LIN-123')", so `commentCreate` takes `OWL-13` as is. Rate-limit headers: 2,500 requests and 3,000,000 complexity points per hour, resets in epoch milliseconds. A rate-limited answer was not observed.
- Libraries. HTTP: `ureq` 3.4, blocking, rustls with the bundled web roots; no async runtime is needed yet. Keychain: `keyring-core` 1.0 with `apple-native-keyring-store` (Keychain), `windows-native-keyring-store` (Credential Manager) and `zbus-secret-service-keyring-store` with `crypto-rust` (Secret Service, pure Rust, no libdbus). The `keyring` 4 facade was set aside: its own documentation sends applications to `keyring-core` and the stores, and its global default store gets in the way of an in-memory store for tests. A store, read and delete round trip passed on the macOS Keychain; the crate type-checks for Linux and Windows, and CI builds it on all three.
  - macOS goes through `/usr/bin/security` instead (OWL-335, decided 2026-10-17). The Keychain trusts the reader of an entry by its code, and `cargo` signs every build ad hoc, so every new `owlshift` binary was asked once or twice per entry, four or five password prompts in a row after each rebuild or upgrade, "Always Allow" included (P1 results). Developer ID signing would end it at a yearly cost the maintainer declined. `security` is signed by Apple and does not change with Owlshift, so one "Always Allow" per entry holds; `gh` reaches the Keychain the same way. Checked live on 2026-10-17 on macOS 26.6.2: two different ad-hoc signed binaries running `security` stored an entry, read it, replaced it and read it back with no prompt, every call returning at once; a secret written through `security -i` on standard input never showed in `ps`; a missing entry exits 44 on `find-generic-password` and `delete-generic-password`; inside a double-quoted `-i` argument `\"` and `\\` stand for `"` and `\`; an unknown `-i` command makes `security` exit 1. With the change, `system_keychain_round_trip` passed twice with a rebuild between, in 0.09 s each, with no prompt. The trade-off: any unconfined process of the user's can run `security` and read Owlshift's entries without a prompt, as it can `gh`'s; confined agent runs still cannot, the sandbox closes the Keychain server. `contains` reads attributes only and never prompts. An entry stored before the change was made by an older `owlshift` binary: reading it asks once, "Always Allow" adds `security`, or `owlshift init` stores it again.
- A ticket's author, checked on 2026-09-29 (OWL-62) by introspection and read-only queries: `Issue.creator` is "the user who created the issue", null when that account was deleted or an integration or system process created it; `Issue.botActor` and `Issue.externalUserCreator` then name the creator. OWL-1 to OWL-4, made by Linear's onboarding, have a null creator and the bot actor `Linear` (type `workflow`); every other issue has a creator, and the 50 most recent, OWL-11 and OWL-13 all have the maintainer's account, the one of the personal API key, and no bot actor. `externalUserCreator` was not observed set; its meaning comes from the schema. The adapter reads the three fields as it does a comment's author, and fails closed: the author is an account only when Linear names no bot and no external user beside it.
- A ticket written through the decider's API key, checked on 2026-09-29 (OWL-74). Schema: Linear's published SDK schema (`linear/linear`, `packages/sdk/src/schema.graphql` at `2a3ac4cfd2`, 2026-09-28) has no field of `Issue` or `IssueHistory` naming the client that created an issue (Linear's app or a personal API key). Live, read-only: OWL-80, OWL-81 and OWL-74, created by an agent through the personal API key, and OWL-82, typed by hand in Linear's app and then canceled, answer alike: `creator` is the key's account (`app` false); `trusted`, `integrationSourceType`, `botActor`, `externalUserCreator`, `sourceComment` and `lastAppliedTemplate` are null; `syncedWith` is empty; the history's creation entry, when there is one, is that account's, with no bot actor and no `changes`. So Linear cannot tell an issue its creator typed from one created through their key. Decision: the adapter reports a Linear issue's creator as an author it cannot vouch for, never as an account, so no Linear ticket's description is the decider's; it reads as a request, and the decider steers through comments. A comment posted through the key is still the account's, since comments are how the decider answers. Restoring decider tickets on Linear would take a signal the adapter can trust, for instance automation acting under its own identity (an OAuth app as an app user) so that the decider's account only creates by hand; that is not verified, and it would take an explicit change of this rule.
- Moving the visible stage, checked on 2026-10-04 (OWL-137) on the Owlshift workspace with the personal API key, by the adapter's own calls (`record_stage_fixture`, recorded as `stage-owl-137.json`). `issue(id: "OWL-137") { id team { key states(first: 50) { nodes { id name } pageInfo { hasNextPage } } } }` takes the identifier and answers the issue's id and the nine workflow states of team `OWL` in one page, by name (In Review, Needs Input, Triage, Duplicate, Done, Backlog, Todo, Canceled, In Progress), the ids those of the tracker table in `AGENTS.md`. `issueUpdate(id: <issue id>, input: { stateId })` with the Needs Input id answered `success: true` and the issue in Needs Input; the same with the In Progress id put it back, `success: true`, and the recorder's closing restore answered In Progress too. An unknown issue on the states query answers as on a ticket read, `INPUT_ERROR` "Entity not found: Issue". So the API key moves a stage with no extra scope, and the adapter needs no other call.
- A team's workflow states by its key, checked on 2026-10-04 (OWL-147) on the Owlshift workspace with the personal API key, read only, with the exact request `owlshift doctor` sends (recorded as `team-states-owl.json`; `record_read_fixtures` records it again). `teams(filter: { key: { eq: "OWL" } }) { nodes { key states(first: 50) { nodes { name } pageInfo { hasNextPage } } } }` answers the one team `OWL` and its nine states in one page, the names above; the key `NOPE` answers `nodes: []`, HTTP 200, no error, which the adapter reports as not found. The filter is case-sensitive, checked on 2026-10-04 (OWL-153) with the same request: `eq: "OWL"` answers the team and `eq: "owl"` answers `nodes: []`, HTTP 200, no error. So the adapter sends the key upper case, as Linear writes team keys, since `do` compares the configured key with a ticket's without regard to case.
- Recorded fixtures of these answers, with every account pseudonymized, are in `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/fixtures/linear/`.
- An issue created directly in Triage, checked on 2026-10-04 (OWL-141) on the Owlshift workspace with the personal API key, by a one-off script, so no fixture was recorded. Linear's [GraphQL guide](https://linear.app/developers/graphql), read that day, documents only the default: an issue created with no `stateId` goes to Triage when the team has Triage on. `issueCreate(input: { teamId, stateId, title, description })` with the id of team `OWL`'s Triage state answered `success: true` and OWL-156 in Triage (state type `triage`), its creator the key's account (`app` false); read back 3 s later it was still in Triage, `triagedAt` null. The script then canceled it with `issueUpdate` to the Canceled state, never a delete. So the API key files a proposal straight into the team's triage inbox, as S13 needs, with no extra scope.
- An app user with polling only, checked on 2026-10-04 (OWL-141) on the Owlshift workspace, with the same script and on that same issue before it was canceled. Linear's [OAuth guide](https://linear.app/developers/oauth-2-0-authentication), read that day: an OAuth app with client credentials tokens turned on gets one from `POST https://api.linear.app/oauth/token` with `grant_type=client_credentials`, a scope list and the app's client id and secret; it is an `app` actor token that reaches every public team of the workspace and is valid for 30 days, with no refresh token, and the guide asks for a new one at the start of each run. Requesting another set of scopes revokes every app token of the app, rotating the client secret invalidates them, and up to 1,000 with the same scopes can be active at once. The token request involves no browser and no redirect endpoint. Webhooks serve the Agent Session events Linear sends when someone mentions the app or delegates an issue to it ([agents guide](https://linear.app/developers/agents)), which the optional `app:mentionable` and `app:assignable` scopes allow. An app that takes neither scope can be neither mentioned nor delegated to, so it has no session to answer; Owlshift's makes outbound calls only and polls.
  - Set up, by a workspace admin: in the workspace's API settings, create a new OAuth application (`https://linear.app/settings/api/applications/new`), named as its comments should appear; leave it private and its webhooks off, turn its client credentials option on, and save. The maintainer created the app from these steps. The token below shows the client credentials option was on; that the app was private with webhooks off was asked for and not confirmed back, and the option's exact label and whether the form required a callback URL were not noted. Every call of the check was outbound, so nothing depended on a webhook. The script read the client id and secret from a local, uncommitted file and printed neither.
  - Token: requested with scope `read,write`, meant to cover the comments, stage moves and issues Owlshift writes, of which only comments and reads were tried with it. It answered HTTP 200, `token_type` `Bearer`, `expires_in` 2591999, `scope` `read write`. Asked `viewer`, the token is an app user (`app` true, active, named as the app), and it reaches team `OWL`, whose visibility is public.
  - Comments: A, posted with the app token, mentions no one; B, posted with the app token, mentions the decider by writing their profile URL in the Markdown; C, posted with the personal key as Owlshift posts today, is the control. Each answered `success: true`. Read back through the author fields the adapter reads (`user`, `botActor`, `externalUser`) and `user.app`: A and B have a `user`, the app user, with `app` true, and no `botActor` or `externalUser`; C's `user` has `app` false. So an app user's comment is not one of the bot or integration comments above, whose `user` is null: the adapter reads `user { id displayName }` without `app` today, and would take the app's comments for an account's. The app's comments are told apart from a person's by `user.app`, the kind of signal OWL-74's item above names for tickets; that rule is unchanged, and issue creation through the app was not tried.
  - Notifications, read through the decider's own key (`notifications`, filtered from just before the comments): the decider, subscribed as the issue's creator, had two unread notifications on the issue within 10 s, `issueNewComment` (category `commentsAndReplies`) for A and `issueCommentMention` (category `mentions`) for B, both from an app actor, and none for C. So Linear told the decider of the app's comments and not of the one posted through their own key; today every comment Owlshift writes goes through the operator's key, so a decider who is the operator should expect none from Linear, and the app identity is what makes Linear tell them. A decider who is not subscribed to the issue, such as a zone owner, is reached by the mention, as B was. Comment A also shows the cost: every comment the app posts notifies the issue's subscribers, a delivery report or a resume comment included, while S6 notifies only when a person becomes the blocker; S6 binds Owlshift's own notifications, and the work that builds the app identity has to settle how the app's progress comments fit it. Only the Linear inbox was read: whether a notification also reached the decider's desktop, phone or e-mail follows their own Linear settings and was not observed.
  - The token was revoked at the end (`POST https://api.linear.app/oauth/revoke`, HTTP 200). Decision: D8 (architecture section 12), with the desktop notification of OWL-140 kept beside it.
  - Before it was built, checked on 2026-10-04 (OWL-157) with a one-off script through the same app and key, on an issue the script made and then deleted to Linear's trash with its comment. Issue creation through the app: `issueCreate` into OWL's Triage made OWL-167, whose `creator` is the app user (`app` true), with no bot actor or external creator. So Linear tells an issue the app created from one a person's key created; OWL-74's rule above stands, since a person's key still creates as the person. A comment through the app holding the line `Waiting for <the decider's User.url>` (`https://linear.app/owlshift/profiles/<displayName>`) was stored and read back verbatim, its user the app user (`app` true). It gave the decider, assigned and subscribed, an `issueCommentMention` (category `mentions`) from the app user, beside the creation's `issueAssignedToYou`. `issueUpdate` moved the stage through the app. `CommentCreateInput` has no field that posts without notifying: `doNotSubscribeToIssue` only keeps the author from subscribing, and `subscriberIds` is internal. Revoked with the token as a `Bearer` header, HTTP 200, the token then answered HTTP 401 `AUTHENTICATION_ERROR`.
