  - `uv sync --group lint --group test` with uv's cache and Pythons redirected: `stack.setup` runs it with `UV_CACHE_DIR="$TMPDIR/uv-cache"` and the network (a cache of the run's own, a speed-up only, since uv cannot use the read-only `~/.cache/uv`), and either `UV_NO_MANAGED_PYTHON=1` with a system Python, or `.local/share/uv` declared and allowed as a cache for its managed Pythons (whether uv finds a managed Python in a read-only folder was not checked here); the gate's commands then run the venv's tools, so `uv run` no longer installs groups in each of them;
  - a Python installed in one command's `TMPDIR` and lost in the next: gone with the shared temporary folder;
  - the SWC cache and `mktemp`: stay in the project, as the quirks above say.

  `gate.sh` then keeps only Locary's own commands (`make lint`, `make test`), and `stack.gate` can name them directly.
- **The bench.** `crates/owlshift-testkit/tests/confined_setup.rs` runs both fixtures, `tests/fixtures/node-project` and `python-project`, through a confined `Executor::run_gate` on a fresh worktree, then a Build `Executor::run` with the fake harness over that kept worktree, whose pre-harness setup and gate find the first install; and the negatives: setup removed, cache dropped, cache not allowed, a setup writing into the cache. The fixtures' packages are vendored and seeded into a fake home's cache by the test, unconfined, with no registry beyond the loopback. Whether those tests run, skip or fail is `fixture_gate(tools_present, sandbox_ok, require)`: they skip without pnpm, uv or a sandbox, as in a Build's own confined run, unless `OWLSHIFT_REQUIRE_FIXTURE_TOOLS` is set, as on CI's Linux and macOS legs.
- **CI.** The Linux and macOS legs install the fixtures' tools, pnpm 10.33.2 and uv 0.11.7, the versions the live checks used, with `pnpm/action-setup` and `astral-sh/setup-uv` pinned to full commits (v6.1.0's `ea17c68df8912ef543352723c149a84f56e3d413`, the commit its annotated tag points to, and v10.3.0's `1c37ad07a6a961277cf70c0d37d6f313000f5884`, resolved with `git ls-remote` on 2026-10-20 at the maintainer's request and given on the ticket): approved by the maintainer's answer to Q5 on OWL-330 (2026-10-17). They also set `OWLSHIFT_REQUIRE_FIXTURE_TOOLS=1`, so the fixtures' tests fail rather than skip there: approved by the maintainer's approval of OWL-330's plan (2026-10-19), which named it. Both change CI settings, which the floor of architecture section 8 says needs an explicit human approval; nothing else in CI changes. The Linux claim of the live checks is recorded in the live checks' item (b): the pull request's `check (ubuntu-latest)` leg (run 39394409069, 2026-10-20) ran the confined gate tests and the fixtures' tests (`confined_setup`: the Node and Python projects' confined gates and install recipes, `fixture_gate`) with both variables set, 1230 tests run and passed, none failed. The Node pnpm runs on there is the runner image's own, since CI sets up no Node: the live checks did not read which Node `ubuntu-latest` and `macos-latest` ship (the Build that wrote them had no network), so that pnpm 10.33.2 runs on it, its `engines` asking Node `>=18.12`, is unchecked here, and the first CI run of OWL-330's pull request is the check.

**OWL-71 — launching agent commands on native Windows (decided 2026-09-29).** Native confinement (OWL-58) was split: this part prepared the launch path, and OWL-72 was to apply an AppContainer through it. Decision D9 is unchanged: native Windows runs no agent. Superseded (2026-09-29): native confinement is set aside, and the launcher was removed (OWL-89, 2026-09-30); see the decision after the OWL-72 results below. What follows is the record of what was built and checked; none of it is in the code any more.

- Premise, checked on 2026-09-29: `std::process::Command` on stable Rust cannot pass a proc-thread attribute, which is how an AppContainer is given (`SECURITY_CAPABILITIES`). A scratch crate calling `ProcThreadAttributeList::build().raw_attribute(..)` and `CommandExt::spawn_with_attributes`, checked with `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` on rustc 1.98.1, fails with E0658, "use of unstable library feature `windows_process_extensions_raw_attribute`" (issue #114854).
- The fork: a `CreateProcessW` path of the runner's own, with a child type of its own, or a small program the runner starts as it starts any command, which calls `CreateProcessW` itself. Decision: the program, `owlshift-launch`. A child type of our own would replace `std::process::Child` wherever the runner takes one (the `Harness::drive` trait, the Claude Code adapter's `drive`, `drive_plain`, `watch::spawn`, the gate, `process::run_command` for the probes), and redo in Windows-only unsafe code what std does: pipes, the environment block, the program's lookup, argument quoting. The launcher keeps `sandbox::wrap -> Command`, as `sandbox-exec` and `bwrap` do; the cost is one more process per agent command and a second binary.
- As built, until its removal in OWL-89 (2026-09-30):
  - `owlshift-launch` is a second binary of `owlshift-cli`, whose `default-run = "owlshift"` keeps `cargo owl` and `bacon owl` working. Its code is `owlshift_platform::launch`, on Windows only; elsewhere the binary refuses to run.
  - Its arguments are `-- PROGRAM LINE`, `LINE` being the rest of the program's command line, verbatim. On Windows `sandbox::wrap` quotes the arguments into `LINE` as std quotes a regular argument, and `sandbox::wrap_line` takes a line as it is, for `cmd.exe`. The gate goes through `AgentEnv::confine_shell`: `confine` rebuilds a command from `get_args`, which hands back the gate's `raw_arg` as a plain argument, to be quoted again.
  - The runner starts the launcher as the root of the run's process tree, inside its Job Object (`ProcessTree::spawn`, unchanged). The launcher finds the program as std does, but for the runner's own folder and `PATH`: `.exe` is added when there is no extension, and a name with no folder is looked for in the absolute folders of the agent's `PATH`, then `SYSTEMROOT\System32` and `SYSTEMROOT`, never in the working directory. It creates the program with `CreateProcessW` and its three standard handles, closes its own copies, waits, and exits with the program's exit code; its own failures exit with 127 and `owlshift-launch: <reason>` on standard error. A job's process starts its children in the job, since `tree.rs` allows no breakaway, so stopping the tree stops the launcher, the program and all it started.
  - Batch files are refused, by `wrap_line` and again by the launcher on the path it found: `.bat` and `.cmd` in any letter case, trailing dots and spaces ignored, and a name holding `:`, a file stream such as `x.bat::$DATA`. `cmd.exe` reads a batch file's arguments again; std guards that case with its own escaping, the launcher does not. So the npm-installed Claude Code, `claude.cmd`, cannot be launched, and the native installer's `claude.exe` can.
- Nothing is granted or denied yet. A shipped build has no launcher: on Windows `wrap` still refuses with `Unsupported`, as `available` does, and `Executor::run` and `owlshift do` refuse before anything is written. Only a test sets one, with `sandbox::use_built_launcher`, compiled with the platform crate's `testkit` feature, which the runner's tests enable: a thread-local override naming the binary Cargo built, so a refusal test running beside it still sees none. `the_cli_never_turns_confinement_off` refuses the name in the CLI's sources, since a workspace build unifies the feature into `owlshift`. The work that would have lifted the refusal, finding the launcher next to the running `owlshift`, confining the program it creates and lifting `available`, is not planned; OWL-72, below, recorded how the candidate confinements behave before it was set aside.
- Checked on windows-latest on 2026-09-29 ([run 36591257645](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/actions/runs/36591257645)):
  - `process::tests::a_launched_program_is_stopped_with_its_launcher_at_the_deadline`: the helper's parent was `owlshift-launch.exe`, and at a 4 s deadline the launcher, the helper and its grandchild were all gone.
  - `executor::harness::tests::a_confined_harness_command_starts_through_the_launcher`, on the harness's path, `AgentEnv::confine`: an empty argument, `a b`, `a"b`, `a\"b`, `c:\x\` and a tab came back unchanged, as did the standard input, the agent's placeholder `GH_TOKEN` and exit code 3.
  - `executor::gate`'s Windows tests, bare and confined: `echo "a  b" \"q\" "c:\x\" && exit 5` logged its line as written and failed with exit status 5, the agent environment was set, and a command past a 1 s deadline was stopped.
  - The refusals held: `native_windows_refuses_to_confine`, `a_confined_gate_command_is_refused_on_native_windows` and `a_run_on_native_windows_is_refused`.
- Limits: the Windows launcher tests need the launcher built, which `cargo test --workspace` does and a test run of one package alone does not (the test names `cargo build -p owlshift-cli --bin owlshift-launch`); `cargo owl` builds `owlshift` only, so a Windows run, once native confinement lands, will need that build too; an argument full of quotes reaches the 32,767-character limit of a command line at about half its length, since `LINE` is quoted once more on the launcher's own command line; Ctrl-C through the launcher was not checked live.

**OWL-72 — how an AppContainer behaves on windows-latest (checked 2026-09-29).** OWL-72 was re-scoped to live checks before any confinement code; two follow-ups (OWL-83, OWL-84) were to rest on them, and were canceled when native Windows was set aside (decision below). Decision D9 stands: native Windows runs no agent. A throwaway program calling the Win32 API through `windows-sys` ran in a temporary workflow on the OWL-72 branch, removed before the pull request, on windows-latest (Windows Server 2025, 10.0.26100.33438), in four rounds: [run 36603469888](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/actions/runs/36603469888), [36604300313](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/actions/runs/36604300313), [36604775652](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/actions/runs/36604775652) and [36605235445](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/actions/runs/36605235445); its source is in the pull request's commits. The secrets were fake: a gh-shaped `hosts.yml` and a sibling project's `.env` planted in the user's temporary folder, and a generic Credential Manager entry the program wrote and deleted. The container was a profile made with `CreateAppContainerProfile`, given to `CreateProcessW` as `SECURITY_CAPABILITIES` through `STARTUPINFOEXW`, as the launcher would give it.

- **Files are closed by default.** Granted nothing, the container's process read `System32` but neither the planted token nor the `.env` ("Access is denied", exit 1), itself or through a grandchild. It could not list the profile or `%APPDATA%`, nor read a file in the runner's workspace on `D:`, outside the profile. It reaches only what grants ALL APPLICATION PACKAGES (`System32`, `Program Files`) or its own SID.
- **The Credential Manager is closed.** The program and an unconfined copy of it read the entry back; the copy in the container got error 5 from `CredReadW`, with or without network capabilities, and `cmdkey /list` there listed nothing.
- **The network takes a capability; the loopback stays closed across the container's edge.** With no capability `curl` resolved no name. With `internetClient` (`S-1-15-3-1`) an HTTPS request to `example.com` got its 200; `privateNetworkClientServer` changed nothing. A listener of the host on `127.0.0.1` was not reached from the container with any set tried (none; `internetClient` and `privateNetworkClientServer`; those and `internetClientServer`), nor a listener in the container from the host; two processes in the same container did talk over the loopback. A proxy on the loopback, such as the forwarding proxy OWL-76 suggests, is out of reach: that takes a loopback exemption, which an administrator sets for the machine (`CheckNetIsolation`), not tried.
- **A granted folder needs no lowered integrity label.** The container runs at low integrity (`whoami /groups`), yet a folder granted "Modify" to its SID (`(OI)(CI)(M)`), with no label, took a new file, an append and a new folder, as a folder labelled low did. An explicit medium label with no-write-up (`S:(ML;OICI;NW;;;ME)`) on a folder inside the grant stopped the writes; an explicit no-read-up label on a file did not stop a read.
- **Speed.** On 20,000 files in 100 folders under the temporary folder, `SetNamedSecurityInfoW` added an inherited entry in 2.53 s (about 7,900 files a second), set a low label in 2.59 s, revoked the entry in 1.90 s and removed the label in 2.76 s. The probe's own build folder took 2.8 ms.

What bears on the follow-ups:

- **A plain AppContainer cannot run git.** It cannot open `NUL`: error 5 for reading, writing or both, `\\.\NUL` alike, and `cmd`'s `> NUL` fails. The device's DACL on this image is `D:(A;;0x1201bf;;;WD)(A;;FA;;;SY)(A;;FA;;;BA)(A;;0x1200a9;;;RC)`: Everyone, SYSTEM, Administrators and RESTRICTED, no ALL APPLICATION PACKAGES and no capability. Git for Windows 2.55 opens `/dev/null` at every start, so `git --version`, `status`, `add` and `commit` all died, standard input given, with `fatal: could not open '/dev/null' for reading and writing: Permission denied` (exit 128); `curl -o NUL` could not write its body (exit 23). Opening `NUL` to containers means changing that DACL as an administrator, for the whole machine.
- **Deny entries do not bind a container.** With a deny entry for its SID present on a file, or one for ALL APPLICATION PACKAGES, the container still read the file; a folder with a deny-write entry for its SID was written, emptied and renamed. A path to hide or protect inside a granted folder has to stop inheriting instead: a file whose protected DACL leaves the SID out was neither read, deleted nor renamed, and a folder whose protected DACL grants the SID read and execute only was read, but not written, appended, emptied, removed or renamed. `SetEntriesInAclW` with `REVOKE_ACCESS` removes a SID's allow entries and leaves its deny entries.
- **The launcher's program needs no grant; what it starts does.** The program `CreateProcessW` put in the container ran from the workspace, which the container cannot read. A program it started from there was refused, and ran once its folder was granted read and execute. The harness's own binary needs no grant; the tool chains it runs do.
- **A profile is needed, and deleting it leaves the grants.** `CreateProcessW` with the SID of a name no profile was made for failed with error 2. After `DeleteAppContainerProfile`, the entries naming the SID stay on every path until revoked.
- **The container joins the launcher's job and stays in its container.** Started from a process in a Job Object, its process was in that job. It could not start a process in another container, nor in its own with one more capability (error 87), and could not open the program that started it (error 5).
- **A restricted token instead.** Restricting SIDs Everyone, Users and a SID for the run, every privilege dropped but the traverse bypass, a default DACL of the user, the run's SID and SYSTEM: the profile's files, the Credential Manager and the starting program stayed closed and `NUL` opened. But git failed to start (`0xC0000142`, a DLL failed to initialise), HTTPS failed (`schannel: AcquireCredentialsHandle failed: SEC_E_NO_CREDENTIALS`), and the process read a file on `D:` and wrote in `C:\ProgramData` and on `D:`, where Everyone or Users may. With the logon SID among the restricting SIDs, git ran `status`, `add` and `commit` in a granted repository and the profile's files and the Credential Manager stayed closed, but the process opened the starting program and read its memory. At low integrity as well, git still ran, the memory read was refused (a query was not), writes landed only in granted folders labelled low, and HTTPS through schannel still failed.

The choices these results left were an AppContainer, once an administrator opens `NUL` to containers on the machine; a low-integrity restricted token with the logon SID, which reads whatever Everyone or Users may (as the macOS and Linux sandboxes read everything outside the home) and whose HTTPS through schannel fails; or native Windows kept refused, under WSL2 (D9).

**Decision (2026-09-29, recorded 2026-09-30, OWL-87): native Windows is set aside.** The maintainer chose the third: a confined run stays refused on native Windows, and WSL2 is the supported path (D9). The AppContainer follow-ups (OWL-83, OWL-84) and the launcher's Ctrl-C test (OWL-85) were canceled. The findings above stay as the starting point if native Windows comes back.

**The launcher is removed (decided 2026-09-30).** Whether to keep `owlshift-launch` as test-only code or delete it was the maintainer's call, and the choice is deletion: with no confinement to apply, it has no use. The removal was done on 2026-09-30 (OWL-89): the `owlshift-launch` binary and `owlshift_platform::launch` are gone, with `sandbox::wrap_line`, `sandbox::use_built_launcher`, the platform crate's `testkit` feature and the launcher's tests. On native Windows `sandbox::wrap` and `available` refuse with `Unsupported`, and so does `AgentEnv::confine_shell` for a confined gate command, directly, since `wrap` would quote the line again; the refusal tests cover both paths. A return of native Windows starts from the OWL-72 findings above and the OWL-71 record, in the history of `crates/owlshift-platform/src/launch.rs`.

**C8 — passed, with one handling rule.** Run on 2026-09-28 (OWL-12) with Claude Code 2.1.283, codex-cli 0.154.0 and git 2.54.0 on macOS. Logged-out states were produced with an empty `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `CODEX_HOME` in a scratch directory; the API-key state with a dummy key in a scratch `CODEX_HOME`. Both commands answer in about 0.1 s, with no network wait observed.

- `claude auth status` prints JSON on stdout (`--json` is the default). Logged in: exit 0, `loggedIn: true`, `authMethod: "claude.ai"`, `subscriptionType: "max"`. Logged out: exit 1, `loggedIn: false`, `authMethod: "none"`; the `--text` form says "Not logged in. Run claude auth login to authenticate." With only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set: exit 0, `authMethod: "api_key"`, `apiKeySource: "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"`. The logged-in JSON also carries the account's e-mail address, organisation id and name: personal data, which Owlshift does not deserialize.
- `codex login status` writes one line on stderr, nothing on stdout. ChatGPT login: exit 0, `Logged in using ChatGPT`. Logged out: exit 1, `Not logged in`; an `OPENAI_API_KEY` in the environment does not change that answer. API-key login: exit 0, `Logged in using an API key - ` followed by the key's first and last characters around `***`.
- Handling rule: the verdict comes from the exit status, and the method from a fixed list of known phrases or JSON values. Owlshift never echoes either command's raw output, since Codex prints part of a key.
- `git --version` prints `git version 2.54.0 (Apple Git-157)`; the version is the third word.

**C9 to C17 — the GitHub App sign-in (OWL-336), run on 2026-10-19.** Run by the maintainer with `scripts/github-app-checks.sh` at commit `4a813f0`, curl 8.7.1 and jq 1.7.1 on macOS, on a GitHub App with Device Flow on and the permissions of D13, and a scratch repository of their personal account where it was installed. Each report was pasted on OWL-336 the same day: runs 1, 2, 4, 5, 7 and 8 of the script's checklist. Account identifiers are left out, as above. Runs 3, 6 and 9 to 12 were not run, by the maintainer's choice, so C10-expired, C14's resume, C15-pending, C16 and C17 are recorded not observed below.

**C9 — passed (2026-10-19).** `POST login/device/code` with the client ID alone, no client secret, answered 200 with a device code, a user code of the form `XXXX-XXXX`, `verification_uri` `https://github.com/login/device`, `expires_in` 899 and `interval` 5. Each sign-in of the six runs started this way.

**C10 — passed for the success path and Cancel (2026-10-19); the expired code not observed.**

- A poll before the code was entered: 200, `error=authorization_pending`. A second poll inside the interval: 200, `error=slow_down`, the interval raised to 10. The token came after 7 polls. Its answer holds `access_token`, `expires_in`, `refresh_token`, `refresh_token_expires_in`, `scope` (empty) and `token_type` (`bearer`). Every answer of the token endpoint was HTTP 200, the outcome in `error`.
- Cancel on the device page (run 2): `error=access_denied` after 11 polls.
- Two device codes open in the browser at once spoiled two first attempts, discarded and run again alone: one ended after 35 polls with no error and no token, the other with `access_denied`. So `init` shows one code at a time.
- *C10-expired: not observed (2026-10-19)*, run 3 not run. Assumption kept, unverified: past the code's `expires_in` the endpoint answers `expired_token`, and `init` ends its wait saying so and naming `owlshift init` to start again.

**C11 — passed for every call, with two points unverified (2026-10-19).** With C10's token, on the scratch repository, the app installed, a workflow run on the commit and a pushed branch:

- Checks: `commits/<sha>/check-runs` 200 with 2 check runs; a check run by id 200, with its `output` (`annotations_count`, `annotations_url`, `summary`, `text`, `title`); the Actions job log of a `github-actions` check run, by id, 302 to a signed URL on `*.blob.core.windows.net`, read without the token: 200, 1,994 bytes. Check (c), the job log under Actions (read), is settled for the app's token.
- GraphQL: `GitHubForge::default_branch`'s query 200 with the name; the repository id 200; `createPullRequest` 200, the pull request opened, then closed by REST (200, `closed`); watch's pull request read (`Follow`, `statusCheckRollup` included) 200 with no error, `state` `OPEN`, cost 1; the reviews query 200, none.
- REST: `pulls/N/reviews`, `issues/N/comments` and `pulls/N/comments` 200; `GET /user` 200; the extra read `commits/<sha>/status` 200, `pending`.
- `X-Accepted-GitHub-Permissions`: `checks=read` on check runs, `pull_requests=read` on reviews and pull request comments, `issues=read; pull_requests=read` on issue comments, `statuses=read` on the commit status, `allows_permissionless_access=true` on `/user` and `/user/installations`. GraphQL and the job log name none.
- Rate limit: `X-RateLimit-Limit` 5,000 on every answer, resource `core` for REST and `graphql` for GraphQL. Whether that budget is shared with the user's other credentials was not checked.
- *Unverified: a rollup with check runs.* The branch's head had no check yet, so the rollup came back with no context (`totalCount` empty). That the app's token reads a rollup holding check runs, which OWL-334's fine-grained token could not, is not shown by this run. GA6's acceptance, watch reading the CI of a delivered pull request, shows it.
- *Unverified: the author.* `GET issues/N`, the read of the pull request's author and its "via app" mark, answered 403: the app asks no Issues permission. That the pull request is the signed-in user's, as a user token's should be, is not shown. GA6 reads it on its delivered pull request.

**C12 — passed (2026-10-19).** `GET /user/installations` 200 with one installation. Its `app_slug` equals the app's slug; account type `User`, `repository_selection` `selected`, not suspended; `permissions` read for `actions`, `checks`, `contents`, `metadata` and `statuses`, write for `pull_requests`, as registered. `GET /user/installations/<id>/repositories` 200, the scratch repository listed.

**C13 — passed: the refresh needs no client secret (2026-10-19).** With user-token expiration on (run 4):

- The token answer gives `expires_in` 28,800 (8 hours), a `refresh_token`, and `refresh_token_expires_in` 15,724,800 (182 days).
- The refresh grant (`grant_type=refresh_token`) with the client ID alone, no client secret: 200, accepted, in the token answer's shape.
- It rotates both tokens, each with a full life again. The new access token read the user (200). The first access token answered 401 right after: a refresh revokes the token it replaces.
- Two refreshes at once with the same refresh token: one accepted, the other refused (`bad_refresh_token`, "The refresh token passed is incorrect or expired."). The first refresh token, already used, sent again: refused (`incorrect_client_credentials`). Each refusal came as HTTP 200 with an `error`, as in C10.
- **branch R: refresh without the client secret.** The design takes the expiring token and its refresh ([architecture](architecture.md) section 8, D13).

**C14 — passed for the sign-in (2026-10-19); the resume not observed.** With user-token expiration off (run 5), the token answer holds `access_token`, `scope` and `token_type` only: no `expires_in`, no refresh token. *The resume run, the token still valid after C13's interval: not observed (2026-10-19)*, run 6 not run by the maintainer's choice: it serves only the non-expiring design, which C13 makes unneeded. Its token file was deleted. The opt-out exists, and the design does not use it.

**C15 — passed for an app not installed and installed elsewhere (2026-10-19); the pending request not observed.**

- *C15-none* (run 7): the app uninstalled, the user's authorization kept. The device flow still signed in. `user/installations` 200 with none. The default branch query 200 with a `NOT_FOUND` error, "Could not resolve to a Repository with the name …"; `GET repos/<owner>/<repo>` 404, `x-accepted-github-permissions: metadata=read`; the check runs 404. The link `https://github.com/apps/<slug>/installations/new` opened the app's install page.
- *C15-elsewhere* (run 8): the app installed on another repository of the account only. `user/installations` 200 with one, its `app_slug` the app's; its repositories 200, two, not the scratch one. Then the same `NOT_FOUND` and 404s as C15-none.
- So the token cannot tell a repository the app is not installed on from one that does not exist: `init` and `doctor` tell them apart by C12's lookup, and show the install link built from the slug.
- *C15-pending: not observed (2026-10-19)*, run 9 not run. Assumption kept, unverified: the lookup does not list a request an organisation owner has not approved, so `init` and `doctor` say a request may be pending only after the install link was shown and no installation appeared within the wait.

**C16 — not observed (2026-10-19).** Runs 10 and 11 not run. Assumption kept, unverified: a suspended installation, or a permission the app raised that the installation has not accepted, shows as a refused probe (403 or `FORBIDDEN`), which doctor words with the link to the installation's settings.

**C17 — not observed (2026-10-19).** Run 12 not run. Assumption kept, unverified: a revoked authorization refuses the access token (401) and its refresh, which the refresh-failure rule words "sign in again" ([architecture](architecture.md) section 8).

## Repository & workspace layout

One repository, a Cargo workspace of six shipped crates split along the design's boundaries, so the pure core never depends on I/O, plus one test-only crate.

| Crate | Holds | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `owlshift-core` | Ticket, pipeline, stage, gate, resource; state machine; scheduler; policy. Pure functions, no I/O | `serde` and `schemars`, for derives only (the shared vocabulary) |
| `owlshift-contracts` | Brief, `result.json`, project and personal config, events, git ref layout, format versions; JSON Schemas generated from the types | core |
| `owlshift-adapters` | Tracker (Linear, plus the Markdown test tracker), forge (GitHub), harness (Claude Code; Codex in P5), notifier; each behind a trait with declared capabilities | contracts |
| `owlshift-platform` | Service install and uninstall, process groups and Job Objects, keychain, platform directories, keep-awake | nothing |
| `owlshift-runner` | Daemon loop, executor (worktrees, spawning, isolation check), writer, local store (SQLite) | all of the above |
| `owlshift-cli` | The `owlshift` binary | runner |
| `owlshift-testkit` | Test only, never published and never a dependency of a shipped crate: the fake harness program, hermetic git and the bare-remote fixture, the scenario format and runner, and the scenario tests. It is a crate of its own because Cargo gives a binary's path only to the integration tests of its own package | core, contracts, adapters, runner (the executor it tests), platform |

Beside the crates: `roles/` (default role prompts in Markdown, versioned with the contract), `schemas/` (generated JSON Schemas), `tests/scenarios/` (S1 to S17 with their fixtures, played by the testkit; those that need `owlshift watch`, P3's S9 and P4's S3, S4, S6, S12 and S13, are played in `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` instead: "P4 · The drain", part K), `docs/`.

**Git through the `git` CLI**, not a library: worktrees, pushes and credential helpers then behave exactly as in the user's own terminal. **Harnesses through their own CLI**, never through a model API: the user's subscription login is what runs. Library choices (async runtime, HTTP client, SQLite binding, CLI parser, keychain access) are made at implementation time against current documentation. Made so far: the HTTP client and keychain access (OWL-13, see C4 in the results).

**Repository hygiene from the first commit:** Apache-2.0 licence, English, a contribution guide, and a CI matrix on macOS, Linux and Windows.

## Contracts to write first

Six contracts are written, versioned and schema-checked before any logic, because every component and every future adapter talks through them.

1. **Brief** (runner to role): format version, role, the ticket (text from anyone but the decider quoted as data), the decider, the numbered question-and-answer thread with authors marked, the checkpoint (plan, ledger), resources and zones, the project rules injected for those zones (a rule that names no zone applies to the whole repository; OWL-61), permitted actions, the project's gate commands (from `stack.gate`, so a role never reads the project config or guesses the gate from the repository; OWL-37), the failure of the runner's own run of that gate after the previous Build `done` (`gate_failure`: the failing command, a one-line reason and the end of its output, quoted as data; OWL-16), why the runner refused the previous run's `result.json` or an artifact it named (`result_refusal`: for Build, the latest refused Build result since one was accepted, OWL-180, kept in the ticket ref whatever command runs it, OWL-192; for the answer check, the previous check on the same ask, kept in the ticket ref, OWL-184; for the resolver, the latest refused resolver result until one is accepted or a round is kept, kept in the ticket ref, OWL-191), whether that refusal was of Build's own decisions (`decisions_refused`, which holds the run to asking; OWL-186), every choice those refused results listed (`refused_choices`; OWL-198), and where to write the result.
2. **`result.json`** (role to runner): validated against its JSON Schema, unknown fields rejected, an exit code never trusted in its place.
3. **Project and personal config**: TOML, with published JSON Schemas so editors complete and check them.
4. **Events**: timestamp, project, ticket, run, kind, data; the one stream behind logs, `why`, the UI and usage reports.
5. **Git ref layout**: `refs/owlshift/claims/<ticket>` holds the lease (holder machine, operator, expiry, format); `refs/owlshift/tickets/<ticket>` points to a commit whose tree holds the stage, plan, ledger, questions and findings.
6. **Marked comments**: a first line such as `[owlshift] QUESTIONS · round 2`, plus a machine-readable footer where the tracker can hide it; exact form checked per tracker.

The contracts live in `owlshift-contracts`; their JSON Schemas are generated into `schemas/`, and a test fails when a committed schema drifts from the types (`OWLSHIFT_UPDATE_SCHEMAS=1` rewrites them). The first cut of `result.json` below is format 1, kept as written; the reference is now [`schemas/result.schema.json`](../../schemas/result.schema.json). Refinements made while writing it (OWL-9): question ids are `Q1` to `Qn` in order, in every result, whatever ids the thread's earlier rounds used (OWL-180); `status: questions` needs at least one question; `pr` is allowed only with `status: done`; a follow-up carries `title`, `why`, `evidence`, `source` (`agent`, `reviewer` or `ci`), `done_when` and `blocked_by_parent`; `artifacts` may name `plan`, `ledger`, `findings` and `report`. A document with a newer `format` is refused with an "upgrade" error. Format 2 (OWL-23) adds the answer check's `verdicts`: one per question of the thread's latest ask (its last `questions` or `reask` entry, so after a re-ask only the re-asked questions), in question order, each with the `question` id, a `class` from the four of the answer check in [architecture.md](architecture.md) (`answered`, `partial`, `unanswered`, `counter_question`) and a `reason` that is not blank; only with `status: done`. The coverage rule needs the brief, so it is checked against it (`RunResult::validate_against`, run by the executor on every result), which also refuses verdicts from any role other than `answer_check`. Format 3 (OWL-115) adds a verdict's `reply`, the answer check's answer to a counter-question, which the runner posts as written ([architecture.md](architecture.md), "Answer check"): required on a `counter_question` verdict, refused on any other class, a string that is not blank, never `null`. A check that leaves it out fails like any invalid result, as a verdict without a reason does. The schema carries the rule as an `if`/`then`/`else` on the verdict. Format 10 (OWL-324) gives a question's `options` as `{label, detail}` and requires its `recommendation`, `{option, why}` ("The QUESTIONS layout" below). The schema cannot see the file system, so the runner reads each artifact without following any symbolic link (OWL-26): a link anywhere between the worktree and the file, even one pointing back inside, fails the run with an error naming the field. A hard link is the same leak with nothing to see (OWL-36): an artifact file whose link count is not exactly 1 fails the run the same way, wherever its other names are, since finding them would take a search of the whole volume; a count of 0 is a file deleted after it was opened. The count is read on the handle the artifact is then read from (`fstat` on Unix, `GetFileInformationByHandle` on Windows), so the file checked is the file read. That does not stop the count from changing between the open and the check: a process removing the inside name of a hard link to an outside file in that window leaves a count of 1. What closes that window is the executor stopping the run's whole process tree before the artifacts are read (OWL-15), so no process of the run is alive at read time. Known limits: a process that escaped the tree, and a network or FUSE mount that supports hard links but reports a count of 1. No legitimate artifact has a second name: checked on 2026-09-28 with git 2.54.0, a checkout made by `git worktree add` holds no file with more than one link outside `.git` (`find . -path ./.git -prune -o -path ./target -prune -o -type f -links +1 -print` printed nothing). It also reads at most 1 MiB of each artifact (OWL-35): an artifact seen to hold more before its end, whether huge, sparse or still growing, fails the run with an error naming the field, after reading one byte past the limit and no more. Artifacts are text a model writes, far below 1 MiB; the limit keeps a faulty or hostile role from exhausting the runner's memory or keeping it reading.

The first cut:

```json
{
  "format": 1,
  "status": "questions",
  "summary": "Plan drafted; two choices need the decider.",
  "questions": [
    {
      "id": "Q1",
      "category": "scope",
      "context": "The ticket asks for X; the code already does Y in module Z.",
      "text": "Extend Y, or build X separately?",
      "options": ["Extend Y", "Build X separately"],
      "recommendation": "Extend Y"
    }
  ],
  "decisions": [
    { "question": "Test framework", "decision": "Reuse the existing one", "basis": "Project convention" }
  ],
  "followups": [],
  "artifacts": { "plan": "plan.md", "ledger": "ledger.json" },
  "pr": null
}
```

`status` takes one of `done`, `questions`, `blocked`, `premise_false` or `failed`. When `status` is `done` after Verify, `pr` carries the branch, title and body; the runner opens the PR itself. A run stopped by a usage limit is not a `result.json` status: the runner detects it from the CLI output and records it as an interruption to resume.

## CLI surface for P0 and P1

Everything runs in the foreground; `continue` arrives in P2, `watch` in P3, the background service and its `start`, `stop` and `uninstall` commands in P7, with `pause` and `resume` for its dispatch.

| Command | Step | Behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `owlshift --version` | P0 | Version and format versions |
| `owlshift doctor [--json]` | P0 | git version, 2.39.0 or later (OWL-199), `claude` installed, and `codex` installed and logged in when the personal file declares it (a warning: no role runs on Codex since OWL-347), the agent runs' Claude Code token stored, capabilities of the configured adapters, a Linear project's `[tracker].states` among its team's workflow states; from P4, the admission setup and the proposals inbox (OWL-155); from OWL-336, the GitHub App installed on the repository, its token valid and each permission it asks ("What `doctor` checks", "The GitHub App"); why each problem matters and how to fix it |
| `owlshift config show` | P0 | Effective configuration and the origin of each value |
| `owlshift init` | P1 | Writes a commented `owlshift.toml` for the repository (`--tracker`, `--team`, `--gate`), then stores the tracker secrets and the agent runs' Claude Code token in the system keychain and, from OWL-336, signs in to GitHub through Owlshift's GitHub App, a personal token being the expert option (`--github-token`, `--skip-secrets`, `--replace-secrets`; "`init`", "The GitHub sign-in") |
| `owlshift do TICKET` | P1 | Runs one ticket to a verified PR, in the foreground |
| `owlshift forget TICKET` | P2 | Starts over what Owlshift keeps on a ticket whose ticket ref has no room left or cannot be read, keeping its round count and the refusals its next runs are told (OWL-202) |
| `owlshift continue TICKET` | P2 | Picks a ticket up once its decider has answered, after the quiet window, or restarts a parked one (OWL-122, named by OWL-127) |
| `owlshift watch` | P3 | Continues each ticket whose questions wait, in the foreground, once its decider's reply counts, until Ctrl-C (OWL-152); follows each pull request Owlshift delivered, with a fix run on a red check and a rebase run on a conflict (OWL-208); with `--drain` (P4), also takes admitted tickets in from the ready column, intake first, one Build at a time (OWL-244, "P4 · The drain") |
| `owlshift logs [TICKET] [--last N] [--follow]` | P1 | Events, with the directory of each run's logs |
| `owlshift unquarantine` | P5 | Shows the `unverified` marker a breach left and clears it once a person types the project's name at a terminal (OWL-368, "Telling a quarantine") |
| `owlshift gate [--at COMMIT]` | P5 | Runs the project's gate once as a Build's gate runs it, in a fresh worktree, and prints each command's tail, status and time (OWL-329, P5 part L) |

**What `doctor` checks, as built in P0 (OWL-12).**

- **Git version (OWL-199, decided 2026-10-08).** Owlshift needs git 2.39.0 or later, declared once (`executor::MINIMUM_GIT_VERSION`). Below it the `git` line is a failure, not a warning: every `owlshift do` reads origin's default branch with `symbolic-ref --no-recurse` (`project::sync_checkout`, after the first clone as after a fetch), which an older git refuses, so the warning's note, that it does not block `owlshift do`, would be false. The version is read by C8's rule, from its digits, so what a build adds after it (`(Apple Git-154)`, `.windows.1`, `.rc0`) is dropped and a release candidate of 2.39.0 passes; it is compared part by part as numbers, a missing part counting as 0; a version that cannot be compared keeps the warning of an unfamiliar answer. The floor is the newest git feature the runner, the gate and the agent environment use: `symbolic-ref --no-recurse` (2.39.0); `repack.updateServerInfo` (2.36.0, OWL-197), which an older git ignores, bringing the false isolation breach back silently; `core.fsmonitor` read as a boolean (2.36.0; before, its value names a monitor command); `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, which carries the agent environment's overrides, and `rev-parse --path-format=absolute` (2.31.0); every other option and setting in use is older. Checked on 2026-10-08. In git's source at the tags: `builtin/symbolic-ref.c` and `Documentation/git-symbolic-ref.txt` have no `--no-recurse` at v2.38.0 and have it at v2.39.0, whose `Documentation/RelNotes/2.39.0.txt` announces it; `builtin/repack.c` reads `repack.updateserverinfo` at v2.36.0, not at v2.35.0; `Documentation/config/core.txt` describes `core.fsmonitor` as a command at v2.35.0 and as a boolean at v2.36.0; `Documentation/git-config.txt` and `Documentation/git-rev-parse.txt` have `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` and `--path-format` at v2.31.0, not at v2.30.0. In Linux containers (Docker, linux/aarch64), with the git of alpine:3.15 (2.34.8), alpine:3.16 (2.36.6) and debian:bookworm (2.39.5), and git built from the v2.38.5 and v2.39.0 release tarballs: `git symbolic-ref --quiet --no-recurse refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` in a fresh clone exited 129 with `error: unknown option 'no-recurse'` on 2.34.8, 2.36.6 and 2.38.5, and printed `refs/remotes/origin/main` on 2.39.0 and 2.39.5; after `git -c repack.updateServerInfo=false gc`, `.git/info/refs` existed on 2.34.8 only, and plain `gc` wrote it on every version; `rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir`, a setting passed through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, the isolation check's `status` (`-c core.fsmonitor=false --no-optional-locks status --porcelain=v2 -z --untracked-files=all --ignored=matching --ignore-submodules=dirty`), `commit-tree --no-gpg-sign` and `rev-parse --verify --end-of-options` all succeeded on every version. `cargo test --workspace` in `rust:1.98.1-bookworm` with the 2.39.0 build first on the `PATH` passed 540 tests and failed 2, the same 2 as with `rust:1.98.1-trixie`'s git 2.47.3 (`process::tests::a_hard_kill_with_the_sentinel_stopped_still_stops_the_live_trees` and `a_stopped_group_is_hung_up_then_continued_when_its_parent_ends`, process-group tests that fail in the container whatever the git); the suite covers the hook, monitor and server-info settings (`the_runners_git_runs_no_hook_and_no_monitor`, `the_runners_git_leaves_the_server_info_alone`), the reset credential helper (`an_agent_reaches_none_of_them`), the gate's checks and the isolation check, with real git. Not chosen: a warning, for the reason above. **`owlshift do` and `continue` check it too (OWL-205, decided 2026-10-09).** Both reach git through `project::sync_checkout`, whose first act is `Git::require_minimum_version`: it runs `git --version` outside any repository and, for a git known to be older than the floor, refuses with the version found, the reason and the same two fix steps as the `doctor` line (one source, the `git_floor_reason`, `git_install_advice` and `git_recheck_note` of `executor::git`), before the checkout is cloned, fetched or read, so a run stops in Owlshift's words rather than at `symbolic-ref` with git's "unknown option". That was not yet the first act of the whole command: the project lock, the sandbox checks and the tracker read of `OnDemand::prepare` came before it. **Moved ahead of them (OWL-207, decided 2026-10-10).** `OnDemand::prepare` calls `Git::require_minimum_version` right after `check_team` (which reads nothing) and before the project lock, which creates the project folder, so an old git is refused before any side effect: no folder, no lock, no tracker read. The call in `sync_checkout_within` stays as a second guard for its other callers. `watch` continues tickets through the same `prepare`, so its continues are refused like a typed `continue`; its own reads of the ticket refs are not checked. Only a known old git is refused: a git that does not run, or whose version cannot be read or compared, passes this check, and the next command or `doctor`'s warning says why (a run on such a git is not worse off than before). Tests: `executor::git`'s `only_a_known_old_git_is_below_the_floor`, and `tests/first_clone.rs`'s `a_git_older_than_the_floor_is_refused_before_the_checkout_is_touched`, where a stand-in git answering 2.38.5 is refused with the install step, and `sync_checkout` runs no other command and creates no project folder; and (OWL-207) `tests/on_demand.rs`'s `a_git_older_than_the_floor_is_refused_before_the_lock_and_the_tracker`, where `do` and `continue` on that stand-in git are refused with no project folder, no other git command and no tracker read.
- **Harnesses.** The ones declared under `[harnesses]` in the personal file are checked; when none are declared, `claude` alone is (both `claude` and `codex` until OWL-347). Each must be on the `PATH`, and Codex logged in, following the rules of check C8. A Codex that is missing, logged out or unreadable is a warning, not a failure (OWL-102): no role runs on Codex since OWL-347, so the machine is ready without it, and the note under the line says so and how to get Codex ready (OWL-299); the single place in doctor that decides this is `codex_not_ready_is_a_warning`. A Codex that `codex login status` reports logged in without the file a Codex run copies its login from (`auth.json` in `CODEX_HOME` or `~/.codex`), such as a login kept in the system keyring, is not ready either, with the same warning (OWL-299). Claude Code's own login is information only (OWL-97): agent runs log in with the token the next check looks for, never with the operator's own login, so doctor still reads `claude auth status` and shows that login's state on the `claude` line, but a logged-out login, or one it cannot read, is a `·` line, not a failure, and has no fix. At an untested version, the line is a warning, as below.
- **Agent login (OWL-94).** When `claude` is required and found on the `PATH`, the `claude agent login` line says whether the token agent runs log in with is in the system keychain (service `owlshift`, account `claude-agent`). Only its presence is asked (`Keychain::contains`); the value is never returned, though the macOS Keychain reads the entry to answer and may ask to allow it, as for `do`. Absent: a failure, fixed by `claude setup-token`, then `owlshift init` (`--replace-secrets` replaces a stored one, which `do` names when a run is refused). A keychain that cannot be read: a failure, naming why. Whether the token still works shows at the first run, since `claude auth status` does not check it. With no `claude` found, its own line fails and the token is not asked, which also keeps the CLI's tests, whose `PATH` has no `claude`, away from the real keychain.
- **Harness versions.** A version outside the harness's tested list (`crates/owlshift-adapters/src/harness/tested.rs`), or one doctor cannot read, is a warning, not a failure (OWL-29). Matching is exact: a suffixed build such as `2.1.283-beta.1` is not the release `2.1.283`. A version is listed once the harness contract tests pass on output recorded with it, and the Claude contract tests fail when a recorded fixture carries a version the list lacks. The list only grows. Codex's contract tests replay the C2 and C7 recordings (`tests/codex_fixtures.rs`), which list 0.154.0 since OWL-299 and fail the same way.
- **Configuration files.** The project file is `owlshift.toml` at the root of the git repository holding the current directory. The personal file's location is given in [runtime & operations](runtime-and-operations.md#install-lifecycle--uninstall).
- **The project's gate (OWL-332, decided 2026-10-17).** The `project config` line checks the gate `owlshift do` would run, read as `do` reads it: the default branch's `owlshift.toml`, else the working tree's when that commit has none (OWL-331). An empty `stack.gate` fails the line, since `do` refuses it ("An empty gate" under "The project gate", below), and so does a file at the default branch that `do` would refuse (invalid, or requiring a newer Owlshift). The fix is to list the gate's commands in `owlshift.toml` and merge it to the default branch; its last step is `git fetch origin`. Doctor fetches nothing: it reads `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` in the person's own checkout as their last fetch left it (`project::project_file_at_origin_head`), with the runner's git, read-only, so a stale checkout gives a stale answer that the person's own fetch cures. The line names the commit it read. A default branch unknown there (`origin/HEAD` not set, or naming a branch that no longer resolves) is no failure: the line checks the working tree's file and says how to set `origin/HEAD`. Not chosen: reading the file through GitHub's API, which is fresh but needs a new forge call, its own live check and a stored token; and reading Owlshift's dedicated checkout, which is no fresher, cannot be fetched by the person, and does not exist before the first `do`. Tests: `doctor`'s `an_empty_gate_where_do_reads_it_fails_the_project_config_line`, and `tests/rules.rs`'s `the_default_branchs_project_file_is_read_at_origin_head` on a real repository.
- **Adapters.** Doctor names the configured tracker and the capabilities its adapter declares, from constants, without opening the tracker or the keychain. A required capability the build does not implement yet is a warning, not a failure: `owlshift do` needs only to read tickets and comments, and refusing a project is `init`'s job (OWL-13). Adapter traits exist from a kind's second implementation; a capability contract is frozen only once three exist (principle 8), and may change until then.
- **Tracker states (OWL-147, decided 2026-10-04).** For a Linear project only, a `tracker states` line checks that each state named under `[tracker].states` is exactly one workflow state of the `[tracker].team` team, by the rule the stage moves by (`linear::find_state`: exact name, case included, once). Every key set there is checked, one added later included. `admit = { state }` is checked with the rest under the key `admit.state` (OWL-155; `admit = "ready"` is read as the same state, so it is checked under that key too, OWL-322): the configuration already refuses a name other than `states.ready`, so it is the same name, and the line says so by naming it. Doctor adds the admission lines below ("P4 · The drain", part D). The states line leaves the machine, as do those two lines (a second and a third read of the key, which on macOS may ask again): it reads the Linear API key in the system keychain (`Keychain::read`, which on macOS may ask to allow the access, as for `do` and the agent login) and the team's states from Linear (`LinearTracker::team_states`, one query, read only, 50 states at most). This reverses the earlier choice to keep doctor off the tracker secret, accepted because a misnamed state otherwise shows only as a `stage_not_moved` warning event on each move. A name that matches no state, or several, is a warning, since a failed move never stops `do`; its note lists the team's states and, for a shared name, says to make it unique in Linear. What stops `do` from reading the ticket at all is a failure: no key stored (fix: `owlshift init`), a key Linear refuses (`owlshift init --replace-secrets`), a keychain doctor cannot read, a team key Linear does not know (fix `[tracker].team`). Linear out of reach, or any other error, is a warning that nothing was checked. Only state names, the team key and Linear's error text reach the report, never the key. The doctor unit tests answer through `System::linear_states`, faked; the CLI's tests run doctor outside any project, so they reach neither the keychain nor the network.
- **Forge access (OWL-334, decided 2026-10-17).** Once the `secrets` line finds the GitHub credential (since OWL-336 the GitHub App's token, or a personal token, the expert option), a `forge access` line tries it, read-only, on the repository's default branch.
  - One GraphQL query (`GitHubForge::default_branch`): GitHub opens a pull request only on refs the token can read, which the GitHub App asks (D13) and a fine-grained token gets with contents (read).
  - Readable is ok, and says the pull request permission is not tried. No default branch yet is information.
  - A refusal (`FORBIDDEN`, or HTTP 403 that is no rate limit) fails, naming contents (read). For a personal token, the fix edits the token on GitHub: it keeps its value, so no `init --replace-secrets`. For the app's token, see "The GitHub App", next.
  - A refused token, a repository the token cannot see and an unreadable keychain fail. GitHub out of reach or rate-limited is a warning.
  - The unit tests answer through `System::github_default_branch`, faked; the adapter's are on a scripted transport.
- **The GitHub App (OWL-336, designed on 2026-10-20; built by GA4).** With the app's token found, `forge access` becomes the first of the probes below, each read-only and each naming what is missing and the one link that fixes it.
  - `github app`: the app installed on the repository, found by C12's lookup (2026-10-19): `user/installations`, each installation's `app_slug` against the slug, then its repositories to the last page. GitHub answers a repository the app is not installed on as one that does not exist (C15, 2026-10-19), so only this lookup tells them apart. Missing, a failure whose fix is the install link `https://github.com/apps/<slug>/installations/new`, built from D13's slug constant or its `github_app_slug` override, then `owlshift doctor`. When no installation appears after the link, the note says that a request on an organisation's repository waits for an owner to approve it (C15-pending, unverified: not observed on 2026-10-19).
  - `github token`: valid, or expired and refreshed through the Writer's refresh under the account lock, reported `expired, refreshed` (C13, 2026-10-19). A refresh that fails follows the refresh-failure rule (architecture section 8): a failure worded "sign in again", whose fix is `owlshift init`, which signs in again over the dead token ("`init`", "The GitHub sign-in"). A revoked authorization is told from an expired token by the stored expiry and the 401 (C17, unverified: not observed on 2026-10-19).
  - One probe per permission the app asks, each a read of C11 on the repository (2026-10-19): contents, checks, commit statuses, actions and metadata; pull requests (write) is not tried, as `forge access` says. A refusal fails, naming the permission, with the installation's settings page as its fix, where the account accepts the app's permissions. A suspended installation, or a permission the app raised that the installation has not accepted, is assumed to show as such a refusal (C16, unverified: not observed on 2026-10-19).
  - A project whose credential is a personal token gets `forge access` and the same probes, then always a warning, which does not block. Its note lists the features the probes found refused, if any, which the token cannot serve, such as watch's reading of checks for a fine-grained token, which GitHub gives no Checks permission (OWL-334). A token that reads every probe, a classic one or one from `gh auth token`, still gets the warning, without the list, so every existing install is told of the sign-in, as decided on 2026-10-18 (OWL-336, round 1). An existing install keeps its token until then.
    - *A token kept because the app cannot be installed.* Doctor cannot tell an install from before OWL-336 from a token chosen with `--github-token`: both are a personal token under the project's `github` account, with no app token. So one warning serves both, and says what each case does: where the app can be installed on the repository, sign in with `owlshift init`; where it cannot, the token is the expert option, and the warning is information that stays, listing what the token cannot serve. Its fix names `owlshift init` for the first case only: for the second, `init` ends in the installation wait, exits 1 and keeps the personal token ("`init`", "The GitHub sign-in").
- **Data directory (OWL-109).** A `·` line in the Project section, `data directory`, prints the path where `owlshift do` keeps its event log, its dedicated checkouts and its worktrees (`owlshift_platform::paths::data_dir`), written with `~` for the home folder, and says `from OWLSHIFT_DATA_DIR` when that variable sets it. When the system has none, the line says so and to set `OWLSHIFT_DATA_DIR` to an absolute path, as `owlshift logs` does. It is information only, never a warning or a failure, and changes neither the exit status nor what blocks `do`. Doctor only reports the path: it does not open, create or list the folder. The JSON report carries the same line.
- **Exit status.** A failed check makes the exit status 1, in text and in JSON. Warnings do not: none blocks `owlshift do`.

**How `doctor` reports, as reworked in OWL-99.** A new user's first command has to say what blocks them and how to get past it without reading the code.

- **Sections.** Tools (git, the harnesses), Agent isolation (sandbox, sentinel, sentinel test, agent login), Project (project and personal configuration, tracker, tracker states on a Linear project, data directory), under a header naming the version and the operating system. One line per check: a symbol, the subject, the detail. `✓` ok, `!` warning, `✗` failure, `·` information.
- **Warnings** carry a note under their line: that they do not block `owlshift do`, and why, with what to do if anything.
- **Summary.** After a rule: when a check failed, `✗ Not ready: N problems to fix before owlshift do`, then one numbered block per failure: **Why** (what breaks and why the check exists) and **Fix** (numbered steps, each command whole on its own line with its note after it, ending with `owlshift doctor` to check). Only failures are counted: a Codex that is not ready is a warning (above), so it does not make the machine not ready. When nothing failed: ready, and the next command: `owlshift do TICKET` when the project has its `owlshift.toml`, `owlshift init` in a repository without one, `owlshift doctor` again from the project's repository when run outside one. Doctor does not look for the forge secret, nor for the tracker's but on a Linear project (the tracker states line above), since each keychain read may prompt on macOS, so the `do` line adds that `owlshift init` stores a secret `do` says is missing.
- **Every failure carries a why and at least one step**, and every warning its note: the doctor unit tests check this on every report they build, so a new failure path without them fails the test that reaches it.
- **Layout.** Colour (ANSI) only when standard output is a terminal, `NO_COLOR` is unset or empty (no-color.org), `TERM` is not `dumb`, and not on native Windows. Text wraps at the terminal's width (read with `tcgetwinsize`, the `termios` feature of rustix, no new crate), 80 columns when standard output is not a terminal, 40 at least; widths are counted in characters. A command, and a span in backticks, is never broken. A path under the home folder is written `~`, whole components only. Everything is escaped as in `logs` (`printable`) before it is wrapped, so no control character or bidirectional override from the machine reaches the terminal.
- **`--json`.** The same report for scripts and the P8 web UI: `owlshift` (the version), `ready`, `problems`, `next` (`do`, `init` or `from_repository`, `null` until ready) and `checks`, each with `status` (`ok`, `info`, `warn`, `fail`), `section` (`tools`, `agent_isolation`, `project`), `subject`, `detail`, `why` (`null` for ok and info) and `fix`, a list of `{"run": command, "note": text or null}` and `{"do": text}` steps. The closing `owlshift doctor` step is the text's own. Strings are as the checks wrote them, `~` included: escaping them for a terminal or a page is the reader's job.

**The test tracker** (published as the Markdown tracker in P9) keeps one folder per ticket in the repository: `tickets/PROJ-1/ticket.md` with a front matter and the description, and `tickets/PROJ-1/comments/` with one file per comment, named by timestamp and author. Answering a question is adding a file; everything stays diffable in git. The format, as built in P0 (OWL-11):

- `ticket.md` opens with a TOML front matter between two `+++` lines, then the description. Keys: `title`, `author`, `stage` (the visible stage: a state name as mapped under `[tracker].states`), `priority` (`urgent`, `high`, `medium`, `low` or `unset`, the default), `assignee` (optional: the decider), `labels`, `blocked_by` (ticket ids) and `related` (ticket ids: a proposal's parent, OWL-267), all empty by default. An unknown key is refused. TOML keeps the project to one configuration language; the choice is revisited before the tracker is published in P9, where YAML front matter is the wider convention.
- A comment is `comments/<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>-<author>.md`: the UTC time in ISO 8601 basic format, which has no colon (Windows refuses one in a file name) and sorts in time order, then the author (ASCII letters, digits, `_` and `-`). The body is the file's content. Several comments can share a second, as on Linear, which keeps milliseconds (OWL-133). The first comment of a second has no suffix. The n-th one carries `.<n>` right after the time (`20261003T101500Z.2-owlshift.md`), with n from 2 to 1,000,000 and no leading zero. Its id is the name without `.md`. The `.` is outside the author alphabet, so an author made of digits and `-` still parses unambiguously. Posting takes the next n of that second, whoever wrote the comments already there. Comments read in order of time, then n, then file name. A comment's time is its second plus n − 1 microseconds (OWL-136, "The answer check", "When it runs"), so the times order the comments as they read, and posting past the millionth comment of a second is an error; files of one second without a suffix, written before it, share the second's time. A file is never overwritten: a poster that finds its name already taken gets an error. Two posters racing on one ticket may therefore tie on an n, and so on a time.
- A reply (OWL-146) opens with a TOML front matter between two `+++` lines holding `reply_to`, the id of the comment it answers; the body follows. Any other comment file is its body alone, so a body the runner writes never opens with `+++`.
- Changing the visible stage rewrites the `stage` line of the front matter and nothing else, a one-line diff.
- A proposal (OWL-267, decided 2026-10-14; architecture section 5, "Follow-ups", "Where") is a new folder named after its parent's key, one past the highest number among that key's folders: a proposal of `DEMO-1` beside `DEMO-7` is `DEMO-8`, `DEMO-007` reading as 7, and a folder not ending in a number is not counted. The folder is claimed by creating it, which fails when it exists, so a ticket is never overwritten and a number taken meanwhile moves on to the next, 64 at most. Its `ticket.md` holds `title`, `author = "owlshift"`, `stage = "Triage"`, `blocked_by` the parent when the follow-up needs its code and `related` the parent, then, after a blank line, the description; no priority, assignee or label. `Triage` is the proposals inbox, a stage name fixed as `Done` is (part B of "P4 · The drain"). `related` is written on the proposal only, so a filing is one file. A parent missing or not ending in a number, or a title blank or on several lines, is refused before any write. A folder a person deleted frees its number for the next proposal, git keeping the old one. Rejected: a counter file, one more file to keep in step with the folders.
- A malformed file is refused with an error that names it.

**The test bench** (OWL-11) lives in `owlshift-testkit` and plays a ticket end to end without a model, a network or a tracker account.

- **The fake harness**, `owlshift-fake-harness --brief <brief.json> --reply <reply.toml>`, runs in the worktree like a real harness. The reply says what to do, in this order: wait (`delay_ms`); create and switch to another branch (`switch_branch`); write files (`files`) and commit them (`commit`, dated `date`, which the scenario runner sets to the virtual clock); write files in the main checkout (`main_checkout`); copy a prepared `result.json`, valid or not, to the brief's result path (`result`); print (`stdout`, `stderr`), or report a usage limit (`usage_limit`, a reset time: it prints `owlshift-fake-harness: usage limit reached · resets <time>` on stderr and writes no result); exit (`exit_code`: 0 by default, 1 with a usage limit). It is a compiled program, not a script, so it behaves the same on the three platforms. `switch_branch` and `main_checkout` break isolation on purpose.
- **A scenario** is a TOML file in `tests/scenarios/` with a fixture folder beside it (named after the file, or by its optional `fixture = "<name>"` key, a sibling folder: a scenario on another one's setup brings only its own prepared results, added to that folder under names of their own): the project repository, seeded as `main` into a local bare remote, and the prepared results. Its steps are `dispatch`, `run` (a reply for the fake harness), `comment` (a person comments), `answer` (a reply for the fake harness, run as the answer check: "The answer check" below) and `resolve` (a reply for the fake harness, run as the resolver: "The resolver" below). A `run` or `resolve` step may add `tracker_refuses_questions = true` (OWL-191, off by default): the tracker refuses the QUESTIONS comment the step posts, and the driver plays it as `owlshift do` plays a failed post, opening no round, keeping nothing of it and leaving the ticket at its stage; every other comment posts as usual. A step may instead add `ticket_ref_refuses_round = true` (OWL-196, off by default, not with the first): the QUESTIONS comment posts and the visible stage moves, but the ticket ref refuses the write keeping the round, so the driver keeps no ask, clears no kept refusal and puts the core state back, as `owlshift do` stops on a keeping that failed after the post. Any step can carry expectations on the core state (stage, waiting, round, re-asks, failed runs), the last brief (its authors, its entries in order, its latest ask, its decisions, the questions it gives the resolver), the tracker (visible stage, comments, text the last one holds or lacks) and the remote (branch pushed, file contents); a failed expectation names the scenario, the step, the expected and the found value. Time is virtual, the scenario's start plus one minute per step, so a run is reproducible to the byte. An optional `timeout_ms` sets the executor's deadline for each run and for the gate, 60 s by default; an optional `gate` replaces the fixture's `stack.gate`, so short scenarios share a fixture; an optional `setup` replaces its `stack.setup` (OWL-330), and a step may expect the failure of the setup before its Build harness (`setup_failure`); an optional `runner_env` adds variables to the runner's environment the agent environment is built from, which the fixture's `stack.gate_env` picks from (OWL-44); a fixture's optional `personal.toml`, the operator's personal file, holds the `allow_gate_env` that declaration is checked against, as when the configuration is loaded (OWL-63). `a_declared_variable_reaches_the_gate` gives its gate `git --config-env=owlshift.probe=OWLSHIFT_GATE_PROBE config --get owlshift.probe`, which needs the variable the same way under sh and cmd: checked on 2026-09-29 with git 2.54.0, it prints the value when the variable is set and exits 128 with "missing environment variable" when it is not; the scenario fails the same way when the fixture stops declaring it, and does not start when the fixture's personal file stops allowing it.
- **Runs go through the executor** (OWL-15), on the fake harness. A stand-in driver plays the writer (OWL-18) until it exists, and only as far as the scenarios need: it builds the brief from the tracker, maps the executor's outcome onto a core event, posts the DECISION, questions, re-ask, RESUME and PARKED comments, sets the visible stage and pushes the branch. The writer replaces it; the scenario files stay.
- **Git is hermetic.** Every git command the bench runs itself, the executor's included, starts with no inherited `GIT_*` variable, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` in the fixture's folder, and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` pointing to a file the fixture writes: identity, no signing, `core.autocrlf=false`, empty ignore and attributes files and hooks folder of its own, and no server information when git repacks (`repack.updateServerInfo=false`, OWL-240: "The bench's own git" below). The host's configuration (signing, hooks, ignore rules, line-ending conversion) never reaches a test. Author and committer dates come from the virtual clock, so commit ids are reproducible too. The fake harness and the executor's credential probes are the exception: they run with the agent environment the executor gives every agent, which drops every `GIT_*` variable, so the fixture writes the same file, less the server-information setting the agent environment carries itself, where git looks for its global configuration under `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, and the reply carries the commit date. Its git still reads the host's system configuration, whose keys that matter here (identity, signing, `core.autocrlf`, hooks, ignore and attributes files) the fixture's file overrides. Check: `git::tests::git_reads_only_the_fixture_configuration`, in `owlshift-testkit`, gives git a hostile environment (`GIT_DIR`, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`) and a hostile home (a global configuration, an ignore file of `*`, attributes with `eol=crlf`). It then requires every configuration entry to come from the fixture's file or the repository's own, and no host ignore rule or attribute to apply. It passed on ubuntu, macOS and Windows in CI on 2026-09-28 ([run 36407077420](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/actions/runs/36407077420)). On Windows, then, no entry of Git for Windows' own system configuration was read. Git for Windows prints a configuration file's path in quotes when the path holds backslashes; the first run of the check failed on that quote alone.

**The answer check** (OWL-116, decided 2026-10-02). The `answer_check` role (`roles/answer_check.md`) reads its brief alone and gives a verdict on each question of the thread's latest ask (`result.json` format 2, above; format 3 adds the reply to a counter-question, format 4 the resolver's resolutions), judging where each stands after the decider's last word on it. Only the decider's comments since that round's `questions` entry answer, and everything in the brief, theirs included, is data it classifies, never instructions.

- **Its event.** `owlshift_runner::answer_check::event`: a `done` result folds its verdicts into one core event (`Event::from_answer_check`). Any other status is a failed run, since questions, a block or a false premise mean nothing while the ticket waits, and every other outcome maps as for any run (`on_demand::core_event`). A failed check counts toward the failed runs, so a second one parks the ticket. A check whose result was refused is one of them, and the next check on the same ask is told why (OWL-184, "A refused answer check" below), but one told of no earlier refusal never spends the last attempt (OWL-190, "A refused answer check at the last attempt" below).
- **Acting on it.** Every check that gives its verdicts keeps them with the ask they judge, in the ticket ref (OWL-123). Answered: a `[owlshift] RESUME · round N` comment (`writer::ResumeComment`) restates every question of the round with what was understood of its answer, the reason of the latest verdict on it (`answer_check::understood`: a question settled before a re-ask reads the earlier check's), and the ticket resumes at its stage. The RESUME is posted before the answered state is kept, as the RE-ASK is: when the post fails, nothing of the check is kept and the next `continue` checks the same answers again, one more answer-check run; a post that went through and a ref write that then failed names the comment. Incomplete: a `[owlshift] RE-ASK · round N` comment (`writer::ReaskComment`) lists only the questions whose verdict is `partial` or `unanswered`, each with the verdict's reason, and says which re-ask of the limit it is; the thread then holds a `reask` entry of those questions under their ids, so the next check judges them alone. At the limit the core parks instead. Counter-question: nothing is re-asked and no re-ask is counted; the reply the check wrote with each `counter_question` verdict (format 3) is posted in a `[owlshift] REPLY` comment (`writer::ReplyComment`, OWL-128), and the ticket keeps waiting for the decider. One REPLY per check, each counter-question under its question id with its text and the reply, flattened, as one RE-ASK holds every open question: the decider reads one answer to one comment. The pairs come from the latest ask (`answer_check::counter_replies`). The REPLY is posted before the check is kept, as the RE-ASK is: when the post fails, nothing of the check is kept and the next `continue` checks the same answers again; a post that went through and a ref write that then failed names the comment, and the next `continue` may post a second REPLY.
- **Parking** (OWL-123). Every park, of a Build run or of an answer check, at the re-ask limit, after a second failed run, on a block or a breach, posts a `[owlshift] PARKED` comment (`writer::ParkedComment`): the reason; the last failure or what a blocked run said, flattened; at the re-ask limit, the round's questions still open with the verdicts' reasons; and what restarts the ticket. That is `owlshift continue TICKET` when a ticket ref keeps its state, after answering the open questions at the re-ask limit, and `owlshift do TICKET` when no round was ever asked, so nothing was kept and `do` runs it again. After a breach the comment keeps what the run changed off the ticket, since it describes the runner's machine, and says a person checks that machine first, Owlshift refusing the project there until the quarantine is cleared, then runs `continue`, or `do` if the project's checkout was deleted (which loses the ticket ref). The comment is posted after the parked state is kept, and also when keeping it failed, as when a run that broke isolation moved the ticket ref: the ticket stopped either way. A post that failed leaves the park as it is, and `do` or `continue` says so (`Stop::Parked`'s `unposted`).
- **When it runs.** Once answers arrive: a decider comment newer than the latest ask, or than the comments the last check that gave its verdicts read (`answer_check::new_answer`; OWL-122 widened OWL-116's "the last check that found a counter-question" to every check with verdicts, so an answer already judged is never judged again on its own, after a restart included). Newer is the comment's last edit against the ask comment's time, both the tracker's, never this machine's clock, and a comment is the decider's as the brief marks it. A failed, interrupted or quarantined check reads nothing, so the next one runs on the same answers. Then the decider's reply must count: the quiet window, or `go` (OWL-127, below). Decided on 2026-10-04 (OWL-136): those times are compared at the tracker's own precision, and they order the comments of one second, so an answer posted in the same second as its ask, or as the comment the last check read, is newer and reads after the ask in the brief's thread. That is a requirement on every tracker adapter (`tracker::Comment::created_at`): Linear's times keep milliseconds (`createdAt` `2026-09-28T13:30:44.787Z` in the exchanges recorded live for check C4 on 2026-09-28), and the Markdown tracker reads the n-th comment of a second n − 1 microseconds after it ("The test tracker", above). So `thread`, `new_answer`, `late_comments` and watch's hold go on comparing times, unchanged by design; the stand-in driver runs the same check (OWL-126), and its scenarios cannot post in an ask's second since they advance a minute a step. A tie still reads before and is not newer: two Linear comments of one millisecond, Markdown files of one second without a suffix, two Markdown posters racing to one n, and a comment posted in the second of a deleted last one, which takes its n. A ticket ref kept on the Markdown tracker before this change holds its times at the second and is not migrated, since that tracker is unpublished until P9: an answer the last check read, posted second or later in its second, is checked once more, and a decider comment posted before an ask in the ask's own second would count as an answer to it. `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` posts an answer in its ask's second, then the check's REPLY and the next answer in that second too, and delivers. Rejected: the tracker's list order in the runner, which needs the id of the last comment the check read beside `checked_through` (`questions.json` format 5) and the list's order in every comparison, watch's included, for a gap only a tracker of whole seconds has; and leaving it, since an answer the runner ignores leaves the ticket waiting with no word of why.
- **The brief's thread.** Each ask, a round's `questions` or a `reask`, takes the place of the runner's comment that posted it, found by its tracker id (`on_demand::thread`); an ask whose comment is gone keeps its place by time, and a comment of the same time as an ask reads before it ("When it runs": the times order the comments of one second). Every other comment stays a `comment` entry, the runner's own RESUME, REPLY and PARKED comments included, which read as `owlshift` since they parse as marked comments. A REPLY is kept in the thread on purpose (OWL-128): the decider's next answer is often read against it ("then no sign-off"), and as an `owlshift` entry it is context and never an answer, even when posted through the decider's own Linear key, so it never makes a new answer nor moves what the last check read. The stand-in driver builds its threads with the same `on_demand::thread` (OWL-126).
  - *Owlshift's own bodies* (OWL-357, decided 2026-10-19). Every run re-read the bodies of the runner's own comments, which repeat what the brief already carries from the ticket ref. A comment the thread does not replace by an ask or a decision, that parses as a marked comment, ends with its footer and was never edited, now reads by kind (`on_demand::brief_body`). A PLAN, RESUME or DELIVERY keeps its header line (its kind and round), its time and its author, then one sentence saying what was left out and where it is, and the tracker's id of the comment: the plan it showed is kept in the ticket ref, and the latest is the brief's `plan`, which the answer check is now given too, its approval question pointing to the PLAN comment; a RESUME's questions are its round's `questions` entry and the answers are the decider's own comments, the answer check's reading of them being left out (since OWL-359, below, the questions and that reading are the round's `answered` entry); a DELIVERY's report is on the ticket only, and its change on the pull request.
