A PARKED or INTAKE comment, and a QUESTIONS, RE-ASK or DECISION comment the ref no longer carries (after `owlshift forget`, from an attempt not kept, or a copy), is cut to 4,096 bytes, its start kept, the cut marked and the comment's id named. A REPLY stays whole, within the comment's own 100,000 characters: a cut would drop the later counter-questions' replies, which the decider's next answer may be read against. Every other comment is whole, byte for byte: a person's, a marked one without its footer, and an edited one, since the runner never edits a comment, so an edit is a person's. The relation does not change: each still reads as `owlshift`. Accepted limit: a person who posts a whole copy of an Owlshift comment, its header first and its footer last, and never edits it, has it read short like the runner's own; it hides only that person's copy, which reads as `owlshift` data anyway, and a quoted reply, whose lines start with `>`, is not one. No format changed: the entry is still a `comment`. Rejected: a typed thread entry, which needs a brief format and every role's front matter for the same text; recording the id of each comment the runner posts in the ref, which changes every post site and never cuts the comments already posted; and recognising the runner by author, which needs the tracker adapters to name their own writer and cuts nothing on Linear without the app, where Owlshift posts as a person's account. Tested in `on_demand::tests` (`owlshifts_plan_resume_and_delivery_read_short_and_a_persons_comment_whole`, `an_edited_marked_comment_reads_whole`, `a_runners_comment_the_ref_does_not_carry_is_cut_to_the_cap`) and on the bench (`a_later_brief_holds_owlshifts_comments_short_and_the_deciders_whole`, a review loop's Build after a plan approved, built and delivered).
    - Measured on 2026-10-19 on OWL-160's briefs from the drain of 2026-10-18, read from their run folders. Each runner's `comment` entry was rebuilt through `brief_body`, with a 36-character id standing in for Linear's, and the brief rendered again with `Brief::render`, which gives each saved file byte for byte before the change. Every one of those comments was posted by the Linear app user, which no person edits, so none counts as edited. The first Build (run 20261018T203335Z) goes from 384,057 to 110,889 bytes; the answer check before it (20261018T203254Z), now given the plan, from 306,689 to 105,235; the last resolver (20261018T225601Z) from 400,979 to 127,811; the last Build (20261018T225623Z) from 397,213 to 124,045. What the first Build's keeps: the plan (62 KB), the `questions` entries of the three rounds (13 KB) and the resolver's four `decision` entries (16 KB), each carried once, the rules and the ticket (8 KB); the decider's own comments weigh 380 bytes. The target is therefore a first Build brief of OWL-160 under 120 KB, not the 80 KB first asked (decided by the maintainer on 2026-10-19): the ticket's count gave 30 KB to the person's comments, which are in fact the ref's questions and decisions, carried once, and under 80 KB would mean cutting the plan or them. Their long texts (a question's option details, a decision's question) are a separate change.
  - *Settled questions and decisions* (OWL-359, decided 2026-10-19). After OWL-357 a run still re-read, whole, the rounds the decider had answered and the questions the resolver had decided: each question's `context` and `options`, the plan approval question's one option per fork included, which the run had already acted on. What a run needs from a settled round is what was asked and what was answered; from a decision, what was asked and what was decided; the options matter only to the one judging an open ask, and to the reader of a reply that names one. So, in brief format 19 (`on_demand::thread`):
    - A round is answered when every question of it has a latest verdict, over its asks (`answer_check::understood`, which the RESUME comment restates), of class `answered`. It reads as one `answered` entry in the place of its first ask, its re-asks folded into it: its `round`, its `at`, and per question its `id`, its `text` and `understood`, the verdict's `reason`, which the decider read as the RESUME's "Understood:" line. Its `category`, `context`, `options` and `recommendation` are left out; the round's QUESTIONS comment keeps them on the ticket. Any other ask is whole: the open one, which is the latest ask an answer check judges, matching `Q1: B` against its `options`, a round partly answered or with a counter-question, and one with no verdict kept (format 2 of `questions.json`). `Brief::latest_ask` ignores `answered` entries, so an answer check on a thread whose every round is answered is refused ("asks no question") rather than judging readings.
    - A `decision` entry's `question` is its `id` and `text` (`DecidedQuestion::Short`): the decision is written to stand under the question, and says what was chosen. It is whole (`DecidedQuestion::Whole`) when the decision is `reversed` or a comment replies directly to its DECISION comment, since a reply may name an option by its letter. `decision`, `basis` and `reversed` are unchanged, and a correction's `reversed` entries keep the whole question.
    - The answer check's `reason` for an `answered` verdict now names the chosen option's letter and label, or gives the answer in the decider's words (roles/answer_check.md), since later runs read the round by it alone.
    - Accepted limits: a reading kept before this change may name only a letter ("A, the recommendation"), and stays as kept; a decider comment that, after a round is answered, changes one of its answers by an option's letter alone reads against the question's text and reading only, the options being on the ticket; a reply threaded under a reply of a DECISION comment does not keep its question whole. Rejected: keeping the `questions` shape with `context` and `options` emptied, under which `Q1: A` reads against nothing and a field changes meaning silently, for about 85.7 KB on OWL-160's first Build; dropping rounds and decisions older than the given plan, which Build is told to follow; rendering the brief as compact JSON, which the agents read with line-based tools. Tested in `on_demand::tests` (`an_answered_round_reads_as_its_readings_and_folds_its_reasks`, `a_round_not_all_answered_reads_whole`, `a_decision_reads_short_unless_the_decider_replied_to_it`), the contract tests (`brief_rejections`) and on the bench (scenarios `numbering`, `reask` and `decisions_and_asks_take_their_comments_places_in_the_thread`, where the answer check reads the round whole and the Build after it reads `answered`).
    - Measured on 2026-10-19 on the same four OWL-160 briefs, with OWL-357's method: each brief's comments rebuilt from its saved `comment` entries (36-character stand-in ids, no edit, no reply: OWL-160's decider comments are its three answers, and none of its decisions was reversed), and its asks and decisions from the ticket ref's `questions.json`, the first Build's as the ref held it before that run and the others as it holds it now, whose asks are the same and whose decisions are taken up to each run's start; for the answer check, round 3's verdict, which that check gave, is left out, and the plan is given as OWL-357 measured it. The rebuilt thread puts each entry where the saved one was. OWL-357's sizes, with this change's RESUME sentence (82 bytes longer), against this change's: the first Build 111,135 to 88,907 bytes; the answer check, its round 3 still whole, 105,399 to 90,823; the last resolver 128,057 to 102,374; the last Build 124,291 to 94,764. The first Build's thread is now 14.7 KB; the rest is the plan (64.6 KB as rendered), the rules (4.9 KB) and the ticket (2.9 KB). The target is a first Build brief under 90 KB, not 80 KB (decided by the maintainer on 2026-10-19): with every question and decision removed it would still be 78.4 KB, so 80 KB means cutting the plan, the given plan's `forks` and `alternatives` (10.6 KB, the design's choices among options, which the approval question restated) being the candidate for Build's brief, a separate change.
  - *The plan in Build's brief* (OWL-365, decided with the maintainer on 2026-10-19). After OWL-359 the plan was most of the first Build brief of OWL-160, and its `forks` and `alternatives`, the design's choices among approaches, which the Build does not follow (roles/build.md, "A given plan"), were the part left to cut. `design::build_plan` gives the Build the plan of `given_plan` with those two lists emptied, its base unchanged; every other brief that carries the plan, the resolver's `asked_by.plan` for a Build's questions included, keeps it whole, and `Plan`'s serialization, whose canonical text an approval's hash binds, is unchanged. The design role writes each choice into the approach or the steps it shapes (roles/design.md). Brief format 20.
    - Accepted limit: a plan kept before this change may lean on a fork in a step ("as fork 2 chose"); the Build then cannot follow that step and asks, its `replan` going through the resolver, which reads the plan whole.
    - Measured on 2026-10-19 on OWL-160's first Build brief (run 20261018T203335Z), the first call's figure: the saved brief, rendered again with the JSON pretty-printing `Brief::render` uses (two-space indent, a final newline), gives the saved file byte for byte; with its plan's `forks` (ten) and `alternatives` (thirteen) emptied it is 12,186 bytes lighter (forks 6,956, alternatives 5,230). The rest of the brief is unchanged by this change, so OWL-359's rebuilt brief goes from 88,907 to 76,721 bytes, under the 80 KB first asked; its plan from 64.6 KB to 52.5 KB as rendered. Later Build briefs, with findings, reviews or a longer thread, weigh more.
- **The ticket ref** (OWL-122, decided 2026-10-02). `refs/owlshift/tickets/<ticket>` points to a commit whose tree holds `state.json` (required; the core state: stage, waiting, round, re-asks, failed runs and, since format 3 (OWL-55), the fix passes pushed since the pull request was last read green and the repairs pushed since the delivery, both counted only at Watch; a format-2 `state.json` is still read with both at 0, and is refused if it carries either field, and is written back as format 3) and `questions.json` (required; contract `TicketQuestions`, [`schemas/ticket-questions.schema.json`](../../schemas/ticket-questions.schema.json): each ask with the id and time of the comment that posted it and, since format 2 (OWL-124), its decider, since format 3 (OWL-123) the verdicts of the latest check on it, and what the last check read; since format 4 (OWL-138) the resolver's decisions, "The resolver" below; since format 5 (OWL-184) why the latest check on an ask had its result refused, "A refused answer check" below; since format 6 (OWL-192) why Build's latest result was refused and whether that holds the next Build run to asking, "A Build refusal kept across commands" below; since format 7 (OWL-191) why the resolver's latest result was refused, "A refused resolver result told to its replay" below; since format 8 (OWL-195) every refused list of choices in Build's refusal; since format 9 (OWL-198) those choices typed; since format 14 (OWL-345) the role whose run asked a kept decision, "The resolver" below). A format-2 `questions.json` is still read, its asks without verdicts, as is a format-3 one, without decisions, a format-4 one, without a refusal, a format-5 one, without Build's refusal, a format-6 one, without the resolver's refusal, a format-7 one, its Build refusal upgraded as format 6's is, and a format-8 one, its lists kept as text, a field a later format added being refused in an older one, and each is written back as format 9: a round asked before OWL-123 then resumes with a RESUME that says, for a question settled before its re-ask, that the earlier check's reason was not kept. Since OWL-209 the tree also holds `delivery.json` (optional; contract `TicketDelivery`, format `DELIVERY_FORMAT` in `owlshift_contracts::format`; since format 2 (OWL-218) a fix or rebase push under way keeps its run's `report`, and a landed one its `owed` report until a pass posts it; a format-1 `delivery.json` is still read without them, refused if it carries either, and written back as the current format, which an Owlshift older than OWL-218 refuses; since format 3 (OWL-146) a correction run's push is kept the same way, its report naming its questions, and a format-1 or format-2 one carrying a correction's push or report is refused; since format 4 (OWL-236) the first delivery's report, with its `notes`, and a correction's that pushed nothing (`unchanged`) are owed too, and a format-1 to format-3 one carrying either is refused), the pull request the ticket's delivery follows, described in the OWL-209 entry below: it is read when present, checked like the two others, and written only when the ticket has a delivery, so a ref with no delivery has no `delivery.json`; `owlshift forget` drops it. Read compatibility was chosen over the refusal OWL-124 gave format 1, so a round waiting for its answers is not lost with the ref. `owlshift_runner::ticket_ref` writes it with git plumbing in the dedicated checkout: blobs with no filter, a tree keeping any other file of the previous one but the three above, which the write replaces or, for `delivery.json` when the record has none, leaves out, a commit whose parent is the previous one, made with `--no-gpg-sign` and the identity `Owlshift`, and `update-ref` from the commit read, so a ref moved meanwhile is refused. It reads it with `for-each-ref` on the exact name (`show-ref --verify --hash` exits 128 on an absent ref, checked that day with git 2.54.0) and refuses a symbolic ref (since OWL-202: Owlshift never writes one), a tree without `state.json` or `questions.json` as regular blobs (or a `delivery.json` that is not one), a file that does not parse, or a ticket that waits for a round its asks do not end with; the refusal names `owlshift forget TICKET` (OWL-202, below). Each file is read only up to 1 MiB (`ticket_ref::MAX_FILE`), and since OWL-200 (decided 2026-10-08) a write of which any file, `delivery.json` included, would pass it is refused before any git command, so a write that succeeds never leaves a ref the next command refuses: the error names the file, its size, the cap and, since OWL-202, `owlshift forget TICKET`, the ref keeps its previous state, and the command stops through the path every failed write takes (a not-kept stop after a posted comment, a refusal otherwise), its message carrying the error. Refusing was chosen over trimming what the ref keeps, which would drop asks or refusals a later command needs. Asks and decisions accumulate, so the refusal recurs at each later command until `owlshift forget TICKET` starts the record over, which also lifts watch's hold. `commit-tree` did not read `commit.gpgSign` (same day: it wrote a commit with `commit.gpgSign=true` and a failing `gpg.program`); the flag stays, so a person's signing setup never stops the runner. A run that touches the ref breaks the isolation check, which covers every ref but the run's branch and the remote-tracking refs; the runner writes it between runs only. **Deviation from architecture section 7:** the ref lives only in the dedicated checkout, not on the git remote. Pushing it comes with claims (P7), when another runner can read it; until then it would also publish the questions' text on the forge before the artifact store is configurable. Deleting the dedicated checkout (the recovery from a quarantine) loses it: the comments stay on the ticket as the runner's plain comments, and a new `do` asks from round 1 again.
- **`owlshift forget TICKET`** (OWL-202, decided 2026-10-10). A ticket ref with no room for its next write, or that cannot be read, is started over by an Owlshift command instead of git run by hand in the dedicated checkout (`owlshift_runner::forget`, `ticket_ref::forget`).
  - What it does. It holds the project's lock, so it is refused while `do`, `continue` or `watch` holds it (`Stop::Busy`, whose message names those three), and never changes a ref one of them read; it opens neither the tracker, the forge nor the keychain, and the ticket's stage on the tracker moves only at the next `do`. With no dedicated checkout (none is made) or no ref, it is refused: nothing to forget. A record that reads gets a new commit on top of it, the old one its parent, so nothing is deleted from git: `state.json` at Ready with the round count kept and re-asks and failed runs at zero, where `do` starts, so the next round is the one after; `questions.json` with only Build's and the resolver's kept refusals, which the next runs are told and which keep a refused choice with the decider ("A Build refusal kept across commands", below). The asks with their verdicts and kept check refusals, what the last check read and the decisions go; their comments stay on the ticket and read in later briefs as `owlshift` comments, and questions that waited for answers are abandoned, the next Build run asking what it still needs. A record holding only what forget keeps is left as it is. A record that does not read but points to a commit gets the same on top of it, from what still reads on its own: the round of a `state.json` that parses, the refusals of a `questions.json` that does, round 0 and none otherwise. A symbolic ref, or one that points to anything but a commit, is deleted with `update-ref --no-deref -d` from the object listed, its target untouched. A git command that fails, such as one on an object missing from the store, changes nothing: that is no broken record. It prints what it dropped (the asks and their rounds, the decisions, what the last check read, the state) and kept, and the new commit with its parent, and records a `decision` event (`forgotten`: `rewritten`, `unchanged` or `deleted`; `previous`, `commit`, `round`, and `asks` and `decisions` dropped or why the record did not read, `unreadable`). Moving the ref lifts watch's hold on the ticket (OWL-158).
  - Messages. A write past the cap says that `owlshift forget TICKET` makes room; a round posted but not kept says to run `do` once the ref can be written; the refusal of a ref that does not read names `forget` instead of `git update-ref -d`.
  - Sizes, measured that day on the QUESTIONS comments Owlshift posted on OWL-173 to OWL-179 (1.1 to 1.8 KB a round of three questions): rendered in `questions.json` with its verdicts, an ask of three questions takes about 3.0 KB, a re-ask of two 1.9 KB, a decision 1.2 KB. 1 MiB holds about 350 rounds without a re-ask, 210 with one each, 120 with the three each, or 880 decisions; the tickets run so far took one or two rounds.
  - Live check, that day, git 2.43.0 (`owlshift-testkit/tests/ticket_ref.rs`): `update-ref --no-deref -d` from an old value the ref no longer holds is refused and leaves it, and on a symbolic ref deletes that ref alone.
  - Rejected: compacting settled rounds at the write that would pass the cap, which buys room only past some 120 rounds, changes later briefs (the comments of dropped asks read as `owlshift` comments, the decider in force before them changes when it changed between rounds) and still needs a command once the current round and the decisions do not fit; parking the ticket at a threshold below the cap, whose restart meets the cap again; deleting the ref outright, which drops the round count, so a new round 1 sits beside the old ones, and Build's hold on refused choices.
  - What one Build result may ask (OWL-213, decided 2026-10-11). A result may weigh 1 MiB (`executor::MAX_RESULT_BYTES`), and its questions are copied whole into `questions.json`: one verbose ask could pass the cap alone, which `forget` cannot undo, since Build asks the same again. `RunResult::validate` therefore refuses, as any contract rule (`Failure::InvalidResult`, whose reason the retry is told), a result whose questions weigh more than 8 KiB each (`MAX_QUESTION_BYTES`), 128 KiB together (`MAX_QUESTIONS_BYTES`), or that are more than 64 (`MAX_QUESTIONS`). The weight is the question rendered as JSON, escaping counted: serde_json writes a quote or a backslash on two bytes, `\n`, `\t`, `\r` on two, any other control character on six, and non-ASCII as is. The values come from the sizes above (about 1 KB a question, 1.1 to 1.8 KB a round) and from the largest ask a valid run can owe: a hold keeps 64 refused choices of two 512-byte texts (`MAX_REFUSED_CHOICES`, `MAX_CHOICE_BYTES`, "A Build refusal kept across commands") and its retry must ask every one, one question possibly covering several; 8 KiB holds one maximal choice even escaped at its worst (about 6 KiB), 128 KiB restates all 64 with twice the room they need, and 64 questions are one for each. The count bounds what the bytes do not see, the indentation of a thousand empty questions. The bound is an eighth of the cap: a maximal round kept with its three re-asks (`MAX_REASKS`) renders to 516 KB in the file (`owlshift-testkit/tests/ticket_ref.rs`, control characters throughout), about half of it, so one result cannot fill a record `forget` emptied. Build's refusal is cleared by a round kept, so they never share the file. What is not bounded here: the answer check's verdicts and the resolver's decisions, which accumulate in the file beside the asks and are what `forget` clears. A result that lists decisions and is over the bound is refused for its decisions first. Not bounded either, the other roles' results, which keep no question. The Build role states the three bounds (OWL-215): `roles/build.md` gives the byte counts, and `build_prompt_states_the_question_bounds` (`owlshift-contracts/tests/roles.rs`) fails if they differ from the constants. No format changed: the rule is of the kind the schema cannot express.
  - What Linear accepts of one comment (OWL-223, checked live on 2026-10-11, `commentCreate` through `LINEAR_API_KEY` on disposable OWL issues, trashed after). A body of 100,000 characters is accepted and read back verbatim; 100,001 is refused with HTTP 200, `INVALID_INPUT` "Argument Validation Error" on `body`. The unit is the Unicode code point: 100,000 `é` (200,000 bytes) are accepted and 100,001 refused, while 100,000 four-byte emoji (400,000 bytes, 200,000 UTF-16 units) are accepted. A body of at most 100,000 bytes therefore always fits. The bounds above allow more: 128 KiB of questions and, under them, up to 64 reopened choices of two 512-byte texts (OWL-203), with a summary bounded only by the result's 1 MiB.
  - A round longer than a comment holds is posted in parts (OWL-223, decided 2026-10-11). `writer::MAX_COMMENT_CHARS` is Linear's 100,000, applied to every tracker. A round that fits is posted as before. Otherwise `QuestionsComment::parts` cuts it between questions, never inside one. Each part is a marked QUESTIONS comment of the round, with the same header and footer, and says "Part k of n". The first part holds what precedes the questions, with the summary cut to 8,000 characters (400 in any round since OWL-324). The last part says how to answer and alone mentions the decider. `post_questions` posts the parts in order and returns the last, so the ask the ticket ref keeps is the end of the round. An answer counts once the whole round is on the ticket, and the decider is notified then. A question with every refused choice it may reopen is about 80,000 characters, so it always fits a part; one that did not would be cut with a visible note rather than refused. Checked live the same day by `a_maximal_question_round_reaches_linear_in_parts` (`owlshift-runner/tests/linear_app_live.rs`, through the app, issue OWL-227, trashed after). 100,000 `é` were taken and 100,001 refused with `INVALID_INPUT`. The largest round the bounds allow, 218,928 characters in ASCII, went in 3 parts of 98,308, 99,482 and 9,644 characters, each read back verbatim and marked QUESTIONS. Rejected: lowering the bounds so a round fits one comment. A hold's retry restating its 64 choices, with those choices shown under it, passes 100,000 already, so `MAX_REFUSED_CHOICES` or `MAX_CHOICE_BYTES` would have to shrink.
  - What remains of the split. Only the last part's id is kept, so `on_demand::thread` folds the earlier parts into the round's `questions` entry (OWL-229): the marked QUESTIONS comments of the kept ask's round that precede the kept comment, a "Part n of n", as its parts n-1 down to 1 of the same n (other comments between them are skipped; with the kept comment gone from the tracker, nothing is folded), posted at or before the ask (`writer::part_numbers`); the parts of an abandoned attempt at the same round (a ref write that failed, a part refused) stay comments, as a round not kept does (OWL-234, `answer_check::tests::only_the_kept_attempts_parts_are_folded`). Later briefs, the answer check's included, see the round once; a later comment, or one of another round, that only copies the marker stays a comment (`answer_check::tests::a_round_posted_in_parts_is_never_an_answer`). A part the tracker refuses stops the round; the error says how many parts reached the ticket, and they stay there as a round not kept does, the next `do` posting the whole round again. The `TrackerWrite` event names the last part only. RE-ASK, REPLY, PARKED and DELIVERY comments are cut to hold in one comment instead (OWL-228, below).
  - The other comments are cut to hold in one (OWL-228, decided 2026-10-12). RE-ASK restates up to 128 KiB of questions with the answer check's reasons, PARKED at the re-ask limit lists them again, REPLY carries the check's replies, and DELIVERY a summary bounded only by the result's 1 MiB, the decisions of every run, the forge's checks and the follow-ups: each could pass 100,000 characters and be refused, leaving the decider without the runner's comment. Each now holds in `MAX_COMMENT_CHARS`, the decider's "Waiting for" line included (`render_within`, `Writer::post_waiting`). A comment that fits is posted exactly as before. Otherwise `writer::fitted` cuts the model's and the forge's texts, never the frame (header, footer, question ids, each verdict's class, the lead and pull request lines): first each text shown elsewhere to its cap, then every text longer than a common length to that length, the longest that holds, so short texts stay whole and every text keeps its start, each cut ending with a note that says why. The caps: in RE-ASK, REPLY and PARKED each text of a question to 1,000 characters (its text, its options and its recommendation since OWL-324, which cuts its context at 1,000 and an option's detail at 300 everywhere and shows its category nowhere), since the round's QUESTIONS comment shows it whole and the ticket ref keeps it for the brief and the answer check; PARKED's detail to 8,000; DELIVERY's summary to 8,000 (the pull request's body has it), its checks to 10,000 (the pull request lists them), its gate to 2,000 and its decisions to 20,000 (each resolver decision is a DECISION comment); what remains for a human, with the follow-ups no other place lists, gives way last, and its lists of failing or pending checks keep their first 2,000 characters' worth of names and say how many more. Bounded, not split: a RE-ASK in parts would need its parts folded into its ask in `on_demand::thread`, and a DELIVERY in parts would break its post-once rule, which finds a report by its lead and pull request lines in one comment (OWL-218, OWL-231). Readers are unchanged: a cut comment keeps its marker, so it is Owlshift's, never an answer, and the RE-ASK the ask keeps still takes its comment's place in the thread (`answer_check::tests::a_comment_cut_to_hold_is_never_an_answer`). The lead line's lists, a fix pass's red checks and a correction's questions, keep their first items up to 10,000 characters and say how many more (`listed`), whatever else the report holds, so one push always gives the same lead. Accepted: two correction lists past 10,000 characters that share those first items and the count of the rest read as one report; a report posted before this change with a longer lead is posted once more. A first report whose summary starts as a lead line does and was cut is still found by the summary's kept start. Not bounded: the frame's identifiers (the pull request's URL from GitHub, the configured base branch, the decider's Linear mention), far below the limit. Tested on maximal inputs in `writer::tests` (`a_maximal_reask_or_reply_holds_in_one_comment`, `a_maximal_parked_comment_holds_in_one_comment`, `a_maximal_delivery_report_holds_in_one_comment_and_is_posted_once`, `texts_are_fitted_to_the_room_longest_first`). RESUME and DECISION hold in it too (OWL-237, decided 2026-10-12), the same way. RESUME restates a round with every answer check reason: the questions give way first, as in RE-ASK (1,000 characters), then the longest texts. No other comment shows a decided question whole, so DECISION caps each text of its question at 5,000 characters, and leaves the decision and its basis uncapped: they are what the decider reverses, so they are cut last, as the longest texts only. Every question of a RESUME keeps its id and its `Understood:` line; readers find both by marker, unchanged. Tested in `a_maximal_resume_comment_holds_in_one_comment` and `a_maximal_decision_comment_holds_in_one_comment`.
  - What remains. Under watch, a write past the cap after an answer check is a refusal, retried every 10 minutes at one answer check each until a person runs `forget` (a not-kept stop is held instead, OWL-158). A round posted but not kept stays on the ticket beside the one the next `do` posts, as any round not kept does. Decisions dropped by `forget` are no longer listed in later delivery reports. The write error reaches the RESUME, RE-ASK and REPLY paths too; the test bench runs the round's (`owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs`: a ref padded 300 bytes short of the cap, since rounds of real size would take hundreds, stops a round with `owlshift forget` named, then the round after it is asked and kept), the others are covered by `ticket_ref`'s tests alone.
- **`owlshift do` asks on the ticket** (OWL-122). A Build `questions` result, or `premise_false` with questions, opens a round: the Writer posts a `[owlshift] QUESTIONS · round N` comment (`writer::QuestionsComment`, every question, model text flattened, after the floor), then the ticket ref keeps the ask and the state, and `do` prints the questions and says to run `owlshift resume TICKET`. A `premise_false` with no question is printed, not posted or kept: there is nothing to classify (S8 is P5's). A post that failed is printed the same way; a post whose ref write then failed names the comment, and a new `do` asks again. While the questions wait, parked or not, `do` is refused and points to `resume`; otherwise it starts from Ready as in P1, keeping the round count and the asks, and makes no ticket ref until a round opens (three exceptions came later: a kept decision, OWL-138, a refused Build result, OWL-192, and a refused resolver result, OWL-191).
- **The QUESTIONS layout** (OWL-324, decided 2026-10-16). Seen live on OWL-256, OWL-257, OWL-277, OWL-280 and OWL-285 (2026-10-13 and 14): options joined by ` / ` on one line, no stable labels, a recommendation missing or written as free text, a context restating the ticket, a raw profile link, the category on every question, and every field flattened to one line. The decider could not see the ask, the options or the pick at a glance, nor answer in two characters.
  - *The contract.* `result.json` format 10: a question's `options` are `{label, detail}`, `detail` optional, which the runner letters A, B, C… in order, then AA, AB… past Z (`result::option_letter`). Its `recommendation` is required: `{option, why}` with options, the option named by its capital letter, and `{why}` alone, the proposed answer, without. `RunResult::validate` refuses a question without one, one naming no option when it offers some, naming one it does not offer (a lowercase letter included) or naming one when it offers none, and a blank `why` or label; each reason names the question and what to write, for the retry. The schema requires the object. The brief (format 16) and `questions.json` (format 12) carry the same shape. `questions.json` formats 2 to 11 are still read: each string option becomes `{label}`, and each string recommendation `{why}` naming no option, since a free text cannot be told to mean one; the type keeps `recommendation` optional for those asks only.
  - *The block.* Every comment that shows a question shows it through `writer::question_block`: `### Qn · text`, the context on the next line, the options one per bullet as `- **A.** label — detail`, `⭐` before the recommended one, then `**Recommendation: B.** why`, or `**Recommendation:** why` without options. RE-ASK, PARKED, REPLY and RESUME add their own line after it (`**Still open** (class): …`, `**Reply:** …`, `**Understood:** …`); DECISION shows it as `### Question · text`, before its decision. The category routes a question and is shown nowhere. A round ends with a rule and ``Reply `Qn: X`, `ok` to take every recommendation, or in your own words.``, Qn and X being the first question with options and the letter it recommends; the line offers `ok` alone when no question has options. `owlshift do` prints the same block. No option is added by the runner: the answer line invites any answer.
  - *Escaping.* Model text is flattened to one line, as before. The context and the run's summary, the two texts that open a line, get a backslash before a first character that could open a Markdown block (`#`, `>`, `-`, `+`, `*`, `=`, `_`, `|`, `<`, a backtick, `~`, `[`, or the `.` or `)` after leading digits), and a heading's trailing `#`s are escaped. Inline Markdown is left as before: it stays inside the block its text sits in, and the marker and the footer are read by line.
  - *Sizes.* In every comment, a question's context shows at most 300 characters (1,000 until OWL-352) and an option's detail 300, cut visibly; a QUESTIONS comment shows at most 400 characters of the run's summary. The contract stays uncapped: a wordy question is cut, not refused, since a refusal costs a failed run and OWL-213's retry of refused choices needs the room. The split in parts (OWL-223) and the fitting of the other comments (OWL-228, OWL-237) are unchanged; a question's texts are four (its text, context, options and recommendation), so the frame no longer grows with its options.
  - *The mention.* The decider's mention moves from a `Waiting for <mention>` line to the end of the header line, `[owlshift] QUESTIONS · round N · <mention>`, on QUESTIONS, RE-ASK and REPLY. `Header::parse` passes over a ` · <mention>` after the kind and round of those three kinds only; the footer's format is unchanged. Check C4 saw the profile link make a mention inline after text on a line of its own; at the end of the header line, `linear_app_live.rs` checks it, run by hand. Checked live on 2026-10-17 (2 passed, 0 failed, through the app, test issues OWL-325 and OWL-326, trashed after): the QUESTIONS comment's header line ended with the decider's profile link, and the decider got an `issueCommentMention` from the app user on that comment; the largest round, 194,131 characters in this layout, went in 2 parts of 97,425 and 97,015 characters, each read back verbatim and marked QUESTIONS. Accepted: an older binary reading the new header takes it for a malformed marker, a downgrade only.
  - *The roles.* The authoring roles write a `text` on one line, a `context` of at most two sentences that does not restate the ticket, short option labels with a one-line detail at most, always a recommendation, and, with `questions`, a one-sentence `summary` of what the run did. The answer check reads `Q1: B` (any case), a bare letter when the latest ask has one question, `ok` as taking every recommendation and `Q2: ok` as taking one; a bare letter on an ask of several questions is `unanswered`. Since OWL-352 it also reads a letter followed by the decider's own words, such as `B, fork 1 option C`, and plain words that settle the question; `fork n option X` names the plan's nth fork and its option by letter, and one the plan does not have is `partial`. The resolver changes a plan by option B with the change in its decision, or by a fork's option when the question offers one; the design role reads `fork n option X` as the check does. That reading is the model's: the bench (`a_two_character_answer_reaches_the_check_and_resumes`) shows each of the three replies reaching the check beside its lettered questions and the round resuming, under a scripted verdict.
  - *The runner's own questions* recommend too: the plan-approval question its approval, a residue finding settling it, and a late comment's round revising the work, each option A.
  - *The runner's rounds at a glance* (OWL-352, decided 2026-10-19). Seen on the 2026-10-18 drain: OWL-160's approval question had 26 options, one per fork alternative as "Fork n: X instead of Y", OWL-340's 12, and OWL-336's residue round 8,099 characters for 4 questions, each context up to 1,000 characters, beneath a PLAN comment of 65,000. On a tracker with documents (a published plan, OWL-350), the details are one link away:
    - The approval question has two options, **A.** "Approve the plan as it is", recommended, and **B.** "Change it", whose detail invites an answer in the decider's own words, such as `B, fork 1 option C`. Its context says the forks are in the plan's document, each fork's options lettered A, B, C… in order; the document letters them so (a nested bullet per option), the PLAN comment does not. Under the question, the QUESTIONS comment alone (`QuestionsComment::notes`) lists one line per fork, its choice and a link to the document: to the whole document, since Linear's API gives a document's headings no anchor, and none was found that a link can target. Checked live on 2026-10-19 (OWL-364, read only): through the key, which reads what the app writes ("Owlshift's identity on Linear" below), on the workspace's two documents, both plan documents made by the app (`creator.app` true; 9 and 8 headings), so no app write was repeated. The `Document` type has no field for a heading or an anchor, and its `url`, `https://linear.app/owlshift/document/<slug>`, no fragment; the Markdown `content` gives plain `#` headings; a string scan of the decoded collaborative `contentState` found no id beside any `heading` node, only its `attribution`. Not checked: whether Linear's web editor gives a heading an id or a link of its own once a person opens the document, since this check had no browser. Even so, such a link would be undocumented, and one derived from a heading's text would change whenever the design role renames a fork, so a link built on it could break without a sign. Settled: the fork lines keep linking the whole document. The ticket ref keeps the question without these lines, so a RE-ASK, a RESUME and `owlshift do`'s print show the two options alone, and the resolver and the answer check read the forks in the brief's `plan`.
    - A residue question's context is `Finding F2 · blocker · critic · confirmed by the arbiter · review log. Evidence: …`, the review log a link to the document, the evidence clipped to what the 300 characters leave. The document's review log shows each finding left whole: its claim, evidence, settlement, criterion and ruling. At Verify, whose passes the document does not log, and without documents, the context is the same without the link.
    - A round of the runner's stays under `writer::ROUND_BUDGET_CHARS`, 3,000 characters, for up to 4 questions: `design.rs` fits each residue question to an equal share of the budget, less `ROUND_FRAME_CHARS` held for the header, the mention, the summary, the resolver's lines and the footer (a test pins that reserve against the longest of them), clipping the claim, the evidence and the settlement over its caps down to a floor; the fork lines are fitted to what the question leaves, their choices clipped, and past that become one line linking the document. A larger round passes the budget at the floor, and the split in parts stays the last fallback. Build's own rounds have no document to move details to: they get the 300-character context alone.
    - Without documents, the approval question keeps its per-fork options.
    - Accepted: a document made again after a person deleted it leaves the links of earlier questions pointing to the deleted one, as the PLAN comments' do.
  - Rejected: a question type of `result.json`'s own, which would need a second copy in the brief and the ticket ref and a conversion between them; and caps in the contract (above).
  - Tests: `writer::tests::the_owl_257_question_renders_as_the_target` (the ticket's target, exactly), `model_text_cannot_open_a_block_in_any_question_comment` (every comment, every marker), the maximal tests; `contracts::questions_recommend_an_option_they_offer`, the format-11 and format-2 upgrades, `comment_headers`; `roles::answer_check_prompt_states_the_short_answers`. OWL-352: `design::tests::the_owl_160_approval_round_fits_at_a_glance` (2 options, 3 fork lines, under 1,500 characters), `a_residue_round_of_four_holds_in_the_budget`, `the_round_frame_reserve_holds_the_longest_frame`, `roles::answer_check_prompt_takes_a_choice_in_own_words`.
- **A thumbs-up answers a one-question round** (OWL-371, decided 2026-10-19; architecture section 4, "Answer check"; the reaction checks of OWL-354 and OWL-371 under C4 above). Most rounds were answered with a bare `ok`. A thumbs-up is one click in the issue's thread, where Linear's agent-session choices are not (OWL-354).
  - *The rule* (`answer_check::thumbs_up`). A reaction answers when it is `+1`, Linear's name for the thumbs-up, on the latest ask's own comment, by that ask's recorded decider, when that ask holds one question carrying a recommendation (`answer_check::answerable_by_thumbs_up`). The ask's comment is the round's QUESTIONS comment, or its last part when it is in parts, which holds the question and the answer line; or the RE-ASK, which counts though it has no answer line to say so. The decider is matched by account identifier, as their comments are. Any other emoji is no answer and asks nothing again, a skin-toned thumbs-up included if Linear names it otherwise. An app user's reaction (Owlshift's own or another integration's), a bot's and an external user's are never an account's (`tracker::Reaction::author`, the rule of comments), so they never answer. Neither does a thumbs-up on any other comment, an earlier round's or an earlier part's included, nor one on an ask of several questions, where a thumbs-up meaning "seen" would take every recommendation at once.
  - *When* (`answer_check::answer`). The decider's new answer is their typed comment when one was last edited after `answers_after` (the latest ask, or what the last check read), else their thumbs-up when it came after that. Words win: the check reads the words, and its `checked_through` is their newest edit, so a thumbs-up newer than every word it read stays an answer of its own. A thumbs-up waits through the quiet window from its own time, as a typed `ok` does, and it cannot end with `go`; the `Stop::Settling` it gives says so. Removed within the window, it is gone from the next read and answers nothing (the live check above); put back, its new time starts the window again. Watch reads it in the comments it reads each pass (`readiness` is shared), and its time is the hold's answer, so a thumbs-up lifts a hold as a new comment does.
  - *Resuming.* No answer check runs, since a thumbs-up has one reading. `OnDemand::check_answers` keeps the question `answered` with the reason "The decider's thumbs-up on the question: its recommendation, B, No." (the recommendation's `why` when the question has no options), sets `checked_through` to the thumbs-up's time, and posts the RESUME as after an answered check. The `gate` event says `answer` = `reaction` and names no run. Later runs read the round as its `answered` entry, which the roles read as the decider's answer. A plan approval kept after a thumbs-up names the approval round's QUESTIONS comment (`answer_check::answering_comment`: the ask's comment when the verdicts the ticket ref keeps on it are the thumbs-up's, `answer_check::resumed_by_thumbs_up`, so a reaction removed after the resume or a design run retried changes nothing; else the decider's newest comment, as before).
  - *Told.* A tracker that reads reactions declares `Capability::Reactions`: Linear does, the Markdown tracker does not, its file format having no reactions. On such a tracker, a QUESTIONS round of one question ends its answer line with "A thumbs-up on this comment counts as `ok`." (`Writer::post_questions`). It is written in words, since Owlshift writes no emoji (visual identity).
  - *Read.* Linear's comments query asks each comment's `reactions { emoji createdAt user { id displayName app } externalUser { name } }`: no request more, 3 complexity points more a page. `updatedAt`, which a reaction changes, is read nowhere.
  - Not built: reactions in the stand-in driver's TOML scenarios, which never react, and in the Markdown tracker's files; reactions on a REPLY, a PARKED or any other comment.
  - Rejected: the answer check run on the thumbs-up, which needs a brief format and a role change to classify what can only mean `ok`, and a model run per click; a synthetic `ok` comment, which would misstate the thread; a request of its own for the ask's comment's reactions, one more request per waiting ticket per pass against Linear's 2,500 an hour.
  - Tests: `answer_check::tests` (`a_thumbs_up_answers_only_as_the_rule_says`, `a_thumbs_up_settles_through_the_quiet_window`, `a_thumbs_up_takes_the_recommendation`, `the_approval_names_the_comment_that_answered`), `linear::tests::reactions_are_read_with_their_authors`, `writer::tests::a_question_asked_alone_says_a_thumbs_up_counts`; on the bench, `a_thumbs_up_of_the_decider_answers_a_question_asked_alone`, `words_win_over_a_thumbs_up` and `a_plan_approved_by_a_thumbs_up_reaches_build`.
- **`owlshift resume TICKET`** (OWL-122). The same checks as `do` before any run, and refused with nothing to resume when there is no ticket ref, or no dedicated checkout (nothing is cloned to find out). A parked ticket is restarted (`Event::Restarted`, recorded as a `decision` event): typing `resume` is the person's gesture. While questions wait, no new answer stops with what it waits for; otherwise the answer check runs on Claude Code with the built-in `roles/answer_check.md`, read-only, no network, a 15-minute deadline and no project rule in its brief (section 9 of the architecture: a small context). Its outcome is kept in the ticket ref and recorded as a `gate` event with each question's class: answered, and the Writer posts the RESUME, then the same `resume` runs Build from the checkpoint, its brief's thread holding the asks and the answers, to a delivery as `do` does; incomplete, and the Writer posts the RE-ASK; a counter-question, and the Writer posts the REPLY and it says what was asked back and where the reply is; a failed check, and it says to run `resume` again; a park, and the Writer posts the PARKED comment and it says why. A ticket at Build runs Build; one past it is refused, pointing to `do`. Exit status 0 for a delivery only. `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` drives both commands on the Markdown tracker, the fake harness and a tracker clock of its own (each comment of the bench takes the next minute, so its times read plainly): a round asked by `do`, `do` refused and `resume` waiting, an incomplete answer re-asked with Q2 alone, a counter-question whose REPLY the tracker first refuses, keeping nothing, then replied to and a wait, the REPLY an `owlshift` entry of the next briefs, then a complete answer resumed to a delivery; and a failed check retried, a quarantined one parking the ticket, and a restart that checks the same answers and delivers.
- **Who answers which ask** (OWL-124, decided 2026-10-02), following architecture section 4's "Who decides", with the zones a ticket declares by its `zone:` labels until intake ([runtime and operations](runtime-and-operations.md#configuration), "Zones a ticket declares").
  - Resolved once per command: `on_demand::pending_decider` and `decide` read the ticket's assignee, or for a ticket without one its `zone:` labels and the owners of `owlshift.toml` at the base commit (`project::zone_owners_at`, read with the plumbing of the project's rules), and ask `owlshift_core::decider::decider`. "Resolved when a question is asked" is read as when the command that asks starts: a round's questions record the decider of the `do` or `continue` whose Build asked them; a re-ask copies its round's, so a round has one decider throughout.
  - Recorded: each ask of `questions.json` carries `decider`, the account identifier and the rule (`assignee` or `zone_owner`), which is what format 2 of `questions.json` adds. A ticket ref written in format 1 (by OWL-122, from that morning) is refused like any other format; the refusal names the ref, and deleting it restarts the ticket's rounds from 1, its comments staying on the ticket.
  - Matched: a comment is the decider's when its author is the decider recorded on the latest ask before it, or before any ask the current decider; the ticket's author is judged against the current decider. So the answers a check accepted stay instructions for the Build they unblock, and a person who became the assignee after an ask cannot answer it. The answer check runs on a comment of the latest ask's decider (`answer_check::new_answer`), and `continue` and `do` name that decider while questions wait. A brief's `decider` is the one in force at the end of its thread.
  - Required: `do` refuses a ticket with no decider, with the core's reason and how to declare zones; `continue` only when Build is about to run, since waiting, the answer check and a re-ask need the recorded decider alone.
  - In the test bench: the stand-in driver records the ticket's assignee as the decider of each round it asks, and a re-ask copies it, as `continue` does; it resolves no zone owner, since its scenarios give every ticket an assignee.
  - In the brief (OWL-129, decided 2026-10-02): `zones` lists the folders of the ticket's well-formed `zone:` labels (`owlshift_core::decider::brief_zones`), for a ticket with an assignee as for one without, since the field tells the agent where the ticket works and not who decides it. A malformed `zone:` label is left out of the list: it refuses only a ticket without an assignee, whose decider it would change. Since OWL-134 `brief_zones` also returns the labels it left out, with the reason (`BriefZones::left_out`; the core does no I/O), and the runner emits one `warning` event per label and per run, after `run_started`, with `what` = `zone_label_left_out`, `role`, `label` and `reason`; the event kind is new in the event contract and its schema. The test bench's driver fills the field the same way and records no warning. Path-scoped rules still wait for intake.
  - Live check of Linear's label name (OWL-129). Checked on 2026-10-02, by GraphQL on the Owlshift workspace, team `OWL`: `issueLabelCreate` accepted the name `zone:backend/billing` with no error and did not rewrite it; the label was set on OWL-129, and `issue(id: "OWL-129") { labels { nodes { name } } }` returned `zone:backend/billing` as written. So the convention `zone:<folder>` holds on Linear and needs no adjustment. Read back through the Owlshift Linear adapter: pending live check. `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/linear_live.rs` (`linear_returns_a_zone_label_as_written`, ignored, read only) reads OWL-129 with `LinearTracker` and asserts the label name, `declared_zones` and `brief_zones`; the maintainer runs it with `OWLSHIFT_LIVE_LINEAR_ZONE_TICKET=OWL-129` and the key in `LINEAR_API_KEY` or `LINEAR_ENV_FILE`, and its dated result replaces this sentence.
- **The visible stage** (OWL-137, decided 2026-10-03). The runner moves a ticket's stage on the tracker through the Writer (`writer::VisibleStage`, `Writer::set_stage`, the floor's `SetVisibleStage`), to the state the project maps under `[tracker].states`: `working` once `do` has dispatched or an answer resumed Build (`continue`, once the RESUME is kept); `needs_input` once a round's QUESTIONS or a RE-ASK is posted, before the ticket ref keeps it, so a posted question always shows, and again on a re-ask in case a person moved the ticket meanwhile; `review` once the pull request is open, before its head and checks are read. `VisibleStage::after` names the core event behind each move, and the stand-in driver moves the stage after the same events through the same Writer. A counter-question keeps the ticket waiting. A park moves the stage to `parked` once the PARKED comment is posted, or tried, also when keeping the parked state failed (OWL-148, decided 2026-10-04, architecture section 4): the state the optional `[tracker].states.parked` names, else the `needs_input` one; `continue` restarting a parked ticket moves it, once the restart is kept, to `VisibleStage::of` the status it lands on, `needs_input` or `working`. A move is a `tracker_write` event (`action` = `stage`, `stage`, `state`); a failed one is a `warning` (`what` = `stage_not_moved`, `stage`, `state`, `reason`) and never stops the command or the delivery. The Markdown tracker rewrites the `stage` line. The Linear adapter reads the workflow states of the issue's team (50 at most, refusing more), takes the one named exactly as configured, refusing none or several, and calls `issueUpdate` with its id, checking that the issue answers in that state (C4 above, "Moving the visible stage"). The conformance suite moves the stage on both adapters (`check_stage`); `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` follows a round, a re-ask, the resume and the delivery, and a tracker refusing every move; a park at the re-ask limit shown as the project's parked state until its restart, one at Build shown as needs input until `continue` shows it working, and a refused park move.
- **Owlshift's identity on Linear** (OWL-157, decided 2026-10-04; decision D8, C4 above). Optional: with the Linear app's client ID and secret in the keychain (accounts `linear-app-client-id` and `linear-app-client-secret`, per project since OWL-308, part K of "P5 · Quality pipeline"), `do`, `continue` and `watch` request one token per command when they open the tracker, before any work (`client_credentials`, scopes `read,write`, fixed since asking another set revokes every token of the app). They check it with one request through each credential: an app user, of the key's workspace, that sees the project's team (an app reaches public teams only, and another workspace's app would write to its own issue of the same identifier). Every write, comments and stage moves, then goes with that token as a `Bearer` (`linear::app::AppTransport`); reads, mentions included, stay on the API key, which stays required. A 401 gets one new token and one more try, a transport error none; the token is revoked when the command ends, and `watch` keeps one for its life. An app that cannot be used refuses the command, with no fallback to the key, which would hide that Linear no longer tells the decider. Without an app, Owlshift writes through the key as before, and `owlshift doctor` warns; with one, doctor runs the same check, then revokes its token. The adapter reads `user.app`: an app user, Owlshift's own or another integration's, is never an account, so never the decider, and Owlshift's own comments read as Owlshift's by their marker and the ticket ref, as before. QUESTIONS, RE-ASK and REPLY carry the decider's mention (`Writer::mentioning`, `Tracker::mention`), on a `Waiting for <mention>` line after their header until OWL-324 moved it to the end of the header line ("The QUESTIONS layout" above): on Linear, the profile link of the decider recorded on the ask (`User.url`, read at each post, left out when it cannot be read, which is reported as a `warning`, `what` `mention_dropped`, since OWL-170), which Linear makes a mention; REPLY joins the ticket's "question and re-ask" since it too waits for the decider's answer. Every other comment mentions no one, and still reaches the issue's subscribers (scenario S6). Issue creation (S13) is not built; C4 above tried it. Tests: the adapter's routing, checks, mention and token lifecycle on fakes; the Writer's mention; a bench round whose QUESTIONS, RE-ASK and REPLY mention the recorded decider though the ticket was reassigned meanwhile; `init`'s pair; doctor's `tracker identity` line.
- **Who runs it.** `owlshift continue`, and in the scenarios the test bench's stand-in driver, which keeps its asks, their verdicts, the resolver's decisions and Build's refusal in memory as the ticket ref's `TicketQuestions` would (OWL-126: `TicketQuestions::keep_check` and `round_asks`, `answer_check::readiness` with no quiet window, `on_demand::thread` and `account_author`, so the bench tests the runner's own loop and not a copy), runs the role and posts the re-ask, the REPLY, the RESUME and the PARKED comment with the same pieces, and moves the visible stage as `continue` does (below). `tests/scenarios/reask.toml` plays two question rounds: an incomplete answer re-asked with the open question alone, a counter-question answered by a REPLY with no Build run, a check refused for a verdict on another ask, and a RESUME after each round's last answer, round 1's restating Q1 from the first check. `crates/owlshift-testkit/tests/scenarios.rs` parks a ticket at the re-ask limit and after a second failed run, each with its PARKED comment and its stage: the parked state the re-ask project names, needs input for the other. It also checks that a counter-question a check read is not an answer again, and that a decision and an ask take their comments' places in the thread, a decision before the round it leaves.
- **`owlshift continue TICKET` and the quiet window** (OWL-127, decided 2026-10-03). `owlshift resume TICKET` (OWL-122, the entry above) is renamed `owlshift continue TICKET`, with no alias: `resume` names only the background service's dispatch command beside `pause` (P7, [runtime and operations](runtime-and-operations.md#observability--the-local-web-ui)), so each name means one action. The two OWL-122 entries above ("`owlshift do` asks on the ticket" and "`owlshift resume TICKET`") keep the name they were written with; read `continue` there. Comments already posted on tickets keep the old name; the dispatch event of the renamed command records `command` = `continue`, free-form `data` that no reader parses, so the event format stays 2. Once answers arrive, `continue` checks them only when the decider's reply counts ([architecture](architecture.md), section 4, "No premature resume"): `answer_check::counts_at` takes the decider's comment with the newest last edit (on a tie, the one created last, then the last the tracker lists) and asks `owlshift_core::reply::counts` whether it was left unedited for the quiet window (`policy.quiet_window_minutes`, default `DEFAULT_QUIET_WINDOW`, 10 minutes) or ends with `go` (`reply::ends_with_go`: its last word, punctuation trimmed, in any ASCII case). The time it was left unedited is this machine's clock (`OnDemand::clock`) less the tracker's last edit, the one place a tracker time meets this machine's; a last edit after the clock counts as none, so the whole window applies from that edit. Too early, `continue` stops with `Stop::Settling`, which says when the reply counts and that `go` makes it count now, and keeps nothing of the check; a parked ticket is restarted first, and that restart is kept, as before. The window is the project's `policy.quiet_window_minutes` (OWL-145, decided 2026-10-03): whole minutes from 1 to 1440, 10 when absent, refused at config load outside the range; 0 is refused because every edit would count at once, which `go` is for, and a day is the longest a ticket is worth holding for an answer to settle. `counts_at` takes the window, `Stop::Settling` carries it for its message, and `watch` passes the same value (OWL-152, below). `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` stops a `continue` typed within the window, with no run and the ticket ref unchanged, checks the same answer at the time it counts, delivers on an answer ending with `go`, and holds a parked ticket's restart through the window.
- **Late comments before delivery** (OWL-139, decided 2026-10-04), architecture section 4's "No premature resume" and scenario S2 step 5. In `OnDemand::build`, for a ticket whose ticket ref keeps an ask, a Build run that is done with its gate passed is not delivered yet: the runner reads the comments again and keeps the late ones (`answer_check::late_comments`: the latest ask's decider's, not marked, last edit newer than the newest decider edit the run's brief held, which a comment of the same time misses, as `new_answer` does). None: delivery as before. Some: a `gate` event (`late_comments`, the comment ids, `outcome` = `integrate`) and one more Build run, from the same state at Build and with the comments read again for its brief; a decision `Late comment` with the comments' times as its basis joins the delivery report; the build role integrates a decider comment newer than the latest RESUME comment, even with every step done, or asks about it (`roles/build.md`, "Resuming"). That run's questions go to the decider without the resolver, as round N+1, its summary prefixed with the late comments' times; a failed run, a usage limit or a breach goes as for any Build run. When it is done, a further late comment (`outcome` = `ask`) opens round N+1 with one `scope` question built by the runner (`on_demand::late_round`), which names the comments' times and asks whether the work takes them into account before delivery; nothing is pushed. The bound counts per command: a `continue` after a usage limit integrates again. A comment the tracker refuses to list stops with `Stop::Delivery`, the ticket left at Build. `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` integrates a late comment into a second Build run and its pull request, a comment by someone else during that run counting for nothing, stops in needs-input when the integrating run asks, and stops after one integration run.
- **`owlshift watch`** (OWL-152, decided 2026-10-04): the decisions are architecture section 5's "Watch mode". `owlshift_runner::watch` holds the loop and its pure bookkeeping (`Holds`: the 10-minute hold of a continue that left the ticket ref as it was, the pause until a usage limit's reset); `ticket_ref::list` lists the ticket refs; `answer_check::readiness` decides, for watch and for `continue`'s answer check alike, whether the decider's reply counts; `OnDemand::continue_seen` is `continue` run only while the ticket ref, read under the lock, is still the commit watch saw. Its `decision` event carries `watch` = `continue` and the decider's newest edit (`answer`), and an unreadable ticket is a `warning` with `what` = `watch_unreadable`: free-form data, so the event format stays 2. `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` plays the acceptance: a round asked by `do`, then passes that wait, read the comments through a tracker failure, hold a reply within a project's 20-minute window, skip a locked and then a quarantined project, and continue the ticket once its reply counts, to a delivery, with no `continue` typed.
- **Watch after delivery** (OWL-208, decided 2026-10-10): the decisions are those of architecture section 5, "Watch after delivery". Below, who builds what, so that no implementation ticket decides them again. Each pending live check is the first step of its ticket, run before the code that depends on it (AGENTS.md).
  - **OWL-209: following the pull requests.**
    - *`delivery.json`.* A new contract, `TicketDelivery`, with its schema in `schemas/`, kept in the ticket ref beside `state.json`. A ref the delivery makes holds a `questions.json` with no ask. Fields:
      - the pull request's number and link, the branch and the base branch;
      - `pushed`, `base_at_push`, `rebased_onto` and `pushing` (commit and kind: `delivery`, `fix` or `rebase`);
      - `ended`: why (`merged`, `closed` or `head_moved`) and when.
    - *Delivery.* Writes `pushing` before the push and `pushed` once the head is read back, and moves the core state to Watch: Completed at Build, then at Verify, which has no role before P5, then at Deliver.
    - *The pass over followed pull requests.* Watch reads them and adopts a `pushing` head. It ends the following of a pull request merged, closed or pushed to by someone else, and reads the ticket's stage before any run. `do` refuses a ticket whose pull request is followed, and `continue` at Watch does watch's step once.
    - *The decision table* of section 5, a pure function in `owlshift-core` over plain data: the read, the base's checks, the counts and the times. It returns wait, fix, rebase (trivial or not), park (with the reason) or end (with the reason). OWL-112 and OWL-210 run its fix and rebase.
    - *Forge adapter additions.* `HeadMoved` carries the head it read. A query for the checks of a commit gives the base's head and `base_at_push`. `rateLimit { cost remaining resetAt }` is added to each query watch polls; delivery's one-shot queries keep their recorded text (see "Implemented", below). A rate-limited answer is its own error kind, which holds the forge reads until its reset.
    - *Events.* Each change watch reads (verdict, mergeability, leaving the list) and each action it takes is a `decision` event whose `watch` names it: free-form data, so the event format stays 2.
    - *Live checks.* (a) The checks query's GraphQL cost, recorded as a fixture from a real call. (b) A real conflicting pull request read as `CONFLICTING`, recorded as a fixture. Neither could be run from the cloud session that designed this, whose GraphQL calls GitHub refuses; both were run on 2026-10-12 by OWL-217 (below).
    - *Scope moved on 2026-10-11, by the maintainer.* Reading the ticket's stage before a run moves to OWL-112 and OWL-210, since OWL-209 starts no run. OWL-209 decides fix, rebase and park, and records each as a `decision` event; it applies no `FixDue`, `RebaseDue` or `ForgeBlocked` and posts no PARKED comment, which OWL-112 and OWL-210 do. The one state change OWL-209 applies is `ChecksGreen`, when it changes the state. Whether a rebase is trivial is OWL-210's, from `git merge-tree`.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-209, 2026-10-11).
      - `owlshift_contracts::refs::TicketDelivery`, `schemas/ticket-delivery.schema.json`: format 1. `pull_request` is optional, since a delivery's `pushing` is written before its pull request exists. `pushing` keeps `base` beside the commit and kind (architecture section 5, "Owlshift's own pushes"). `branch` and `base_branch` follow git's branch-name rules.
      - `ticket_ref` reads and writes `delivery.json` beside the two other files, and every write carries it forward. `forget` drops it.
      - `owlshift_core::follow::decide` is the table. The runner's `follow` module maps the forge's read onto it (`read_of`), keeps watch's follow memory (`Following`: the forge's pause, the last state recorded per pull request, the first-failure times), and runs the step that watch and `continue` share.
      - The adapter: `GitHubForge::follow` (the pull request's read, without an expected head) and `GitHubForge::base_checks` (the base head's checks and `base_at_push`'s, paged per commit) carry `rateLimit`. Delivery's queries keep their live-recorded text, so their cost is not reported. `ErrorKind::RateLimited` comes with its reset (`Error::resets_at`): a GraphQL `RATE_LIMITED` error, or HTTP 403 or 429 with `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` or a `Retry-After`. The GraphQL `Response` keeps those rate-limit headers only, never any other, so a recording still holds no credential. `HeadMoved` carries the head read (`Error::head`).
      - Events: one `decision` per followed pull request when what watch reads changes. `watch` is `read` for the first look in a watch's life, else the first change among `ended`, `adopted`, `mergeable`, `verdict` and `action`; beside it are `pull_request`, `head`, `verdict`, `mergeable`, `action`, `base` when the base was read, and `reason` once ended. Warnings are `forge_rate_limited` (once per hold), `watch_forge_unreadable` (once per kind of failure, until a read succeeds) and `checks_overdue` (once per head). The delivery adds `base_head_unread` when it cannot read the base head before its push. A look whose ref change finds the project busy, or the ref moved, writes and records nothing, and the next pass looks again.
      - `owlshift continue` at Watch returns `Stop::Watched`, printed with exit status 0 and no notification.
      - Tests: the table row by row in core; the follow memory in the runner; the adapter on fixtures; on the bench, a pull request going red, then conflicting, then merged (one event each, no read after the merge), `do` refused, and `continue` at Watch.
    - *Checked live on 2026-10-12 (OWL-217).* OWL-209's cloud session could not run checks (a) and (b) either (GraphQL answered HTTP 403, "GitHub GraphQL is not available from Claude Code sessions"), so its fixtures of watch's queries were made up; they now replay real answers, recorded from the maintainer's machine.
      - (a) Every query watch polls cost 1 point: the pull request's read red, conflicting and merged, and each base-checks query. The base-checks fixture holds six queries, 6 points, only because it is recorded one check per page to keep the paging covered (3 checks at the base head, 4 at `base_at_push`). At the adapter's page size of 100 the base read is one query, 1 point. Architecture section 5, "Cost", holds.
      - (b) A pull request whose base took a conflicting commit read `CONFLICTING` and `DIRTY`, after `MERGEABLE` and `UNSTABLE` while only a check failed. A merged one reads `UNKNOWN` for both.
      - `records_watch_reads` (`github_live.rs`) records `follow-red.json` and `follow-conflicting.json` on a disposable repository with Actions enabled (`pitchopp/owlshift-live-sandbox`; never `pitchopp/owlshift`, which the test refuses). Its branch adds a workflow with a passing and a failing job; it pushes a commit to the repository's `main` to make the conflict, then closes the pull request and deletes its branch. `record_watch_fixtures` (`github.rs`) records `follow-merged.json` (PR #16) and `base-checks.json` read-only on `pitchopp/owlshift`, with `base_at_push` a `main` commit whose CI failed:

        ```sh
        OWLSHIFT_LIVE_GITHUB=<owner>/<test-repo> \
          cargo test -p owlshift-adapters --test github_live -- --ignored --nocapture records_watch_reads
        cargo test -p owlshift-adapters --test github -- --ignored --nocapture record_watch_fixtures
        ```

      - `rate-limited.json` stays made up, and says why: spending the primary budget would drain the credential's budget shared with everything else using it, and a secondary limit cannot be provoked safely. The hygiene test allows that one fixture only.
  - **OWL-55: the core's counts and events.** Its question, a dedicated counter for gate failures, is answered no: the gate keeps `MAX_FAILED_RUNS`, as "The project gate", "Follow-ups" records. Its work becomes the fix-pass and repair counts below; its title is to be changed to match.
    - *State.* `TicketState` gains `fix_passes` (`MAX_FIX_PASSES` = 2) and `repairs` (`MAX_REPAIRS` = 6). `TICKET_STATE_FORMAT` goes from 2 to 3, and a format-2 document reads with both at 0. `restore` refuses either count past its bound, or above 0 at a stage other than Watch.
    - *Events, valid at Watch.* All are added to `Event::ALL`.
      - `FixPushed` and `RebasePushed`: the ticket stays at Watch, the failed runs are cleared, and `repairs`, plus `fix_passes` for a fix, gain one.
      - `ChecksGreen` clears `fix_passes` and the failed runs.
      - `FixDue` and `RebaseDue` park at a bound (`ParkReason::FixPasses`, `ParkReason::Repairs`) and otherwise leave the state as it is.
      - `ForgeBlocked` parks (`ParkReason::Forge`): a red no run fixes, or a push the forge refused.
    - *Existing events.* `ResultRefused` becomes valid at Watch as at Build, and `Restarted` clears both counts.
    - *Doc comments.* The `Event` table's Watch row, `Pipeline::loop_back` and `Stage::default_role` stop saying that fixes and rebases loop back to Build: at Watch, only review comments (P5) loop back.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-55, 2026-10-11) in `owlshift_core::state` (`MAX_FIX_PASSES`, `MAX_REPAIRS`, `TicketState::fix_passes` and `repairs`, the six events, the three park reasons) and `owlshift_contracts::refs::PersistedState` (format 3, reading format 2 with both counts at 0 and refusing a format-2 document that carries either). Three points the design left open:
      - Leaving Watch, which only review comments do (P5), clears both counts, as a new stage clears the failed runs: the next delivery is a new one, and `restore` refuses the counts elsewhere.
      - `FixPushed` with the fix passes or the repairs at their bound, and `RebasePushed` with the repairs at theirs, are invalid transitions: the runner gives `FixDue` or `RebaseDue` before each run.
      - `FixDue` with both counts at their bounds parks for the fix passes.
  - **OWL-112: the fix run.**
    - *The brief's `check_failure`.* A new brief format, with its schema, filled with the failed checks and their excerpts. The job log is read through REST `actions/jobs/<id>/logs` by the runner, the forge's only credential holder, which follows the redirect without the token. Timestamps and ANSI escapes are removed, the 16 KiB ending with the first `##[error]` line are kept, and the brief's 64 KiB limit applies.
    - *`roles/build.md`.* What a fix run is, and that a failure outside the branch is asked about.
    - *The run.* The build role at Watch on the Build routing, the gate, then the leased fast-forward push, a new DELIVERY report, `FixPushed`, and parks through `FixDue` and `ForgeBlocked`. Before the run, the ticket's stage is read (architecture section 5, "A ticket a person moved"), moved here from OWL-209. OWL-112 also parks on the table's non-code red (`ForgeBlocked`), which OWL-209 only records.
    - *Pending check.* (c) A fine-grained token needs "Actions: read" to download a job log, checked with the maintainer's token; `owlshift init`'s help for the personal token says so. Settled for the GitHub App's token on 2026-10-19 (C11): the app asks Actions (read), and its token read the log. For a personal token, the expert option, it stays pending.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-112, 2026-10-11); the rules as built are architecture section 5's "The fix run", "As built (OWL-112)".
      - Contracts: `Brief::check_failure` (`CheckFailure`, `FailedCheck`, `BaseState`), brief format 12, `schemas/brief.schema.json`; every role's front matter says 12.
      - Adapter: the follow query reads each check run's `databaseId` and each status's `description` (`Check::id`, `Check::output`); `GitHubForge::job_log_excerpt` streams `actions/jobs/<id>/logs` and keeps the excerpt, following the redirect with an agent that holds no token; `GitHubForge::check_run_output` reads another app's output by REST; `Transport::get` carries both, refused by default. `push_command` takes the lease.
      - Runner: `fix` assembles the brief's `CheckFailure`; `OnDemand::repair` (and `repair_seen` for watch) reads again under the lock, reads the stage (`Tracker::stage`, by the state's name on Linear and the `stage` line on the Markdown tracker), applies `ForgeBlocked` or `FixDue`, runs the build role at Watch through `run_build_once`, which `build` shares, pushes with the lease, applies `FixPushed` and posts the DELIVERY report naming the pass. `follow::changed` applies `FixPushed` when it adopts a `fix` push. Watch holds a repair as a continue (`Holds`). `GITHUB_TOKEN_HELP` names actions (read).
      - `roles/build.md`: "A fix run".
      - *Live check, 2026-10-11 (OWL-220).* The stage query (`query TicketStage($id: String!) { issue(id: $id) { state { name } } }`) was run against the Owlshift workspace: OWL-112 answers `{"data":{"issue":{"state":{"name":"Done"}}}}`, and an unknown ticket answers HTTP 200 with `data: null` and an `errors` entry `Entity not found: Issue` (code `INPUT_ERROR`). Both are recorded in `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/fixtures/linear/read-stage-owl-112.json` and replayed by `the_linear_tracker_reads_a_tickets_stage`; `record_read_stage_fixture` records it again. The unit test on a made-up answer is gone.
      - Tests: the excerpt, the redirect and the follow fields in the adapter; the leased push against a bare remote; the check-failure assembly and its 64 KiB in the runner; on the bench, red to a fix pushed, green, two more passes and the park; a moved ticket left alone, then a fix run that asks, parked; a red no run fixes and a push the forge refuses, parked; a person's push during the run, which ends the following.
      - *Deviation until OWL-211, removed.* A fix run's questions parked the ticket (`Blocked`) with a PARKED comment listing them; since OWL-211 they open a round returning to Watch.
      - *Check (c) still pending (2026-10-11).* This session had no personal token: its GitHub App user token read #89's check runs (200) and got the 302 on `actions/jobs/110572493177/logs`, as on 2026-10-10. The maintainer runs, with the stored token:

        ```sh
        OWLSHIFT_GITHUB_TOKEN=<the stored token> OWLSHIFT_LIVE_GITHUB=pitchopp/owlshift OWLSHIFT_LIVE_GITHUB_JOB=110572493177 \
          cargo test -p owlshift-adapters --test github_live -- --ignored --nocapture reads_a_failed_jobs_log
        ```

        Then the dated result goes into architecture section 5 and here. Until then a token refused the log makes the repair stop before any run (architecture section 5, "Failing closed on the token").
    - *Follow-up: OWL-218 (2026-10-11).* A DELIVERY report the tracker refused after the push landed, or the report of a push adopted after a crash, which OWL-112 never posted, is now owed in `delivery.json` (format 2) and posted at the next watch pass or `continue`, once: architecture section 5, "The fix run", "The owed report". Rebase pushes share it, and since OWL-146 correction pushes (format 3), each with its own arm in the adoption's count, `post_owed` and `push_what`, so a correction counts no fix pass and its report's lead line, "Corrected after delivery", is told apart from a fix pass's. The push under way keeps the run's summary, gate, checks and follow-ups (`KeptReport`), so an adopted push, and a push sent again after no verdict, reports what the run said. `OnDemand::post_owed_report` is called from `Watch::follow` and `settle_owed_report` from `watch_once`, one line each, after the look is kept and before any repair. Tests: the contract's format-1 read and refusals; `Writer::post_repair_report` posting once per push; adoption owing the report in `follow::changed`; on the bench, a refused report posted at the next watch pass and not again after a crash that lost the ref's write, `continue` posting it and an end dropping it, and an adopted push reported with what its run kept. Since OWL-231 (2026-10-12) the first delivery's report and a correction's with nothing pushed are posted once by the same rule, their lead line (none, for the first) and pull request line, instead of a body identical to the newest report; `post_repair_report` is now the same function under its OWL-218 name.
