Tests: a first report posted once per head whatever its checks, never stood in for by a repair's on the same head; and on the bench, a delivery whose ref was rewound to its push under way, its checks now read red, posting no second report. Since OWL-236 (2026-10-12) the first delivery's report and a correction's that pushed nothing are owed from the ref write that makes them due (`delivery.json` format 4), and every report goes through `post_owed` and `post_and_settle`: architecture section 5, "The owed report". Tests: the contract's format-3 read and refusals; on the bench, a first report refused at `do` posted once at the next watch pass and not again by `continue` after a crash that lost the ref's write, and the same for an unchanged correction's through `continue`. Since OWL-238 (2026-10-12) checks read at a moved head (`forge::ErrorKind::HeadMoved`) post nothing: `settle_owed` leaves the report owed and ends the pass, or stops `continue`, before any repair, and `post_and_settle` stops before the post, which replaces the stop OWL-236 gave the first delivery alone: architecture section 5, "The owed report". Tests, on the bench: the head moving before the owed report's checks are read, the report held, warned of once across a refusal before and after, then dropped when the next read ends the following; a fix report held after its push; and checks that cannot be read for another reason still posting it.
    - *Moved stage recorded once* (OWL-219, 2026-10-11). `stage_moved` is recorded when the stage read changes, not at each repair attempt; watch's memory (`Following`) keeps it, so a restart records it again. Architecture section 5, "A ticket a person moved".
  - **OWL-210: the rebase run.**
    - *`roles/rebase.md`.* `Role::Rebase` already exists in the core; only its prompt is new.
    - *Classification.* By `git merge-tree`, then the fast tier and its escalation to the Build routing. `Snapshot::take_rebase` is wired in, and the gate runs on the rebased commit. The ticket's stage is read before the run, as for OWL-112 (moved from OWL-209), and `RebaseDue` is applied before it.
    - *The forge adapter's leased forced push.* The one forced push, a new forge capability. A refused push ends the following or parks.
    - *Triggers.* The refresh onto a base gone green, and the failed run for a conflict still read against `rebased_onto`.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-210, 2026-10-11); the rules as built are architecture section 5's "Conflicts and the rebase run", "As built (OWL-210)".
      - Contracts: `Brief::rebase` (`RebaseBrief`: the pull request, the branch, its head, the base branch, `onto`, `refresh`, the conflicting paths), brief format 13, only in the rebase role's brief; `Pushing::onto`, kept for a rebase's push only (the delivery stays format 1); `rebased_onto` is the base the last landed rebase was onto.
      - `roles/rebase.md`: rebase onto the commit `onto`, never a branch name, without `--update-refs`; resolve keeping both sides' intent; abort before asking or failing, never end mid-rebase; the gate before `done`; never push.
      - Adapter: `Pushed::Forced`, read only by `read_forced_push_parts`; `read_push` still refuses a forced update.
      - Runner: `rebase::classify` (the merge-tree rule, a pure `parse` over its `-z` output); the executor's `take_rebase` for a brief with a `rebase`, refused unless its `onto` is the run's base, and the gate after a rebase `done`; `OnDemand::repair_rebase` (`RebaseDue`, the re-push, the classification, the fresh worktree per attempt, the tiers), `rebase_failed`, and `repair_runs`, the run loop fix and rebase runs share; `push_repair`, the fix's push generalised, with `Writer::push_rebased` and `writer::rebase_keeps_delivery`; `follow::landed` writes `pushed`, `base_at_push` and `rebased_onto` for a push that lands or is adopted. `OnDemand` gains `rebase` and `rebase_fast`; `owlshift do`, `continue` and `watch` run the rebase role on the standard tier's model and on `[models].fast`, else the standard one.
      - Tests: the rule on recorded output, the forced reading, the adoption, the report line and the brief's validation in their crates; against a bare remote, the forced leased push, its refusals before git (the base, no change, not on the base, a delivered file lost) and the lease refusing a moved or deleted branch, never recreated; the executor refusing another base; on the bench, a trivial conflict rebased on the fast tier and force-pushed by the runner, then read conflicting against the same base twice and parked; a modify/delete conflict on the Build routing at once, with the base moving during the run; and a rebase failing on the fast tier, then on the Build routing, parked with the branch back at the last push.
  - **OWL-211: back to the decider.**
    - *Questions.* A fix or rebase run's questions go to the decider without the resolver, in a round whose return stage is Watch. Since OWL-342 they go through the resolver first ("Every question through the resolver", below).
    - *The visible stage.* After an answer at Watch it shows review, not working.
    - *After the RESUME.* The ticket waits for watch's next pass, or a `continue`, which decides by the table. The run that follows carries the round in its brief's thread.
    - *Implemented* (OWL-211, 2026-10-12); the rules as built are architecture section 5's "Back to the decider", "As built (OWL-211)".
      - Runner: `OnDemand::repair_runs` opens the round through `ask` (`Round::of`, no resolver) for fix, rebase and correction runs; `check_answers` gives the RESUME its return stage (`ResumeComment::return_to`) and shows `VisibleStage::of` the resumed status; `VisibleStage::of` shows Watch as review; `ask`'s not-kept message names `owlshift continue` at Watch; watch keeps a followed ticket's hold across passes.
      - Tests: the RESUME's Watch lead and `VisibleStage::of` in the writer; on the bench, a fix run that asks after one fix pass, answered, then a fix run whose thread holds the round, pushed on the same branch, then green; a pull request green after the answer, with no fix run; a rebase run that asks; a correction that asks, answered, then corrects; a round not kept under watch, posted once.
      - Residual, resolved by OWL-242: a pull request merged or closed while its round waits was not read until the round was answered, since watch read no pull request of a waiting ticket.
  - **OWL-242: a round the pull request outlives** (decided 2026-10-13); the rules are architecture section 5's "Back to the decider", "A round the pull request outlives".
    - *Implemented* (OWL-242, 2026-10-13).
      - Core: `Event::FollowingEnded`, from a round waiting at Watch back to Watch, the counts kept.
      - Runner: `follow::changed` applies it when it ends the following of a waiting ticket; `Following::look_ending` reads the pull request with no base read; `Watch::close_round`, before the comments of a waiting ticket at Watch, moves the stage to review while it shows needs input (`OnDemand::review_after_round`), then writes the ref.
      - Tests: the transition table in the core; `changed` on a waiting record; on the bench, a round left waiting by a red read, then kept while its ref cannot be written, then closed by the merge with an answer posted meanwhile, which starts nothing; a closed pull request whose ticket a person moved to done keeps that stage.
  - **OWL-243: a park the pull request outlives** (decided 2026-10-13); the rules are architecture section 5's "Back to the decider", "A park the pull request outlives".
    - *Implemented* (OWL-243, 2026-10-13).
      - Core: `Event::FollowingEnded` is valid from a park at Watch too, back to Watch with the round, the fix passes and the repairs kept, the re-asks and failed runs cleared.
      - Runner: `follow::ends_with_following` names the statuses an end brings back to Watch, for `follow::changed` and `watch::outlived` alike; watch's pass reads a ticket parked at Watch as a waiting one, never looking at its comments; `Watch::close_round` became `Watch::close_outlived` and `OnDemand::review_after_round` became `OnDemand::review_after_end`, which moves the stage to review while it shows the state the ticket's status set (`VisibleStage::of`); `watch::ended_marks` gives the `ended` event's `parked` and `round` and the line's words.
      - Tests: the transition table and the counts in the core; `changed` on parked records; `ended_marks`; on the bench, a ticket parked at Watch by a red no run fixes, left as it was while its pull request is open, then shown in review once it is merged, `continue` then finding nothing to continue; and, on a project that maps a parked state, a closed pull request moving the ticket from it to review.
  - **OWL-146: the correction run** (decided 2026-10-11), scenario S7; the rules are architecture section 5's "A reversed decision".
    - *Implemented* (OWL-146, 2026-10-11).
      - Contracts: `KeptDecision::reversed` (`Reversal`: `through`, `comment`), `questions.json` format 10, format 9 still read; `PushKind::Correction` and `KeptReport::questions`, `delivery.json` format 3, formats 1 and 2 still read; `Brief::correction` (`Correction`, `ReversedDecision`, a reply cut to 16 KiB) and `ThreadEntry::Decision::reversed`, brief format 14; every role's front matter says 14.
      - Core: `Event::Corrected` at Watch, which clears the failed runs and counts nothing.
      - Adapters: `Comment::parent`, read from Linear's `parent { id }` (fixtures recorded again on 2026-10-11) and from the Markdown tracker's `reply_to`.
      - Runner: `reversal` (which replies reverse which decisions, when they count, what the brief gives); `OnDemand::correct` (and `correct_seen` for watch), in `on_demand/correction.rs`, on `repair_runs` and `push_repair` with `RepairKind::Correction`; Build's delivery keeps the replies its brief held; `Watch::follow` reads the comments of a followed ticket with decisions; the DECISION comment and the DELIVERY report's correction line (`writer::Corrected`); a reversed decision is reported as such (`resolver::reported`).
      - `roles/build.md`: "A correction", and a `reversed` decision is never followed.
      - Tests: the reversal rules in the runner; the reply in both trackers; the formats in contracts; on the bench, a reply under watch to a pushed correction past a stranger's reply and the quiet window, a reply followed before delivery that starts nothing then one the pull request already follows, and a correction that fails then asks and parks.
      - *With OWL-218* (merged first): a correction's push keeps its report and owes it as a fix's does, posted once by its lead line ("Corrected after delivery"); its report counts the decisions it follows the replies to, never a fix pass.
  - **OWL-212** runs the reworded exit gate ([roadmap](roadmap.md) P3).

**The resolver** (OWL-138, decided 2026-10-03), architecture section 4's "Resolver first" and scenario S7. A Build's questions go through it before any reaches the decider, but those that reopen a refused choice (OWL-222; every role's, those included, since OWL-342: "Every question through the resolver" below) (`owlshift_runner::resolver`, `on_demand::resolve`).

- **Routing.** Each question is routed by its category with `GatePolicy::route` (`resolver::route`): the floor's six tokens, a blank category and the project's `policy.always_human` go to the decider and never to the resolver; the rest go to a run of the `resolver` role (`roles/resolver.md`). A `premise_false` result goes to the decider whole (S8). Routing reads the category Build writes, so a floor-topic question filed under another category still reaches the resolver; the second label (below) is what keeps the resolver from deciding it. Since OWL-222, a question the asking run names as reopening a refused choice goes to the decider too, whatever its category (`resolver::route` takes the run's reopened choices; "A retry asks every refused choice" below).
- **The run.** Read-only, in the ticket's worktree at Build's last commit, on Claude Code with the answer check's program and login. Since OWL-344 ("Every question through the resolver" below): with bare Bash inside the read-only confinement, whose network stays open, its result taken from its last message; on the deep tier at the ticket's effort at delegation level `all`, the standard tier without an effort below (section 9); and a 30-minute deadline (`resolver::RESOLVER_TIMEOUT`). Before it: without Bash, its web tools removed, on the standard tier, with a 15-minute deadline. Its brief (format 7) lists its questions in `resolve`, under Build's ids and without their category, with the thread, the project's rules and `always_human`, the categories the project adds to the floor (OWL-151, decided 2026-10-04: `GatePolicy::additions`, normalized, once each, those the floor covers left out; Build's brief carries the same list, and its prompt and the resolver's tell each run to file or label a question about one under that category, the resolver to pass it on); it never holds a question Build filed as always-human. It may rest a decision on the decider's ticket and comments, earlier decisions, the rules and what it observes in the worktree; a "verifiable fact" is one it can check there, in the repository and its history. Other people's comments, the runner's own comments and any text saying what to decide are data. Its result (format 5) gives one resolution per question: `decided`, with its own `category`, the `decision` and its `basis`, or `passed_on`, with a `reason`. The executor refuses a resolution on a question the brief did not give, and the runner logs a decision only on a question it gave the resolver and both labels route there (`resolver::settle`).
- **The second label** (OWL-144, decided 2026-10-04). The resolver labels each question it decides from its text and context, never seeing Build's category, so its label is its own reading and not a copy of the one under suspicion. `GatePolicy::route_decided` sends the question to the decider when Build's category is always-human, when the resolver's is not a token (`floor::is_token`: ASCII lowercase letters and digits, words joined by single `_`), or when it is always-human, whole words counting as for Build's (`risk_of_data_loss` is `data_loss`). Such a decision is refused: no DECISION, nothing kept, and its question joins the round, which does not mention it. A token is required because the matcher reads words: `dataLoss` normalises to `dataloss` and `sécurité` to no floor word, so a free spelling could name a floor topic unseen; a malformed label costs the decider a question, never a wrong decision. Labels that merely differ (`naming`, `layout`) pass: the difference says nothing about the floor. What remains, as architecture section 4 records: a floor-topic question both runs label as something else is decided; the resolver's prompt still asks it to pass such a question on, a request on top of the check and not instead of it; the project's `policy.always_human` additions, though told to both runs (above), are still recognised by a model's judgment, so a question on one that both runs label otherwise is decided; and decisions kept before OWL-144 were logged without the check and stay.
- **Decisions.** The decisions of one resolver run are posted as one `[owlshift] DECISION` comment (`writer::DecisionComment`), one line each, numbered D1..Dn and never under Build's id, which a round's numbering would contradict, with the resolver's run in the footer; their reasoning is in the ticket's decisions document (OWL-351, "One DECISION comment per run" below; until then, one comment per decision with the question, the decision, what settles it and how to reverse it). They are then kept in the ticket ref (`questions.json` format 4, `decisions`), in one write for the run. A post that fails sends every question of the run to the decider: no decision is applied unlogged. A post whose keeping then fails stops the command and names the comment; a later run may post a second DECISION. A kept decision makes the ticket ref even when no round opens, an exception to "makes no ticket ref until a round opens" (`owlshift do` above): `continue` then runs Build at Build, and `do` starts from Ready keeping the decisions. Each kept decision takes the place of its comment in every later brief's thread as a `decision` entry, built from the ref alone: a comment that only looks like a DECISION, whoever wrote it, stays a comment read as Owlshift's, never a decision. Build follows a `decision` as the answer to its question, and a decider comment that says otherwise overrides it, wherever it sits in the thread (`roles/build.md`). The delivery report lists every kept decision among the decisions taken without a human. Deleting the dedicated checkout loses the kept decisions with the asks: their comments stay on the ticket as the runner's plain comments.
- **What is left.** Always-human questions, those that reopen a refused choice, passed-on ones and those whose DECISION was not posted go to the decider as the round, in Build's order, renumbered Q1..Qn as a round must be; the QUESTIONS comment says how many others were decided. The core's `Questions` is applied only then: the ticket stays at Build through the resolver. When nothing is left, Build runs again in the same command, with no stop and no counter moved, and without the quiet window of `continue` (OWL-127), which holds a round's answers only: no reply is awaited. After the project's pass limit (`policy.resolver_passes`, 5 by default, `MAX_RESOLVED_PASSES` in `owlshift_core::state`, 3 before OWL-337; or its dollar ceiling, below) such runs in a row in one command, the next run's questions all go to the decider, which architecture section 4 records as the bound on "Resolver first"; at `all`, since OWL-375, the runner takes their recommendations instead ("The bound at `all`" below).
- **Fallback.** No other harness can take over yet (P6, and a deviation from section 9's fallbacks): a resolver at its usage limit, a failed one, one without a valid result or past its deadline sends every question to the decider in a normal round, which says why. A refused result is kept for the resolver run that replays its questions when the round does not reach the decider (OWL-191, "A refused resolver result told to its replay" below). A resolver that breaks isolation is quarantined and parks the ticket; its restart runs Build again from its checkpoint, which asks again.
- **Events.** A `gate` event for each routing that reaches the resolver or its bound: `resolver` (`done`, `usage_limit`, `failed` or `pass_limit`), `raised_by` and `run`, and the question ids `given`, `decided`, `to_decider` and, after a run, `passed_on`, `refused` (decisions the second label refused) and `unposted`, the last two also in `to_decider`. A `tracker_write` for each DECISION names its `questions` (since OWL-351; its `question` before), and the round's `gate` event names Build's ids of the questions it asks (`raised_as`).
- **Delegation and passes** (OWL-328, decided with the maintainer on 2026-10-17; as built by OWL-337, below). Architecture section 4, "Delegation level" and "Resolver passes", gives the rules; what the build settles here, so that the implementation ticket decides none of them again:
  - *Configuration.* `[policy]` takes `resolver_delegation` (`none`, `facts`, `discoverable`, `ticket` or `all`; `discoverable` when absent, today's behaviour), `resolver_passes` (a whole number from 1 to 50, or `"unlimited"`; 5 when absent) and `resolver_budget_usd` (a positive number of dollars). `"unlimited"` without `resolver_budget_usd` makes the file invalid, naming both keys. A file using any of the three raises its `requires`. `owlshift config show` prints them with their source, and `owlshift doctor` adds a `resolver` line naming the level, the pass limit and the ceiling, as a note, since none blocks `do`.
  - *Core.* `GatePolicy` holds the level, the pass limit and the ceiling, read from the person's project file like `always_human` and every other `[policy]` key (architecture section 5, "Which commit configures a run", OWL-331); `MAX_RESOLVED_PASSES` becomes the default of the pass limit, now 5. The always-human set of a level is the floor (`security`, `irreversible`) plus the project's additions plus, below `ticket`, `scope`, and below `all`, `data_loss`, `money` and `legal`; `route` and `route_decided` read that set. `none` routes every question to the decider and no resolver run starts. The core stays pure: the chain's cost is a number the runner passes in.
  - *The basis kind.* The resolver's result gains, on each `decided` resolution, `basis_kind`: `fact` (observed in the repository and its history), `settled` (the ticket, the decider's comments, earlier decisions, the project's rules) or `ticket` (the ticket's own text, with `quote`). The result format and the resolver's brief, which now carries the level, are bumped. The runner refuses a decision whose kind the level does not allow (`facts` allows `fact`; `discoverable` adds `settled`; `ticket` and `all` add `ticket`), and a `ticket` decision whose quote, spaces collapsed, is not found in the ticket's title, description or a comment of its decider, as intake's dependency quotes are checked (OWL-248). A refused decision sends its question to the round, as the second label's refusals do. At `ticket`, a `scope` question may be decided on a `ticket` basis only.
  - *The chain's ceiling.* The runner sums the `usage` events' `cost_usd` of the resolver runs since the command's last stop (a round, a park, a delivery or the command's start) and compares it before each resolver run; a resolver run with no reported cost ends the chain. The asking runs counted too until OWL-376 (below). The next run's questions then go to the decider, with the reason in the round and the `gate` event.
  - *Visibility.* Each DECISION comment names the level that allowed it on its closing line, the last one the reader sees, above the machine-readable footer (since OWL-351, on its lead line, the closing line saying how to reverse), the delivery report's list of decisions taken without a human says it once, and the `gate` event `resolver` gains `level` and, for a chain ended by its ceiling, `pass_limit` with `reason` `budget`; since OWL-376 it also gives the resolver's spend against the ceiling (below).
  - *Tests.* Core: routing of each category at each level, the floor at `all`, the additions at every level, the pass limit at its default, a set value and unlimited with its ceiling. Runner: a basis kind above the level refused, a `ticket` quote found and one not found, a chain ended by its ceiling and by a run without a cost; since OWL-376, a chain whose asking runs cost more than the ceiling going on. Bench: one scenario where `ticket` lets a quoted `scope` question through, and the same ticket at `discoverable` sending it to the decider.
  - *As built* (OWL-337, 2026-10-17).
    - Corrections to the entry above, made with the build: the policy is read from the person's project file, as `always_human` and every `[policy]` key are (OWL-331); "at the base commit" was stale wording. The DECISION comment's level is a visible line, "Allowed by this project's delegation level, `ticket` (`policy.resolver_delegation`).", just above the machine-readable footer, whose contract is unchanged (footer format 1): the decider reads the level, which a field of the hidden footer would not show. "Raises its `requires`" is the file author's convention, as for every key added since P2: nothing enforces it beyond an older Owlshift refusing the unknown key.
    - Contracts: `[policy]` `resolver_delegation`, `resolver_passes` (a whole number or `"unlimited"`; a number outside 1 to 50, a word other than `unlimited` or a fraction is refused, naming the key), `resolver_budget_usd` (refused unless a finite number of at least 0.000001, the millionth of a dollar the gate counts in, below which the ceiling would round to nothing); `"unlimited"` without a ceiling names both keys. `Policy::resolver` gives the core's `ResolverPolicy`, and `ProjectConfig::gate_policy` the whole `GatePolicy`, which `owlshift do` and the scenarios' stand-in driver build alike. `result.json` format 11: `basis_kind` on each `decided` resolution, `quote` with `ticket` and with no other kind, refused when blank, holding a control character or over 512 bytes, the rules of an intake dependency's quote (`check_quote`); the schema carries the quote's rule. Brief format 17: `delegation`, in the resolver's brief and only there; `always_human` lists the project's additions the level does not keep already, so at `all` a project's `money` is told. Every role's front matter says 17 and 11, and `roles/build.md` and `roles/design.md` no longer say the six categories always go to the decider: `security` and `irreversible` do, the other four as the level says.
    - Core: `DelegationLevel` and `BasisKind` in the vocabulary, `DelegationLevel::allows` the kinds; `FloorCategory::human_at` is the one place the levels' sets are written, and `floor::is_human_category`, by level, replaces `is_floor_category`. `GatePolicy::with_resolver`, `route` (everything to the decider at `none`), `route_decided` with the basis kind (the scope rule at `ticket` reads either label), `chain_end` over a `Chain` (settled passes and cost). The ceiling and the costs are held in millionths of a dollar; a reported cost that is negative or not a finite number counts as unreported; the ceiling is reached when the chain's cost is at or above it.
    - Runner: one `Chain` per command in `Prepared`, across Intake, Design and Build (intake's own counter is gone): the asking run's cost is added when its questions reach the resolver, the bound is checked, and the resolver run's cost is added once it ran; a settled pass counts in `resolve` itself. The quote is looked for in the resolver brief's `ticket.title`, `ticket.description` and the thread comments whose relation is `decider`, each text's whitespace runs collapsed (`resolver::Quotable`); a quote across two texts is not found. `Fallback::PassLimit` carries the limit, `Budget` and `CostUnreported` end a chain at its ceiling. Every `gate` event with `resolver` carries `level`; a chain ended at its ceiling is `pass_limit` with `reason` `budget`, or `cost_unreported`, an addition to the entry above, for a run without a cost. The DELIVERY report names, once, the level in force for the command that delivers, when the resolver's decisions are listed: a level changed between two decisions of one ticket is not kept per decision, each DECISION comment naming its own. `owlshift doctor`'s `resolver` line is an information line; `owlshift config show` adds a `resolver` section, each value with its file or `default`, and lists the always-human categories the level keeps beside the floor's.
    - Tests: the core's routing table per level, the additions per level, the kinds per level and the scope rule, the chain's ends; the contracts' keys, formats and quote rules; the runner's quote search and refusals per level, `Fallback`'s reasons, the DECISION line, the report's mention, the round's texts, the doctor line and `config show`; on the bench, the quoted `scope` question decided at `ticket` and asked at `discoverable`, a quote the ticket does not hold refused, and an unlimited chain ended at a $1 ceiling by the costs the fake harness reports (`Reply::cost_micros`) and by a resolver run that reports none. The three benches written for 3 passes set `resolver_passes = 3`.
    - What remains: Codex reports no cost, so with a ceiling set a Codex run ends the chain at the next check (since OWL-376, a Codex resolver run only).
  - *The resolver runs alone* (OWL-376, 2026-10-19; architecture section 4, "Resolver passes", gives why).
    - Core: `Chain::resolved` adds a resolver run's cost, in place of `Chain::ran`, which the asking runs called too. `Chain::asked` adds an asking run's cost to a sum the ceiling never reads, so one that reports no cost, a Codex Build, no longer ends the chain. `ChainEnd::Budget` and `Fallback::Budget` carry the spend and the ceiling, in millionths of a dollar.
    - Visibility: every `gate` event with `resolver` carries `resolver_spent_usd`, the chain's resolver spend after the event's own run (`null` once a resolver run reported no cost), `ceiling_usd` when a ceiling is set, and `asking_spent_usd`, measured only: what the asking runs whose questions reached the resolver cost, not every run of the chain (a run that asked nothing is not counted), `null` once one reported no cost. `owlshift do` and `owlshift logs TICKET` show them for a ticket; the figures are the chain's and start again at each stop, and the `usage` events give each run's cost. A round ended by the ceiling says what the resolver runs spent of it, and `owlshift doctor`'s `resolver` line says the ceiling is on the resolver runs of one chain. `owlshift why` is not built yet; a per-ticket total exists since OWL-378 (*The ticket's budget*, below).
    - Tests: the core's chain ends, with asking runs no longer counted; the round's texts; on the bench, a chain at a $1 ceiling whose Build runs cost $2 each, one reporting no cost, resolved four times before its resolver runs reached $1.20 and the round said `budget`, every `gate` event giving the spend so far, and a resolver run without a cost ending its chain.
    - What remains: a free resolver run (a cost of 0 reported) would never reach the ceiling; Claude Code reports a cost on every run. The asking runs' cost, a ticket's whole cost, was bounded per run only; since OWL-378 the ticket's budget bounds it (*The ticket's budget*, below).
  - *The bound at `all`* (OWL-375, 2026-10-20; architecture section 4, "Resolver passes", gives the rules and why).
    - Core: `BasisKind::Recommendation` (`recommendation`), which `DelegationLevel::allows` at `all` alone. `Chain` keeps the asking roles that took recommendations at its bound (`Chain::took_recommendations`, `Chain::recommendations_taken`), carried across watch's steps with the rest of the chain and dropped at each stop.
    - Runner: in `resolve`, once `chain_end` says the chain ended, at `all`, unless the asking run found the premise false or its role already took recommendations in the chain, `resolver::recommended` makes a `decided` resolution for each question given the resolver that reopens no refused choice and has a recommendation: the question's own category as its label, the recommended option's label (or, without options, the recommendation's `why`) as its decision, the `why` as its basis, the recommended letter as its option. `resolver::settle` sorts them as it sorts the resolver's, so `route_decided` holds the category to the level's set and to the token rule, and a blank or malformed category goes to the decider. They are posted and kept on the resolver's path (one DECISION comment, its lead line naming the bound, `DecidedBy::Recommendation`, the asking run in its footer). Only those posted and kept count: they mark the role and leave the round; no settled pass is counted. What is left is a round with the bound's reason. An approval taken so keeps the DECISION in `approval.comment`, and its `plan` event says `by` `recommendation`.
    - Contracts: `questions.json` format 16, for a kept decision's `recommendation` kind; formats 2 to 15 are still read, and one of them holding that kind is refused. A resolver's result giving it is refused, by its schema and by the executor: the kind is the runner's. `BasisKind` is shared by the resolver's result and the kept decision, so the result refuses a value its type has, a wart a basis type of the result's own would remove.
    - Visibility: the DECISION comment's lead line says Owlshift took the asking run's recommendation and names the bound. The `gate` event stays `pass_limit` with its `reason`, and gains `decided` and `recommended`. The DELIVERY report lists such decisions first, under "Taken on the asking run's recommendation", with how to reverse one. A round left at the bound says the rest of the run's questions come to the decider.
    - Tests: core, the kinds per level and the chain's roles; contracts, the result's refusal and format 16; runner, the resolutions made, the DECISION lead line, the report's heading and the round's text; on the bench, at `all` with its ceiling spent mid-chain, Design's two questions and then the approval taken on their recommendations up to Build, a reply naming `D2` reversing that decision alone, and the delivery report's heading; the same chain at `discoverable` asking both; a `security` question asked beside one taken; a chain carried to a step with its role marked asking the decider.
    - What remains: no second label at the bound, as architecture section 4 records.
  - *The ticket's budget* (OWL-378, 2026-10-20; architecture section 4, "Ticket budget", gives the rules and why).
    - Core: `TicketSpend` (the runs counted, those without a cost, the spend and the spend at the last restart, in millionths of a dollar; `ran`, `restarted`, `reached`) and `BudgetReached`, in `gate`. `ParkReason::TicketBudget` and `Event::BudgetReached`, valid wherever a run can be in flight, parking where the ticket stands, waiting for input if it was.
    - Contracts: `[policy] ticket_budget_usd`, checked by the rule `resolver_budget_usd` now shares, the amount as written at least 0.000001 (a smaller one that rounded up to the millionth was taken before), and given in millionths (`Policy::ticket_budget_micros`). `cost.json` in the ticket ref (`TicketCost`, format 1, schema `ticket-cost`), refused when its parts contradict each other.
    - Runner: `OnDemand::execute` counts each run as soon as the executor gives its report; `Prepared::cost`, read from the ref, rides on every write of it that keeps something else, never alone. `within_budget` holds `run_role_once`'s runs and the answer check's; `resolve` checks the budget before the direct routes and the chain's bounds (`Fallback::TicketBudget`, `pass_limit` with `reason` `ticket_budget`). A person's restart counts the budget afresh; `owlshift forget` keeps `cost.json`, and says so. The round's sentence, the PARKED comment and the DECISION lead line at `all` name the spend, the budget and the runs without a cost (`writer::budget_line`). `owlshift config show` prints the key under `budget`, and `owlshift doctor` adds a `budget` information line.
    - Tests: core, the spend and the transitions in the table; contracts, the key, its smallest amount and `cost.json`; the ref's round trip, `forget` and its salvage; the round's text; on the bench, a round at the budget with no resolver run, then a park waiting for input and a restart that delivers, a park at Build across commands, the recommendation at `all` then the park, and the counts of every run of a `standard` ticket and of Watch's fix runs.
    - What remains: the residuals architecture section 4 accepts, a run without a cost counted as nothing and the cost of a command that writes nothing after its runs, or of a ticket without a ref, not kept.
- **Every question through the resolver** (OWL-342, decided with the maintainer on 2026-10-18). Architecture section 4, "Every question through the resolver", gives the rules; what the build settles, so that the implementation tickets decide none of them again:
  - *Routing.* `resolver::route` keeps two direct routes to the decider, a category of the level's always-human set as filed by the asking run (at `all`, the floor and the project's additions) and the plan-approval question under `plan_approval = "always"`, and `none`, which starts no run. Every other route around the resolver goes: the refused-choice route (OWL-222), the whole-round route of `premise_false` (OWL-306), the review residue, approval and `replan` rounds (OWL-296, "The rounds" below), the late-comment run's round (OWL-139) and the questions of fix, rebase and correction runs at Watch (OWL-211, "Questions" above). A blank category reaches the resolver; `route_decided` holds the resolver's label to the level's set, as for any question. The questions of one run are routed together: the resolver run is given all those not routed directly, in the asking run's order. What is left is a round as before, returning to the stage that asked (Watch for a repair run).
  - *The `judgment` kind.* `BasisKind::Judgment`, allowed by `DelegationLevel::All` alone. `result.json` gains it on a `decided` resolution, its `basis` holding the reasons and the cost of the choice, no `quote`. The DECISION comment's visible line reads "Decided on judgment, allowed by this project's delegation level, `all` (`policy.resolver_delegation`).", and the delivery report lists judgment decisions before the others.
  - *Plan approval and premise.* A `plan_approval` question the resolver decides records `approved_by` in `plan.json`: the DECISION comment's id instead of the decider's comment, with the plan's hash; a fork option chosen sends the ticket back to Design as an answer does. A `premise_false` question's closure option cannot be decided: the runner refuses it and the question goes to the decider; the re-scope is held to the `scope` rule of the level.
  - *The brief.* Format bumped: `asked_by` names the asking run's role and run id and carries its `summary`, and, when the role produced them, the draft plan (Design), the findings with the arbiter's rulings (Design review, Verify) and the path of the branch's diff from its base, written in the run files. A `review_finding` question carries its finding's id. The resolver's prompt (`roles/resolver.md`) gains the `judgment` rules, what it may decide on a finding, an approval and a false premise, and loses "No level lets you decide what nothing settles".
  - *The run.* At `all`, the deep tier and the ticket's effort (`routing.rs`); below, the standard tier as before. Deadline 30 minutes (`resolver::DEADLINE`). Bash allowed in the read-only permission level for the resolver only: `--permission-mode dontAsk --allowedTools Bash`, with no `Edit(...)` rule, inside the read-only confinement, the result given as the run's last message (as built by OWL-344, below: the read-only profile keeps the result out of the worktree, so no rule could name it). The network stays open to that Bash, as the confinement leaves it to every run (architecture section 8); the decider accepted it on OWL-344, the web tools still removed. This waited on a live check, the first step of its ticket: on macOS (`sandbox-exec`) and Linux (`bwrap`), with Claude Code's current version, a resolver-shaped run with Bash allowed reads files, runs `git log`, `git blame` and `grep`, and has a write in the worktree and in `.git` and a write outside it refused by the confinement; its dated result is in OWL-344's entry below. Codex's read-only sandbox already runs commands.
  - *Events.* The `gate` event `resolver` gains `asked_by` (the role) so that the share of rounds the resolver settled shows per role.
  - *Tests.* Core: the routing table without the bypasses, at each level, `always` approval direct, a blank category given. Contracts: the kind per level, the brief's `asked_by`. Runner: a residue finding, an approval, a `replan`, a `premise_false` re-scope decided and its closure refused, a repair run's question decided at Watch, a judgment decision's line and the report's order. Bench: a Design question decided on judgment at `all` and passed on at `discoverable`; a residue finding decided and applied by the next Design run.
  - *As built* (OWL-343, 2026-10-18; the resolver's tier, deadline and Bash are OWL-344's).
    - Corrections to the entry above, made with the build: `plan.json` is unchanged; a resolver's approval keeps its DECISION comment's id in `approval.comment`, where the decider's keeps theirs, as architecture section 4 says ("`approved_by`" above named that field's role, not a new one). `asked_by` also says whether the asking run found the premise false (`premise_false`), the one way the resolver tells a premise question, and gives the kept plan whenever the pipeline has Design, Build's `replan` included. A round the resolver decides whole at Design review or Verify buys the pass after it, as an answered round does (core `Event::RoundDecided`, below), which `policy.resolver_passes` and the ceiling bound; the resolver's prompt passes on a finding that repeats one a `decision` entry settled, the reviewers disagreeing with it.
    - The option a decision takes: `result.json` format 12 adds `option` to a `decided` resolution, the letter of the option it takes, written as `Recommendation.option` is (`^[A-Z]+$`); the executor refuses a letter its question in the brief does not offer. The runner reads it twice. On the approval question, which the runner writes, option `A` ("Approve the plan as it is") keeps the approval and the plan gate takes the ticket to Build in the same command; any other decision on it, as a decided residue round, is `RoundDecided`: back to Design, whose run reads the `decision` entry as it reads the decider's reply (`roles/design.md`), an `approved` answer then keeping that DECISION comment. On a `premise_false` result's questions, the runner adds the closure itself as each one's last option, "Close the ticket: its premise is false" (`resolver::with_closure`), for the resolver and the round alike, and logs a decision only when it takes another option, a re-scope, held to the level's `scope` rule as a decision on a `scope` question; the closure, or no option, is refused. The asking roles offer the re-scope alone (`roles/intake.md`, `roles/design.md`, `roles/build.md`). What remains: a role that writes its own closure option too gives the resolver a closure it can take under the re-scope's letter; the run that reads that decision ends `blocked` and the ticket parks, so a person still sees it.
    - Core: `BasisKind::Judgment`, allowed by `DelegationLevel::All` alone. A blank category routes to the resolver (`floor::is_human_category` no longer counts it; a blank resolver label is still refused, not being a token). `GatePolicy::approval_is_human` (the risky variant or `always`). `Event::RoundDecided`, valid at Design review and Verify: to `Pipeline::round_return`, the pass ended and counted, no round.
    - Contracts: brief format 18 (`asked_by` in the resolver's brief, always, and only there: `role`, `run`, `premise_false`, `summary`, `plan`, `findings` as `review.json` keeps them with their rulings, `diff`; `resolve`'s `finding`); `result.json` format 12 (`judgment`, `option`); `questions.json` format 13 (a kept decision's `basis_kind`, absent on one kept before; formats 2 to 12 still read). Every role's front matter says 18 and 12.
    - Runner: one `OnDemand::resolve` for every asking site, given an `Asked` description of the run: Intake's, Design's, Build's (the late-comment run's and a `replan` on an approved plan included), the residue and approval rounds of Design review and Verify (the pass's last run, its summed cost for the chain, the findings per question), and fix, rebase and correction runs at Watch, whose questions all decided run the repair again, an attempt not counted. The only direct routes left are the level's always-human set as filed and the approval question under `always`; the runner's own question after a second late comment stays the decider's. A review round is put to the resolver before the review record takes its pass, kept with the state the outcome moves to, so a command cut short runs the pass again. The resolver is told the branch's diff from its base, written at `.owlshift/run/diff.patch` when the branch has commits of its own. The `gate` event `resolver` carries `asked_by` (the role); a resolver's approval is a `plan` `approved` decision event with `by` `resolver`. The DECISION comment's visible line is "Decided on judgment, allowed by this project's delegation level, `all` (`policy.resolver_delegation`)." on a judgment; the DELIVERY report lists judgment decisions first, each basis followed by "(decided on judgment)". The PLAN comment's next step no longer says the decider answers: "The plan waits for its approval, by a DECISION comment or by the decider's answer below.", and likewise for the findings.
    - Tests: the core's routing table with a blank category, the kinds per level, the approval route and `RoundDecided` at a last pass; the contracts' formats, `asked_by`, `option` and `judgment`; the runner's routing, the premise rule, the report's order and the DECISION line; on the bench, the judgment decided at `all` and refused at `discoverable`, a residue finding decided and applied by the next Design run, an approval under `on-fork`, a premise re-scope decided and its closure refused, a fix run's question decided at Watch, and every former bypass now through the resolver (refused choices, the late-comment run, `replan` on an approved plan, the residue rounds, false premises, repair runs); the TOML scenarios `open_points*` play the reopened choice the resolver decides again, as before OWL-222.
    - What remains: a stale `.owlshift/run/diff.patch` from an earlier run is left in place when the branch has no diff of its own, and is named in no brief. Reversing a resolver's approval or residue decision by reply reached the next Build only (OWL-146), the plan gate not reopened; OWL-345 returns the ticket to the author stage before delivery (next entry).
  - *As built* (OWL-345, 2026-10-18). A reply to the DECISION comment of a resolver's plan approval or residue decision returns the ticket before delivery (architecture section 5, "A reversed decision"): the decision kept as the plan's approval, and a Design review residue, to Design; a Verify residue, to Build.
    - Contracts: `KeptDecision::asked_by` (the role whose run asked the question), `questions.json` format 14, formats 2 to 13 still read and a document before 14 carrying `asked_by` refused. A residue question of Design review cannot be told from one of Verify by its category (`review_finding`) or its id (they restart at Q1 each ask), and the review record does not link a finding to its decision, so the field is the runner's own record rather than an inference from wording. A plan approval needs no role: it is the decision whose DECISION comment is `plan.approval.comment` (since OWL-351, the one on the approval question, a comment holding a run's every decision).
    - Core: `Event::ReversedToDesign` (Design review, Build or Verify to Design, no pass counted, the pass count kept from Design review and restarted from the others) and `Event::ReversedToBuild` (Verify to Build, count kept); both refused when the pipeline lacks the target, so `trivial` has no return to Design. `Event::ALL` has 29 events.
    - Runner: `reversal::reopens` (the stage a kept decision's reply returns to) and `reversal::reopening` (the earliest destination reachable from the ticket's stage, the reversals it serves, whether the approval is dropped); `OnDemand::reopened`, called at the top of each `drive` iteration, which does nothing in a review loop (`in_review_loop`), waits for the quiet window, and in one `store` write drops the approval, marks the served reversals followed and moves the state, with a `decision` event `reversed` = `reopened` naming the stage left and entered, the question ids and the replies. The Build that completes outside a review loop no longer marks followed a reversal that `reopens` gives a stage, so a reply the check left settling stays for the correction run.
    - Tests: `reversal.rs` (the pure rules), `state.rs` (the two events), the contracts' format 14, and the bench's `reversal_scenarios.rs`, one file for the scenarios instead of the three stage files of the plan, for they share setups: an approval reversed returns to Design with the approval dropped and asked again, a Design review residue to Design, a Verify residue to Build, a Verify residue reversed while the fix Build is cut off by a usage limit, where the ticket is already at Build and nothing moves (the hook's in-place branch, recorded from Build to Build), a stranger's reply, a reply inside the quiet window that a completed Build leaves unfollowed, and a reversed approval during a review loop that leaves the ticket at Build. There the loop's Build follows the reply from its brief and marks it followed, as before, so no correction run follows for a reply it read: a deviation from the plan's step (f), which left that reply to the correction run, decided on the ticket so as to add no post-delivery path (architecture section 5, "A reversed decision", "After delivery"). A reply to a decision of Build moving nothing is covered by the pure rule's unit test (`reversal::reopens`), not by a bench scenario. The existing correction scenarios after delivery are unchanged.
    - What remains: a reply posted during a run, between the Build, Verify and Verify fix runs of one `build` call (the check runs only when a command starts or restarts the ticket), or between the last check and the push is not returned and starts the correction run after delivery; a reply still inside the quiet window at a `continue` or watch resume is not returned either: the stage runs, the decision stays unfollowed (the hook does not stop with `Stop::Settling`, as the answer check and the correction run do), and the correction run after delivery follows the reply if nothing returns the ticket sooner; after delivery nothing returns to Design or Build (no path from Watch, a decision of the resolver on OWL-345's Q2); a residue decision kept before format 14 keeps the old behaviour, the next run (Design after a Design review residue, Build after a Verify residue) following the reply with the plan gate not reopened; a pipeline without Design (`trivial`) has no return to Design.
  - *As built* (OWL-344, 2026-10-20): the resolver's tier, deadline and Bash.
    - Corrections to the entry above, made with the build: the argv has no `Edit(/<result path>)` rule beside `Bash`. The read-only profile puts the brief and the result in the run's own files, outside the worktree, where no rule anchored at the working directory reaches, so the resolver gives its result as its last message, as Codex does. "Without network" does not hold for Bash: the confinement leaves the network open to every run, and the decider accepted it for the resolver on OWL-344 (2026-10-19); the web tools are still removed. The ticket's `resolver::DEADLINE` is `resolver::RESOLVER_TIMEOUT`.
    - Core: `routing::resolver_routing(level, ticket)`, Claude Code on the deep tier at the ticket's effort at `DelegationLevel::All`, `role_routing(Role::Resolver, ..)` (the project default) at every level below.
    - Adapter: `claude::Request::bash`. A read-only request with it passes `--allowedTools Bash` in place of the result's `Edit` rule; a write-in-worktree one is refused (`CommandError::Unsupported`), that mode allowing every tool already. Every other read-only launch keeps its `Edit` rule and no Bash.
    - Runner: `ClaudeHarness::bash` passes the flag, asks for the read-only profile and the result from the last message (`SandboxNeeds::read_only`, `result_in_message`), and tells the run where its brief is in the run's own files (`harness::prompt_with_brief_file`, shared with Codex, whose text is unchanged). Its end (`claude_end`, with `in_message` set by the flag) reads the last message as the executor reads a read-only run's message (OWL-323): bare JSON, or the last fenced block when its closing fence ends the message; any other message comes whole, and the executor writes it at the result's place, where it is refused as an invalid result. The executor writes a message given as the result whole, as before, so a Codex run's result is read as it was. `OnDemand.resolver` is a `Router`: `resolve` routes each run with `resolver_routing(level, ..)` over the ticket's routing as a dispatch reads it (`read_routing`: the `tier:` and `effort:` labels over intake's verdict, so at Intake, before any verdict, the label's effort). `RESOLVER_TIMEOUT` is 30 minutes, the executor's deadline `resolve` gives each resolver run; no test restates the constant, and a bench run cannot show the deadline short of reaching it.
    - CLI: `do_cmd::resolver_router` builds the resolver's router from the shared harness with the resolver's prompt and `bash: true`.
    - Tests: the core's routing per level; the adapter's contract test `the_resolver_argv_allows_bash_without_an_edit_rule` (the whole argv the live check ran, `dontAsk` then `Bash` alone, no `Edit(` argument, with and without network; the write-in-worktree refusal), the Bash launch added to `every_launch`, and no Bash in a plain read-only launch; the harness's needs, argv and prompt with and without Bash; `a_bash_run_gives_the_json_of_its_last_message`, the end of a run with Bash reading bare JSON and a closing block after prose, and prose alone whole; the executor bench writing such a message whole, refusing prose even with a closing block; the CLI's `the_resolver_is_built_with_bash`; on the bench, the resolver run routed deep at the label's effort at `all`, standard without an effort at `discoverable`, without an effort at `all` for a ticket with no `effort:` label, and at the label's effort at Intake. `confinement.rs`'s read-only probe adds `git log`, `git blame`, `grep` and a write beside the worktree under the real sandbox: it checks the profile the resolver's run asks for, a shell script standing for Claude Code, and is skipped where there is no sandbox; Claude Code under that profile is the live check below, repeatable since OWL-386 as `executor_live.rs`'s ignored `the_resolvers_bash_reads_and_never_writes`.
    - The live check, how it was run. A person ran it on macOS and in Linux, through the real `Executor::run` with `AgentEnv::from_runner`, the agent-run token on an inherited descriptor and the read-only sandbox, a throwaway test wrapping `ClaudeHarness` with the two changes of this ticket (`--allowedTools Bash`; `read_only` and `result_in_message`), and posted the output on OWL-344. The argv is the contract test's, with `--model` and `--effort` as routed: `claude -p --output-format stream-json --verbose --permission-mode dontAsk --allowedTools Bash --permission-prompts none --disallowedTools RemoteTrigger PushNotification DesignSync Workflow CronCreate CronDelete CronList SendMessage ListAgents EnterWorktree ExitWorktree Agent WebFetch WebSearch --setting-sources '' --strict-mcp-config --disable-slash-commands --settings '{"crossSessionInbound":"refuse"}' --no-session-persistence`. The environment: the variables of `agent_env::INHERITED`, then `OVERRIDES` (`GH_TOKEN` and `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN` without a credential, the `GIT_CONFIG_*` settings, `GIT_ASKPASS`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`), `CLAUDE_CODE_HARBOR_KITE=0`, then on the harness command alone `TMPDIR`, `TMP` and `TEMP` to the run's own folder, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` to its empty `harness-config`, `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` to `harness-tmp`, the token's descriptor named by `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN_FILE_DESCRIPTOR`, and on macOS `SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/ssl/cert.pem`; Bash saw no `ANTHROPIC_*` variable and no token. The folders, `AgentEnv::policy` of a `RunPaths` with `read_only: true`: the worktree and the common git folder read, never written; written, the run's `run-files`, `harness-config` and temporary folder; read, the folders `claude` is installed in, `~/.gitconfig`, `~/.config/git`, `~/.rustup`, `~/.cargo/bin`, `~/.cargo/registry`, `~/.cargo/git` and the `PATH` folders under the home; hidden, the credential files and the runner's run folder; closed, the rest of the home and the shared temporary folders. The profile, the generated text pasted with the output: `sandbox::seatbelt_profile` under `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec` on macOS, `sandbox::bwrap_args` under `/usr/bin/bwrap` on Linux, both through `AgentEnv::confine`, from the same input as the executor's own call. The probes, one Bash call each: read a file, `git log`, `git blame`, `grep`, a write in the worktree, three writes in `.git` (a redirection, `git update-ref`, `touch` through `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`), writes outside (the test's folder, the home; on Linux also a folder outside every private one), and a network call (`curl https://example.com`), each written file then looked for on the host.
    - Differences from a production resolver run, none of which changes what Bash can read, write or reach: the run used main at b2c3daf, newer than this branch's base, whose `OVERRIDES` set four more git housekeeping settings (OWL-339) and whose policy reopens the run's own worktree entry, empty under the read-only profile; the model was `claude-sonnet-5-5` at effort `low`; the prompt named `.owlshift/run/brief.json` (`prompt_with_brief`), not the run's own brief file, which the probes do not read; the environment was inherited from a `cargo test` in the person's login shell, filtered by `AgentEnv`.
    - **Checked on 2026-10-20**, the output posted by the decider on OWL-344, each probe as its stream-json log gave it, the files then looked for on the host. Passing on both systems: Claude Code started and finished under the read-only profile (`permissionMode` `dontAsk`, a valid result), reads and the three commands allowed, every write that reached the host refused, the network reachable as accepted.
      - macOS 26.6.2 (arm64), Claude Code 2.1.302, `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec` with the generated read-only Seatbelt profile, the environment and folders above:
        - read a file (`cat README.md`): allowed, `hello`;
        - `git log --oneline -5`: allowed, exit 0, after the Xcode shim's warning `couldn't create cache file …/T/xcrun_db-…`, the shared temporary folder being closed;
        - `git blame README.md`: allowed, exit 0, after the same warning;
        - `grep -rn hello README.md`: allowed, `README.md:1:hello`;
        - a write in the worktree: refused, `operation not permitted`;
        - a write in `.git` by a redirection: refused by Claude Code itself before the sandbox (`dontAsk` protects `.git` paths: "Permission to use Bash has been denied");
        - a write in `.git` by `git update-ref refs/heads/probe-ref HEAD`: refused by the sandbox, exit 128, `Unable to create '…/.git/refs/heads/probe-ref.lock': Operation not permitted`;
        - a write in `.git` by `touch` through `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`: refused, `Operation not permitted`;
        - a write outside, in the test's folder beside the worktree: refused, `operation not permitted`;
        - a write outside, in the home: refused, `operation not permitted`;
        - the network (`curl https://example.com`): reachable, 200;
        - on the host, none of the probe files exists.
      - Linux: Ubuntu 24.04.4 in a container (Docker Desktop on macOS, linux/aarch64), not a desktop Linux; bubblewrap 0.9.0, Claude Code 2.1.302, an unprivileged user, the container run with `--security-opt seccomp=unconfined --security-opt systempaths=unconfined`, the token on a tmpfs file read by the test; `/usr/bin/bwrap` with the generated arguments, the environment and folders above:
        - read a file: allowed, `hello`;
        - `git log --oneline -5`: allowed, exit 0;
        - `git blame README.md`: allowed, exit 0;
        - `grep -rn hello README.md`: allowed, `README.md:1:hello`;
        - a write in the worktree: refused, `Read-only file system`;
        - a write in `.git` by a redirection: refused by Claude Code itself, as on macOS;
        - a write in `.git` by `git update-ref`: refused, exit 128, `Read-only file system`;
        - a write in `.git` by `touch` through `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`: refused, `Read-only file system`;
        - a write in the test's folder under `/tmp` and one in the closed home (`/home/owl`): succeeded inside the sandbox, nothing on the host: they landed in bwrap's private `/tmp` and in the tmpfs over the closed home, which vanish with the run, as the profile intends (`executor_live.rs` documents the same);
        - a write in `/outside`, a folder the user can write outside every private one: refused, `Read-only file system`;
        - the network: reachable, 200;
        - on the host, none of the probe files exists.
    - **Rerun as a live test on 2026-10-20** (OWL-386): `OWLSHIFT_LIVE_CLAUDE=1 cargo test -p owlshift-testkit --test executor_live -- --ignored --nocapture the_resolvers_bash_reads_and_never_writes`, to run again after each Claude Code update. It runs `ClaudeHarness` with `bash: true`, the one field that makes the resolver's harness (`do_cmd::resolver_router`), on `sonnet` at effort `low`, through `Executor::run` with `AgentEnv::from_runner` and the agent-run token, on a read-only Verify brief, the prompt naming the run's own brief file as the resolver's does. It asserts the spawned argv's `dontAsk`, `--allowedTools Bash` alone, no `Edit(` rule and the web tools refused, and the `init` event's mode and tools. Then come the probes above, each one Bash call matched on its exact command, a missing or repeated call inconclusive, a write's refusal taken only from the sandbox's own error (not the Xcode shim's cache warning, which every `git` call prints), every probe file looked for on the host; `env` with no `ANTHROPIC_` variable and neither the token nor the log's redaction marker; the network printed, not judged. Added: the brief read with the Read tool and with `cat`, at least one giving back its title, which nothing but the brief holds.
      - macOS 26.6.2 (arm64), Claude Code 2.1.302, `claude-sonnet-5-5`: passing, every probe as in the hand run above; the redirection into `.git` refused by Claude Code, the other writes by the sandbox (`operation not permitted`; `git update-ref` unable to create its lock), the network 200. The brief: the Read tool refused by Claude Code ("Permission to use Read has been denied because Claude Code is running in don't ask mode"), `cat` allowed, the title given back.
      - Linux: not run by the test yet; its Linux branches (the private `/tmp` and home, `OWLSHIFT_LIVE_OUTSIDE_DIR`) are the OWL-346 test's.
    - What remains: in the run's own files, outside the working directory, the brief is refused to Claude Code's Read tool under `dontAsk` and read by Bash's `cat` (OWL-386, above), so a resolver reads it through Bash. `roles/resolver.md`, the `facts` level and the `fact` basis in `vocab.rs`, and the schemas made from them said the resolver had no network and wrote `result.json`, "offline": aligned by OWL-385 (a fact is checked in the repository and its history; the open network establishes none; the result is the last message).
- **Reversal** (OWL-146, decided 2026-10-11): replying to a DECISION comment reverses it (scenario S7). Before delivery, the next run reads the reply over the decision, and a reply to the resolver's plan approval or residue decision returns the ticket to its author stage first (OWL-345); after it, watch starts a correction run (architecture section 5, "A reversed decision"; "Watch after delivery", OWL-146, above). Checked live on 2026-10-11 against the Owlshift workspace: `issue(id: "OWL-146") { comments { nodes { id parent { id } } } }` answered `"parent": null` for a top-level comment; `commentCreate(input: { issueId: "OWL-146", parentId: "5d06b27d-…", body: "[test] …" })` answered `success: true` with `parent { id: "5d06b27d-…" }`; `issue.comments` then listed the reply with that parent; `commentDelete` answered `success: true`, and the list was back to its one comment.
- **One DECISION comment per run** (OWL-351, decided 2026-10-19). The 2026-10-18 drain posted 43 DECISION comments on 9 tickets, one per decision, 10 of them between two PLAN comments on OWL-336, each repeating its question, decision, basis, reversal and level. The maintainer reads every thread Owlshift writes; they wanted one short comment to scan.
  - *The comment.* One per resolver run that keeps decisions: the header; a lead line saying how many questions were decided without the decider and the delegation level that allowed them, with a link to the decisions document; one line per decision, `**Dn · <question>** — <choice>. <reason>`, the question cut to 60 characters, the choice the option taken (`B. <label>`) or the decision cut to 80, the reason the basis cut to 150, each on a word boundary, and `(on judgment)` for a `judgment` basis; then one sentence on how to reverse; then the footer, unchanged. Three decisions hold in about 1,000 characters, the footer aside. The resolver's prompt asks the basis to open with a reason of at most 150 characters.
  - *The reasoning.* On a tracker with documents (`Capability::Documents`), the document "Decisions · <ticket>", beside the plan's (one issue holds both: checked below), is rewritten whole from the ticket ref before each such comment is posted, so that the comment links to it: under `## Decisions`, one `### <time>` group per DECISION comment, each decision `#### Dn · <question>` with its context, options and recommendation, the decision, what settles it and its kind, the level named once. Its id is kept in `questions.json` format 15 (`decisions_document`). It records the decisions as taken: a reply reversing one is on the thread and in the runs' briefs, not in the document. A document that cannot be published is a warning, and the comment then carries the reasoning folded, as it does on a tracker without documents: Linear's collapsible section, `+++ Reasoning` to `+++`, whole questions and decisions inside, held to the comment's cap. The plan's document is not touched (a section of it was set aside: it is rewritten only with a PLAN, and a ticket before its first PLAN would always fold).
  - *Reversal.* A decider reply threaded under the comment reverses the decisions it names: a word `Dn`, or `Qn` taken as `Dn`, any case, anywhere in the reply, `n` among the comment's numbers (`owlshift_core::reply::named_decisions`); a reply naming none of them reverses every decision of the comment. `Qn` is accepted because a decider answers rounds that way; the cost is that a reply under a DECISION comment speaking of a round's `Q2` names D2. Each kept decision keeps its number (`KeptDecision::number`, format 15; absent on one kept before, which is D1 of a comment of its own), so `reversal::reversals`, the return before delivery, the correction run's brief (each decision's reply opening with the runner's line naming its number) and the thread (a decision whole when a reply names it) read the same replies. The plan's approval is the kept decision on the approval question whose comment is `plan.approval.comment`, no longer any decision of that comment.
  - *Checked live* on 2026-10-19 by the orchestrator, in a terminal, with the maintainer's personal API key (not the app token), on the canceled test ticket OWL-271: a comment whose body held `+++ Reasoning, folded`, a paragraph and `+++` came back with `body` unchanged and `bodyData` holding a `collapsible_section` node, its `collapsible_heading` "Reasoning, folded" and the paragraph inside, the text after the closing `+++` outside it (comment `6bbc0a4a-…`); two `documentCreate` on the same issue both answered `success` (`c836777d-…`, `f7e25c0f-…`), and `issue.documents` listed both. The three stay on OWL-271.
  - *What remains.* Build reads a reply naming two decisions with different instructions from the decisions' order in its thread, which shows each one it names whole, the brief carrying no number (its format is unchanged). A comment that fails to post after the document was published leaves that run's decisions in the document until the next resolver run rewrites it, and a document made by that run is not kept, so the next one makes another. `roles/build.md` still speaks of replies "to the DECISION comments", which stays true.
- **Who runs it.** `do` and `continue`, and in the scenarios the stand-in driver's `resolve` step, with the same pieces and without the bound, which is per command. `crates/owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand.rs` covers a decision and a round of the rest through to a delivery, a run whose questions are all decided going on without a stop and continued after a usage limit, a resolver at its usage limit, a refused DECISION, the bound and a breach, a decision the resolver labels `data_loss` refused beside one that stands (OWL-144), and a question that reopens a refused choice sent to the decider beside one the resolver decides (OWL-222); `tests/scenarios/resolver.toml` plays a decision beside a project-added always-human category, then a run decided whole.

**Build takes no decision of its own** (OWL-176, decided 2026-10-07), architecture section 4's "Resolver first". On the P2 exit gate, OWL-174 listed three open points "to be decided on the ticket before building"; its Build run decided all three, on bases such as "an example is most useful when it documents the options", and the delivery report listed them among the decisions taken without a human. The prompt let Build "decide what can be discovered", a looser bar than the resolver's, and a Build decision met neither the routing by category nor the second label, so the floor never applied.

- **The role.** `roles/build.md`: how the work is built is Build's (code structure, names, tests), and the pull request's body says what a reviewer should know. A choice of what the work delivers (its content, behaviour, interface or wording) that the ticket, the thread, the rules or the repository do not plainly settle, and every point the ticket leaves open, is a question, filed under `scope` when its answer changes what the ticket delivers. Following the decider's word, a `decision` entry or a rule is not a decision, and is listed nowhere.
- **The runner.** `RunResult::validate_against` refuses a Build `result.json` that lists `decisions`, whatever its status (`result.json` format 6; the field stays in the contract, which no role fills today). The refusal is a failed run, so nothing of it is accepted or delivered, while its commits stay on the branch; the next Build run of the command is told why (`result_refusal`, "A refused result" below): ask each choice as a question, and leave them open even though the work is committed. A retry that lists decisions again, or a `blocked` run that lists any, is one more failed run, and a second one parks the ticket; since OWL-186, so is a retry that ends `done` ("A retry after refused decisions asks" below). The resolver is then the only one that decides without the decider, and the delivery report's decisions are its kept ones and the runner's note of late comments given to a run (OWL-139).
- **What remains.** The runner sees only what a run reports: a choice a run makes without recording or asking it goes unseen, and the reviewer reads the diff. A retry that drops its refused decisions can no longer end `done` (OWL-186, below), nor ask some and drop others (OWL-193, below). Telling a choice of what the work delivers from one of how it is built stays a model's judgment. A run that would have recorded a choice now stops to ask it, at the cost of a resolver run and a Build run that asks.
- **Rejected.** The prompt alone: nothing outside the run checks it, and OWL-174's run ignored the ticket's own instruction. The resolver reviewing Build's recorded decisions before delivery: a resolver mode to confirm a choice already built, a step between the gate and delivery, a resolver run on every delivery with decisions, and the points asked after the build. A category on each Build decision, floor-labelled ones refused: the label is the run's own, with a reason to choose another.
- **Tests.** `tests/scenarios/open_points.toml` is the acceptance: a decider's ticket lists two open points; a Build run that decides them and is `done` is refused before the gate, with nothing posted; its retry, told why, asks both, and both reach the decider as round 1: whether the example shows every key, under `scope`, and, since OWL-222, where the README lists the example, which the resolver decided before. `decisions_against_the_brief` (`crates/owlshift-contracts/tests/contracts.rs`) refuses a Build result with a decision under every status and passes one without.

**The Claude Code harness adapter** (OWL-14), in `owlshift-adapters::harness::claude`, runs one role with `claude -p` on the user's own login, following the OWL-14 results above.

- **Split with the executor.** `command` builds the invocation for a request (working directory, model, effort, permissions, result path, an optional JSON Schema, an optional dollar cap passed as `--max-budget-usd`, from the personal file's `budget_usd`: a dollar cap on each run of a harness, not on a day's total, whatever the billing, [runtime and operations](runtime-and-operations.md#configuration)); the executor spawns it in its own process group or Job Object and hands the child to `drive`, which writes the prompt on standard input, streams every output line to a callback (the executor's run log) and returns the run. The executor stops a run by stopping the process group; `drive` returns at most two seconds after the child exits, even when a process it started holds the output open.
- **Permissions.** Read-only is `dontAsk` plus an allow rule for the result file alone, anchored at the working directory, `Edit(/<result path>)`, so that a Bash `cd` neither refuses the result nor allows a file of the same name below (OWL-323, under C1 in the results above); write in worktree is `bypassPermissions`, bounded by the sandbox and the isolation check rather than by the CLI, and a completed run whose `init` reports another mode fails (OWL-346, under C1); no network removes the web tools; a browser is refused. A result path that would be a pattern in a rule (anything but ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-` and `/`, or a `.` segment) is refused. C1's guardrail flags are always passed, and no settings file applies, the worktree's own `.claude/settings*.json` included (OWL-53); the agent environment (OWL-22 in the results above) covers what lies outside Claude Code. `Run::mcp_servers` names the servers of the `init` event.
- **Outcome.** Completed needs exit status 0 and a final record with `is_error: false`. Otherwise a usage limit still in force at the end of the stream (a `rate_limit_event` with status `rejected`, or an assistant message with `error: "rate_limit"`, not lifted by a later `allowed` report) makes the run a usage limit, with the reset time and window of the rejection when there was one, never read from the message text. Anything else is a failure: no final record, no `is_error` flag, a signal, the prompt not delivered, or an error with its API status, terminal reason and error kind. Completed means the harness finished; the role's `result.json` is still validated by the executor.
- **Usage.** Tokens, cost estimate, duration and turns come from the final record, per model too; a run without one has unknown usage, not zero. The last rate-limit report gives the window, its reset time and the five-hour and seven-day utilisation. `apiKeySource: none` means no API key: the run logged in, with the subscription token unless a gateway or provider variable reached it, which the agent environment prevents (OWL-120); any other value fails the run.
- **Tests.** Contract tests replay the scrubbed recordings in `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/fixtures/claude/` in CI; the usage-limit one is constructed from C7's shapes until a real limit is recorded. A tiny real run is an ignored test, run by hand with `OWLSHIFT_LIVE_CLAUDE=1`.

**The executor** (OWL-15), in `owlshift-runner::executor`, turns one role into one run: `Executor::run` takes a `RunSpec` (main checkout, worktree, branch, base, run directory, brief) and a `Harness`.

- **Worktree and brief.** The ticket's worktree is created on its branch from the base, resolved to a commit first, or reused when it is the root of a linked worktree of the main checkout's repository (the same common git directory, and a top level of its own, not the main checkout's; paths compared once resolved), on that branch, at the commit the branch has there. Anything else refuses the run: a worktree on another branch, a separate clone on the right branch and commit, the main checkout itself. The branch name must be one git takes as is (`check-ref-format --branch`, no leading `-`). The run's own files live in `.owlshift/run/` in the worktree, kept out of git by a `.gitignore` of `*` in that folder, which touches nothing the worktrees share (`info/exclude` is shared): the brief, whose `result_path` the executor sets to `.owlshift/run/result.json`, the result, and the build role's plan and ledger. An earlier agent could write there, so nothing is written through a link: `.owlshift` and `.owlshift/run` must be real directories, each file is removed and then created anew, and the previous result is removed. The caller's run directory, outside the worktree, gets `stdout.log`, `stderr.log` and a copy of the brief.
- **The base** (OWL-51, decided 2026-09-29). The isolation check leaves the remote-tracking refs out (below), so a run can move `origin/main`, or make it or `origin/HEAD` point elsewhere, unseen. The next run does not start there: its caller fetches, then passes `RunSpec.base` as a commit resolved after that fetch, as `owlshift do` does (`project::sync_checkout`, see "The project's rules" under OWL-20). The check is not extended to `origin`'s default branch instead: the runner's own fetches for other runs write remote-tracking refs during a run (see "What P6 holds to"), so a moved ref could not be blamed on the run, and the next fetch resets it anyway. One shape survives the fetch: a remote-tracking branch made symbolic, pointing at another branch of origin, which git follows; `sync_checkout` refuses a default branch in that shape. Checked on 2026-09-29 with git 2.54 on a clone of a local bare repository: `fetch --prune` put back a moved and a deleted `refs/remotes/origin/main` and pruned a planted `refs/remotes/origin/evil`, `remote set-head origin --auto` pointed `origin/HEAD` back at `origin/main` from it, but a `refs/remotes/origin/main` made symbolic to `refs/remotes/origin/topic` stayed symbolic, and `rev-parse` then gave `topic`'s commit. The executor takes `RunSpec.base` as a full commit id or as a remote-tracking name looked up as exactly `refs/remotes/<name>` (`show-ref --verify`), then peeled to a commit; an abbreviated id, a local branch name and a revision expression are refused (OWL-66). Checked on 2026-09-29 with git 2.54: `rev-parse origin/main` gave a planted `refs/heads/origin/main`; `rev-parse refs/remotes/origin/main`, with that ref absent, gave a planted `refs/heads/refs/remotes/origin/main`; `show-ref --verify` refused the absent ref and `origin/main~1`; a full commit id resolved to itself despite a branch of that name.
- **Before the spawn.** `AgentEnv::check` runs in the worktree for the forge hosts, and a finding refuses the run. The caller builds the `AgentEnv` with the project's declared variables (`stack.gate_env`, OWL-44) and the operator's `allow_gate_env` (OWL-63), and names the forge hosts (OWL-20). Whatever fails up to the spawn (worktree, run files, credentials, the first snapshot, the command) is an error of `run`: nothing ran, no run is counted.
- **Spawn and stop.** A `Harness` builds its command and reads its own output; the executor sets the working directory, replaces the whole environment with the agent's, pipes the three streams and spawns the command as the root of a `ProcessTree`.
