    - *Roster.* `roster::roster(variant, stage, codex_usable)` gives the seats of the table, the critic first, holding conformance when alone, and on Claude Code as a stand-in without Codex; since OWL-347 `roster(variant, stage)`, every seat on Claude Code. `roster::other_model` picks a reviewer's tier on Claude Code: the deep tier, or the standard tier when the author ran on deep; then the other of the two; then the author's own, flagged `same_model`.
    - *Routing.* `routing::ticket_routing` reads the verdict under the labels' values, which the runner parses (OWL-305), and flags a Codex verdict as not followed. `role_routing` gives each role its layer. `seat_routing` puts the Codex critic on the standard tier and the Claude Code reviewers on `other_model`'s tier, every reviewer on the ticket's effort (architecture section 5, "Per-ticket routing", the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-15). The machine's layer is the roster's stand-in.
    - *Types.* `Effort`, `Routing`, `Severity` and `Mandate` moved to the core's vocabulary, re-exported where the contracts had them, schemas unchanged.
    - *Runner.* The runner's only change is `VisibleStage::after(ReplanRound)`, needs input. Everything else is F's, G's, H's and J's to apply.
- **E · The role prompts.** (OWL-302) After C. `design`, `design_review`, `verify` and `arbiter`, and `build`'s additions (the plan, the findings to answer in author mode, `replan`); each played once by hand on a past Owlshift ticket, the output recorded with the pull request.
  - *As built* (2026-10-15). `roles/design.md`, `roles/design_review.md`, `roles/verify.md` and `roles/arbiter.md`, built into the runner beside the others (`DESIGN_ROLE`, `DESIGN_REVIEW_ROLE`, `VERIFY_ROLE`, `ARBITER_ROLE`) for F and G to run. `roles/build.md` follows a given `plan` step by step, a base newer than the plan's changing nothing by itself, and asks a `replan` question rather than planning around it; it fixes findings in both modes the contract gives Build, `fix` (the Verify fix run) and `answer`, answering the latter in `finding_responses`. `premise_false` from design gives its evidence in `summary`, as Build's does, until I adds the question. The review roles ask no question: what a plan or a diff leaves unclear is a finding. The arbiter rules two findings of one defect alike, the later one's reason naming the earlier, and never rejects one as a duplicate, since each may carry its own severity and criterion. The prompts are harness-neutral: a run that cannot write `result.json` gives it as its last message, as the Codex critic does (B). `tests/roles.rs` pins each prompt against the formats: front matter, the fields and values it writes, the brief fields it reads, and its example validated against a brief of its role. The brief's `diff` is under C's "As built" above. Played on OWL-251 (pull request #238): design at its base, design review (the critic on Codex, conformance on Claude Code), the arbiter on that pass, verify (the critic on Codex) on #238's diff, the arbiter on that pass, and a Verify fix run answering its findings; each result validated against its brief, and the outputs posted on this ticket's pull request.
- **F · Design and Design review in the runner.** (OWL-303) After B, D, E, OWL-131 and OWL-132, so that no Codex run reviews a ticket before its guardrails. The dispatch from Ready to Design, the base fetched there (OWL-274's fast-forward moved) and again at the dispatch to Build; the design run, the PLAN comment and `plan.json`; the roster with its stand-in; the arbiter or the author's responses; the revision loop, its residue round and the pass after it; the approval round, its answer and the design run after it; the Build's plan from the ref; a settled `replan` question sending the ticket back to Design.
  - *As built* (2026-10-16).
    - *The pipeline.* Each command reads the ticket's variant: its `variant:` label (a value naming no variant is a `label_ignored` warning, as `tier:`'s is), else intake's verdict, else `pipeline.default`; nothing stores it. From Ready to delivery the runner follows that pipeline; Watch's paths keep the trivial one, whose moves there are every variant's. One command runs as far as no person is needed: `on_demand/design.rs`'s drive loop takes Design and Design review, hands Build the ticket, and takes it back on a settled `replan`. Entering Build from Design or Design review is a dispatch of its own: the base fetched again, OWL-274's fast-forward, the rules read at that base, a `dispatch` event and a `routing` one. `continue` resumes Design and Design review as it resumes Build.
    - *Design.* The design role runs read-only on the ticket's routing, its brief with the project's always-human additions, as Build's, and `stack.rules`, then its own `[roles.design]` rules (the design review and arbiter briefs likewise take `[roles.design_review]` and `[roles.arbiter]`, accepted from OWL-318, and the Verify reviewers' briefs `[roles.verify]`, from OWL-321). Its questions go through the resolver; the resolver's settled runs count against one bound per command across Build and Design. Its plan is kept in `plan.json`, unapproved, with the hash of the ticket's text and the branch head it read as its base, and the findings its brief gave are marked fixed, or answered, on the pass that raised them. What a design run is given is read back from the state's review passes and the latest Design review pass in `review.json`, settled again by the core, with no format change: after a pass that asked a revision, its findings (the confirmed ones to fix, or every one to answer in author mode); after a passed pass, the approval round, while the plan gate still requires it, so a variant changed during the wait does not make an approval the core refuses; after a residue round, the answers in its thread; before any pass of the loop, nothing but the kept plan, which every design run gets when one is kept, a revision, a `replan` or a dispatch again from Ready alike.
    - *Design review.* The roster's seats run one after the other on `seat_routing`'s routing, then the arbiter, on its role's routing, when the pass has findings and the project confirms them so. Codex runs the critic when the command finds it usable (`codex_machine::codex_critic`: `codex` on the `PATH`, its `auth.json`, and no `/etc/codex/requirements.toml` that refuses `danger-full-access` or cannot be read); otherwise, or when the Codex harness's own check fails at the command's start (a `critic_stands_in` warning), the critic stands in on Claude Code, named in the PLAN comment and in `review.json`. Since OWL-347 every seat runs on Claude Code and nothing looks for Codex. Findings are numbered `F1..Fn` across the seats. A refused design, review or arbiter result is a failed run, as intake's is: those briefs carry no refused result to fix. A failed seat or arbiter run runs again, the pass's other findings kept, and the stage's failed-run bound parks the ticket at Design review whichever runs failed; a usage limit stops the command, and `continue` runs the whole pass again.
    - *Author mode.* An author's rejection settles that finding: it is listed with its reason and never carried into a later brief. The runner does not match a later pass's findings against it: nothing in the briefs lets a reviewer see earlier passes, and a claim's text is no key. After a last pass, author mode settles on all of its findings, the author having answered none, as the core's `settle` does.
    - *The PLAN comment* (`[owlshift] PLAN`, a new marker kind, footer format 1 kept) is posted when a pass ends without asking a revision: before Build, before the approval round, before the residue round. It shows the plan whole, flattened and cut to one comment, the reviewers of the pass with their models, the findings left (asked, noted, or not asked in the round), and the rejections of the loop's passes with their reasons. A PLAN post that fails stops the command before the pass is kept, as a QUESTIONS post that fails keeps nothing, so `continue` runs the pass again. Since OWL-350 (part Q below), on a tracker with documents the plan and its review log go in the ticket's document and the PLAN comment points to it.
    - *The rounds.* Both go to the decider without the resolver (since OWL-342, through the resolver first, the approval only under `on-fork`: "Every question through the resolver" above). The residue round asks one question per finding (category `review_finding`), blockers first, at most a round's 64 questions, each cut to hold in the round's bytes; a finding past the 64th keeps no outcome, is listed as not asked, and the pass after the round reviews the plan whole, which a case the design did not foresee leaves it to. The approval round asks one question (category `plan_approval`): approve the plan as it is, or each fork's other options, cut to hold in a question's bytes, then one option per fork pointing at the PLAN comment. An `approved` answer keeps the decider's newest comment and the plan's hash in `plan.json`, and the core's gate takes the ticket to Build.
    - *Build and `replan`.* Build's brief carries the kept plan and its base in a pipeline with Design. A Build question marked `replan`, on such a pipeline, opens a `ReplanRound` when a round opens, or, settled by the resolver, a `ReplanDecided` back to Design in the same command; on an approved plan it goes to the decider whatever its category.
    - *Events.* `decision` events with `review` = `pass` (outcome, next step, each reviewer's harness, tier, effort, model, stand-in until OWL-347, and findings), `ruling` and `residue`; `plan` = `kept` or `approved`; `replan` = `decided`. Each of a run's `RunReport::warnings`, such as a Codex login not written back, is a `warning` event (`run_warning`).
    - *Tests.* Unit tests on the variant label, the Codex router and `codex_critic`, the PLAN comment, the pass's numbering and settling, what a design run is given, the two rounds' questions and their bounds. On the bench (`tests/on_demand/design_scenarios.rs`): a clean `standard` pass to delivery with the stand-in, Codex and the arbiter confirming one finding and rejecting another, author mode with an unanswered finding refused, the residue round and the pass it buys, a `risky` approval given while the base moves and one that changes the plan, a `replan` decided and one on an approved plan, a `variant:trivial` label, a reviewer parking the ticket.
- **G · Verify in the runner.** (OWL-304) After F, whose on-demand path it shares. The roster on the diff after the runner's gate and the late-comment re-read, before the push; confirmation; the Verify fix loop; the residue as follow-ups through P4's proposals, or as a question round at Verify for a `blocker` or a finding on a criterion, and the pass after it; the DELIVERY report's new lines.
  - *As built* (2026-10-16).
    - *The pass.* Every variant runs it, `trivial` included, once Build's gate passed and the late-comment re-read is done: `on_demand/verify.rs` runs the pass Design review runs, shared since (`run_review_pass`: the roster's seats on their routing, the Codex critic or its stand-in until OWL-347, the arbiter, the core's settling, the `pass`, `ruling` and `residue` events, `stage` `verify`). The diff's base is the merge base of the base the command fetched and the Build's commit: the commit the Build started from on every path, a `continue` included, which does not fast-forward the branch. Before each pass the runner writes `git diff --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-color` of that range to `.owlshift/run/diff.patch` in the worktree, out of git. Verify reviewers take `stack.rules`, then `[roles.verify]` (OWL-321); the arbiter takes `[roles.arbiter]`, and a Verify fix run, a Build run, `[roles.build]`.
    - *The fix loop and the residue.* A Verify fix run is the build role given the latest Verify pass's findings, worked out again from the state's passes and `review.json` as a design run's are: the confirmed ones to fix, or every one to answer in author mode; after a residue round, nothing, the answers being in its thread. They are marked fixed or answered once its result is accepted. A pass that asks a revision loops back within the same Build, which keeps its one late-comment run per delivery. The residue round's questions are F's, asking how the change settles each finding. Every finding the core proposes becomes a follow-up at its pass, a pass that also asks included: its claim as title, cut to 120 characters, and as reason, its evidence, what would settle it as when it is done, source `reviewer`; P4's filing takes it with the DELIVERY report.
    - *What the Build gave, and `continue`.* `review.json` keeps the Build Verify reviews (`build`: its gated commit, summary, pull request text, gate, notes and follow-ups, and the newest decider edit its brief held), written when the Build completes, the ticket's ref made then when it has none. The delivery's notes and follow-ups gather there over its Build runs and passes, a `replan` included; it goes once the ticket is delivered or dispatched again from Ready. So `continue` on a ticket cut short or parked at Verify runs the whole pass again and delivers, part F's rule for Design review ("`continue` runs the whole pass again"); a pass that passed before its delivery failed runs again too, in place of its own record. Before a park is restarted or anything kept, `continue` refuses, and `owlshift do` builds the ticket again, when the branch is no longer at the kept commit, or when the decider of the latest ask commented after that Build read the thread, a comment only a Build run follows (OWL-139).
    - *Contracts.* `review.json` stays format 1, unreleased, as OWL-302 kept the brief's: `build`, each pass's `reviewers` (those that found nothing included) and a reviewer's `same_model`, which the reports show.
    - *The DELIVERY report.* A delivery's report adds a Verify section, read from `review.json` when it is posted: the passes of the loop the record ends with, back to its pass 1, the reviewers of the last with their model, the stand-in until OWL-347 and the Build's own model when so, the findings rejected with their pass and reason, and those proposed as follow-ups, which "What remains for a human" lists with the Build's.
    - *Tests.* Unit tests on the contract, the fix run's findings, the loop, the follow-ups, the diff file and the report. On the bench (`tests/on_demand/verify_scenarios.rs`): a clean `trivial` pass, the arbiter and a fix run on Codex, author mode, the residue round and its filed follow-up, `continue` after a usage limit, and the refusals that keep a park. Elsewhere on the bench the verify seats review clean unless a scenario plays them (`CleanReview`), and every delivery shows its pass.
- **H · Per-ticket routing.** (OWL-305) After C. The verdict's tier and effort, the labels, the roles that follow them, the verdict's harness shown as not followed on the INTAKE comment, the `routing` events.
  - *As built* (2026-10-15). At every dispatch, `do`'s, `continue`'s, the drain's and Watch's fix, correction and rebase runs alike, the runner reads the ticket's `tier:` and `effort:` labels, the prefix and the value without regard to case, and the verdict from the intake record (none for a ticket intake never ran on: the standard tier, no effort), and resolves them through the core's `ticket_routing`. A value that names no tier or effort, and two labels of one prefix that disagree, are each a `warning` event (`label_ignored`, the label, the reason) and are left out; one value repeated counts once. The dispatch then emits one `decision` event, `routing` = `followed` or `not_followed`, with the command, the verdict, the build role's harness, tier, effort and model, and `tier_from` and `effort_from` (`label`, `verdict` or `default`). It is the ticket's routing: a rebase run's own tier stays its `rebase_run` event's. Build, the fix run and the correction run take it; the rebase role takes no effort, on the fast tier for a trivial conflict's first run and on the ticket's tier after. A tier's model is `[models].<tier>.claude`, the harness's own default when the project names none, but `fast` falls back to `standard` as before. The seam is the runner's `routing::Router`, a harness for a core `RunRouting`, one router per harness family: `OnDemand.build` and `OnDemand.rebase` are routers, `rebase_fast` is gone, and F and G route their roles through it with `role_routing` and `seat_routing`. The INTAKE comment adds "Harness not followed" under a verdict naming Codex. Tests: the labels, the models and the router in `routing.rs`; the INTAKE line in `writer.rs`; on the bench, a Build under a verdict with an `effort:` label and an unknown `tier:` one, a Codex verdict, a fix run and a failing rebase on `tier:` and `effort:` labels.
- **I · The false premise.** (OWL-306) After C, E and F, which runs the design and its rounds. `roles/intake.md` and `roles/design.md` on `premise_false`, accepted from both; the round with the evidence and the two options; the record kept as for any round of their stage.
  - *As built* (OWL-306, 2026-10-16). `on_demand/intake.rs` no longer refuses `premise_false`; it and `design_run` send a `premise_false` that asks to `resolve`, which gives every question to the decider, then to `ask`, the round returning to Intake or Design; the intake round keeps no `intake.json`, the contract giving `intake` with `done` and `questions` only (a record kept by an earlier intake round stays). One that asks nothing parks the ticket (`Event::Blocked`) through `OnDemand::park_false_premise`, which the fix run's park shares. The role prompts ask for the evidence and two options (a re-scope, a closure) and tell a re-run what to do with the answer: the re-scope is worked from; the closure, the decider's act in the tracker, ends `blocked`, so the ticket parks again until it is closed, which the runner does not enforce. `scenario.rs`'s stand-in still applies `Questions` to a `premise_false` that asks nothing, where the runner parks.
- **J · Review comments on the pull request.** (OWL-307) After G, then check (c). The forge's read of reviews and their comments, for each followed pull request at a pass with no repair due, one more request each; `[forge].reviewers` with the decider rule and doctor's line; the loop back to Build at Watch in the same pass, one Build on the delivered branch for every review due, Verify, the push and the DELIVERY report; the answered ids; the following kept while the ticket is at Build or Verify; its questions.
  - *As built* (2026-10-16). The rules are architecture section 5's "Review comments on the pull request" and its "As built" notes; the maintainer narrowed a context review to the review loop's runs that day.
    - *Adapter.* `GitHubForge::reviews` (`query Reviews`) and `Capability::ReadReviewComments`; check (c)'s recording replayed by `tests/github.rs`.
    - *Contracts.* Unreleased formats kept: `delivery.json` 5 gains `delivered_at`, refused in format 4, and a review loop's owed report may be `unchanged` and keep notes, counting 1; `review.json` 1 gains `answering`, the reviews of the loop under way, each once, none answered, each as a brief carries it.
    - *Core.* `FollowingEnded` from Build or Verify, working, waiting or parked there, to Watch; the drain's slot is `holds_slot`'s, unchanged.
    - *Runner.* `pr_reviews` is the due rule, pure; `on_demand/review_loop.rs` starts the loop (`review_seen` for watch, `continue` at Watch), reconciles a loop's pull request at `continue`, runs its Build again when its Verify cannot resume, and ends it unchanged; its push is `push_repair`'s, `RepairKind::Review`; Verify's diff starts at the delivered commit; watch reads the reviews after the correction, through `Following::reviews` (a `reviews_unreadable` warning once per kind), holds a loop as a continue, and ends or adopts a loop under the lock (`close_loop`). Events: `decision` with `watch` = `review` and `outcome` = `loop`, `rebuild` or `unchanged`, the decider's ids under `instructions` and the others under `context`; an ended loop's `ended` event carries `review_loop`. The DELIVERY report leads with "Changed after reviews on the pull request", or "No change after reviews on the pull request", each review linked with its author. `roles/build.md` says how a Build reads `pull_request_reviews`. Doctor: the `forge reviewers` line.
    - *Tests.* Unit tests on the due rule, the cut, the lead lines, `changed` for an ended loop and an adopted push, the core's loop, the contracts and doctor's line. On the bench (`tests/on_demand/review_scenarios.rs`): a loop with the decider's, a context and an unlisted review, then a second loop by `continue` whose Verify passes count from 1; a Build that changes nothing; a question round at Build answered under watch; a merge ending a loop that waits at Build.
- **K · Secrets per project.** (OWL-308) The keychain accounts named per project, so a machine serves Owlshift's workspace and Locary's at once; `init` and `--replace-secrets` per project; existing secrets read under their old account until replaced; doctor's lines. Decided on 2026-10-15 (OWL-308), with the maintainer for the first two points:
  - *What names a project.* Its GitHub repository, the owner and name of `origin` as `do` reads them, which already name its folder in the data directory. Each tracker and forge secret is kept under service `owlshift`, account `github.com/<owner>/<repo>/<name>` in lower case (GitHub names ignore case), where `<name>` is the shared account's: `linear`, `linear-app-client-id`, `linear-app-client-secret` and `github` (and, since OWL-336, `github-app` for the GitHub App's token), so `github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/linear`. No `owlshift.toml` key. Two repositories in one Linear workspace store the key twice, and a renamed repository runs `owlshift init` again. Rejected: the tracker's team or workspace, which a GitHub token does not follow and a Markdown project has none of; a name in the project file, which every developer shares.
  - *The Linear app goes with the key.* The app's client ID and secret are the project's too, since an app belongs to one workspace. The tracker set, the key and the app's pair, is read whole from one place: the project's accounts when any of the three is stored there, otherwise the shared ones when any of those is, otherwise the project's, which then hold nothing. So a project with its own key and no app of its own writes through its key, never through another workspace's app. The GitHub token is resolved the same way, on its own; since OWL-336 that is the GitHub credential, by the order of "`init`", "The GitHub sign-in" (P1). Each account is read at most once per command.
  - *The shared accounts.* The accounts every project of a machine used before OWL-308 are still read for a project that has none of its own, so an existing install keeps working, and Owlshift never deletes them. `do`, `continue` and `watch` say on their error output, once, which set comes from a shared account, naming the accounts and the repository, never a value. The Claude Code token for agent runs (`claude-agent`) stays one per machine: a model login, not a tracker or forge secret.
  - *`init`.* It stores under the project's accounts, read from `origin`; it asks for a project's secret even when a shared one is in use, and an empty answer keeps the shared one. `--replace-secrets` replaces the project's. The app's pair is asked once the project has its own key, saying that a shared app is not used with it. Without a GitHub `origin`, `init` stores the Claude Code token, names the project's secrets as not stored and why, and exits 1.
  - *`doctor`.* A `secrets` line names the account each tracker and forge secret is read from: a warning, which does not block, when one is shared, whose fix is `owlshift init --replace-secrets` in the repository; a failure without a GitHub credential (since OWL-336 an app token or a personal token, the line naming which the project uses), as `do` refuses then, without a GitHub `origin`, or when the keychain cannot be read. The tracker lines name the resolved accounts, and the advice to delete the app's two entries to write through the key is given for the project's own entries only, never for shared ones another project may use.
  - *The platforms.* The account names hold `/` and `.`: the Windows store (`windows-native-keyring-store` 1.1.0) checks their length only, at most 513 characters for the user name, and the Secret Service keeps them as attribute strings; a name here is at most about 170 characters. Read on 2026-10-15 in the crates' sources; the tests use the in-memory store on the three platforms, and the round trip through the macOS Keychain on such a name is a live check (`system_keychain_round_trip`, ignored).
  - *Since OWL-336 (2026-10-20).* The "GitHub token" of the points above, decided on 2026-10-15, is now the expert option's personal token, under `github`. The forge's credential is the user token of Owlshift's GitHub App, under the project's `github-app` account, which `init` writes and no shared account holds ("`init`", "The GitHub sign-in", P1). The rest of this part stands.
- **L · Locary's adoption.** (OWL-309) After K, then check (d). Locary's `owlshift.toml` (team `LOC`, its states, `admitters`, `[forge].reviewers`, `stack.gate`, `stack.rules` naming `CLAUDE.md` and its rules), proposed to Locary through its own process; its secrets through K; the Linear app of D8 on its workspace or the key with doctor's warning; `owlshift doctor` clean on it.
  - *`owlshift gate`* (OWL-329, 2026-10-17). Locary's gate took four Build rounds on LOC-1407 to get right.
    `gate_once` tries a gate without a Build run: version, lock, marker and confinement as a Build's
    preparation, then the dedicated checkout synced and a fresh worktree on `owlshift-gate/check`.
    The commit is origin's default branch, or `--at` (a full id or `origin/<branch>`, read as a run's base).
    The gate comes from the project file at that commit, or the operator's when it has none, as OWL-331 reads a Build's.
    `gate_env` and `allow_gate_env` come from the loaded configuration; names that differ are noted, as a Build warns.
    `Executor::run_gate` shares `project_gate` with a Build's gate: same `RunPaths`, temporary folder,
    credential check, snapshot and guard. A breach writes the `unverified` marker and keeps the worktree.
    Otherwise the worktree is removed from the file system, then pruned, and its branch is deleted.
    *Temporary folder, as a Build's gate has it:* one run folder for every command (`RunPaths::temp`).
    Under bwrap a folder in `/tmp` is made anew and empty for each command; on macOS commands share it.
    Native Windows is refused (D9).
    *Checked on Locary, 2026-10-17* (debug binary at 16e62a3, macOS):
    - `--at 4c5d3f82` (`make lint`, `make test`): red, `make lint`: exit status 2, in 0.5 s.
    - `--at 302a7ca5` (`ci/owlshift/gate.sh` setup, lint, test): all three pass, then red on
      "the gate changed files: scripts/__pycache__/*.pyc". A Build there is red the same way;
      LOC-1407 passed because its Build ignored `scripts/__pycache__` (squashed into be3b17dc).
    - `--at be3b17dc` (the same gate with that ignore): green, 3 commands in 757.0 s, exit 0.
      Run from a clean clone: the operator's owlshift.toml held a field this binary refuses.
- **M · The scenarios.** (OWL-310) After F, G, H, I, J and OWL-32. S8, S9's findings and review passes, S17's routing part, each variant walked end to end, plan approval given and refused, a `replan`, and a review loop, played by the test bench on the Markdown tracker with the fake harness and the bench's fake GitHub, as acceptance.
  - *As built* (OWL-310, 2026-10-16). Played as part K played P4: in Rust, on the runner's own `owlshift watch --drain` (`Watch::run_drain`), in `owlshift-testkit/tests/on_demand/pipeline_scenarios.rs`, a module of `on_demand.rs` whose bench and helpers it shares with parts F, G and J's files. The drain dispatches, watch continues every answer, and no command is typed. Six tests, each asserting the runs of every pass:
    - *S8* (`scenario_s8_a_false_premise_at_intake_is_rescoped`, `scenario_s8_a_false_premise_at_design_is_closed`). Intake finds the premise false and asks to re-scope or close: needs input, no intake record, nothing built or pushed. Watch continues the re-scope; intake runs again with it in its thread, and the drain dispatches the ticket in that same pass, since watch's continue runs before the drain reads the column. At Design, the closure answered makes the design run end `blocked`: the ticket parks at Design, with no plan, no Build and no dispatch after the park. That pins the runner's park on `blocked`, not the closure, which is the decider's act in the tracker.
    - *S9's findings and review passes* (`scenario_s9_findings_and_review_passes`), on a `standard` ticket with Codex.
      - At Design review, the arbiter confirms the critic's finding, the design fixes it, and the next pass is clean.
      - At Verify, pass 1's confirmed finding is fixed and its rejected one listed. Pass 2 leaves a `blocker` and a `minor` on a criterion, both asked, and a `minor` off every criterion, proposed.
      - Watch continues the answers: a Verify fix run, the pass the round buys, and the delivery. The off-criterion `minor` alone is filed at `Triage` through the Markdown tracker's inbox, and the DELIVERY report lists the rejection and the filing.
      - The `pass` events read `design`, `build`, `build`, `questions`, `deliver`.
    - *S17's routing part* (`scenario_s17_the_tickets_routing_is_followed`). A verdict names Codex, `deep` and `high`, an `effort:max` label overrides its effort, and the project names a model for `deep` and for `standard`.
      - Design and Build run on Claude Code, `deep`, `max`. Both dispatches tell `not_followed`, the tier from the verdict and the effort from the label.
      - The Codex critic runs on `standard` at `max`, conformance on Claude Code's other model (`standard`, `same_model` false) at `max`, and the arbiter with no effort. Since OWL-347 the critic, at Design review and at Verify, runs on Claude Code's other model too, `standard` at `max`.
      - The INTAKE comment says the harness is not followed.
    - *The `standard` walk and a review loop* (`scenario_a_standard_ticket_walks_to_its_merge_through_a_review_loop`).
      - Intake, then Design, the critic and conformance, the Build on the plan, Verify's critic alone holding conformance, and the pull request.
      - The decider's review sends the ticket back to Build with the plan and the review in its brief, then Verify and a push to the same pull request.
      - Watch reads the merge.
    - *The `risky` walk: approval refused and given, and a `replan`* (`scenario_a_risky_plan_refused_approved_and_replanned`).
      - Three reviewers review the plan, then the approval round. The decider's reply picks the fork's other option: the plan comes back `changed`, gets one pass, and is asked again, with no Build in between.
      - Approved, the Build follows it, then asks a `replan`, which goes to the decider, not the resolver (through the resolver first since OWL-342). Watch continues the answer back to Design: a new plan, its pass, and its approval asked and given.
      - Then the Build on that plan, the two Verify seats, the pull request and the merge read. Each Build's brief holds the plan approved for it, and `plan.json` keeps the last approval.
    - *The `trivial` walk* is part K's: every drained ticket of `drain_scenarios.rs` runs intake, Build, Verify and the delivery, and S4's chain plays the merge.
  - *What the scenarios showed.* No integration defect. A Build entered within watch's continue of an answer (an approval, a Verify round) is dispatched with `command` = `continue`; the drain's own dispatches are those from Ready and the Build that follows it in the same command. No snapshot shows a ticket waiting for the slot of one being designed: one command takes a ticket from Ready to its delivery unless a person is needed, and a ticket waiting for an answer holds no slot. So the slot at Design and Design review is the core's (`schedule.rs`'s tests) and S6's.
  - *Played elsewhere,* part by part through `do` and `continue`:
    - `design_scenarios.rs`: the arbiter's rejection in the PLAN comment, author mode, the residue round at Design review and the pass it buys, a reviewer parking the ticket, the `variant:` label, a `replan` the resolver settles, and a false premise at Design without a question.
    - `verify_scenarios.rs`: author mode, a pass cut short by a usage limit, the parks `continue` refuses, and a pass run again after a failed delivery.
    - `review_scenarios.rs`: context and unlisted reviews, a loop that changes nothing, a loop's question, and a merge ending a loop.
    - `on_demand.rs`: a false premise at intake without a question, and the routing of Build, fix and rebase runs under labels.
    - The core's and the runner's unit tests: settling a pass, the roster and the routing.
  - *Not played.* S8's dated finding, which waits for a key naming the project's facts file. S17's fallback on a usage limit (P6). Browser QA (P6). Codex's own stream, which `tests/codex_fixtures.rs` and `tests/codex_live.rs` cover. S9's red checks, conflicts and fix runs are P3's, played by watch's tests in `on_demand.rs`. The tests run on Linux and macOS; on Windows they are built and linted, not run, as every test of `on_demand.rs` since OWL-241.
- **N · The exit gate** (OWL-311), below. After M, L, OWL-131 and OWL-132.
- **P · Every reviewer on Claude Code.** (OWL-347) Decided by the maintainer on 2026-10-18, after a Codex quota ran out and held four tickets at their design review for more than two hours: the reviews run on Claude Code only, so that no second subscription is required (architecture section 5, "Codex"; D4).
  - *As built* (OWL-347, 2026-10-18). `roster::roster(variant, stage)` takes no Codex parameter and its `Seat` holds mandates only; `routing::seat_routing` becomes `reviewer_routing`, one routing for every seat: Claude Code on `other_model`'s tier. `OnDemand`'s `design_review` and `verify` are one router each, checked with the other routers at the command's start; the command's Codex probe, the `critic_stands_in` warning and the stand-in clause of the PLAN comment, the DELIVERY report and the residue questions are gone. `owlshift do` no longer calls `codex_critic`.
  - *Contracts.* `review.json` stays format 1, unreleased: `Reviewer` drops `stand_in`, and its reading form (`ReviewerForm`, as `TrackerForm` is `Tracker`'s) still accepts the key a file written before OWL-347 holds, ignored, never written and left out of the schemas. A finding of such a file keeps its `harness`, so a reviewer that ran on Codex still reads "on Codex". The brief keeps format 18: a field it no longer carries breaks no reader. The `pass` event drops its `stand_in` key.
  - *Doctor.* With no harness declared in the personal file, `claude` alone is checked; the `codex`, `codex machine config` and `codex requirements` lines show only when `[harnesses.codex]` is declared, a Codex not ready being a warning whose note says no role runs on Codex for now.
  - *Kept for OWL-348, unwired.* The Codex harness adapter and `executor::CodexHarness` with their guards, `routing::CodexRouter`, `routing::codex_model` and `codex_machine::codex_critic`: no command calls them, and their own tests are what keeps them working.
- **Q · The plan as a tracker document.** (OWL-350) On the 2026-10-18 `owlshift watch --drain` run of nine tickets, Owlshift posted 1.2 million characters of comments, most of them 23 PLAN comments of 38,000 to 65,000 characters re-posted at each pass (OWL-160: 38k, 45k, 55k, 57k, 65k), which the maintainer could neither follow nor answer. The rules are architecture section 5, "Kept and shown".
  - *Checked live.* On 2026-10-18 through the personal key, on OWL-160: `documentCreate(input: { issueId, title, content })` made a document listed in `issue.documents`, with a URL, a 64,691-character plan accepted; `documentUpdate(id, { content })` replaced it in place; 250,000 characters is the cap; Linear normalises the Markdown it stores (`[owlshift]` read back `\[owlshift\]`), harmless since no run reads a document back; a `fileUpload` `.md` asset is a raw file behind sign-in, not rendered, so a document is chosen. On 2026-10-19 through the app's client credentials token (scopes `read,write`, `viewer.app` true, workspace `owlshift`), on OWL-350, by a one-off script printing outcomes only: the same create (72,897 characters, `creator.app` true) and update (same id; the final line break stripped), 250,001 characters refused `INVALID_INPUT` and 250,000 taken; a deleted document left the issue's listing, yet `documentUpdate` on it answered `success: true` with `archivedAt` set, and an unknown id answered `Entity not found: Document`, `INPUT_ERROR`; the documents were deleted and the token revoked. Once built, the same day, through Owlshift's own code (`a_plan_document_reaches_linear_through_the_app` in `owlshift-runner/tests/linear_app_live.rs`, run by hand, test issue OWL-358 trashed after, 1 passed): the document made through the app and the Writer, titled "Plan · OWL-358", listed, its creator the app; updated in place, same id; the pointer, 323 characters, read back verbatim with the document's link; in the trash, the document not found.
  - *As built* (OWL-350, 2026-10-19). `Tracker::publish_document(ticket, document, title, content)`, an error by default, declared by `Capability::Documents` (doctor's "ticket documents"), at most `tracker::MAX_DOCUMENT_CHARS`, 250,000, on every tracker. Linear: `documentCreate` with the issue's identifier, or `documentUpdate` of the content alone, through the app; an update answered with `archivedAt` set is `NotFound`. Markdown tracker: `tickets/<ID>/documents/<n>.md`, its title on a `# ` line, `n` one past the highest, the file's path from the repository's root as its link. The Writer publishes under the floor's new `Action::Document`.
  - *The runner.* At each PLAN post (`PlanNext::Build`, `Approval`, `Findings`), on a tracker with documents, `design.rs` publishes `writer::PlanDocument` (a version line, the plan's sections as the full comment shows them, then "Review log", each Design review pass of the loop with its reviewers, the findings left and those rejected), then posts `writer::PlanPointer` (the version and pass, the link, at most three bullets naming the parts changed, the forks and the findings open, the same next step), within `writer::MAX_POINTER_CHARS`, 800, for a link of 120 characters and the longest ticket id. A tracker without documents gets the full comment, unchanged. `plan.json` (format 1, unreleased, a field added) keeps `published`: the document's id and link, the version, the pass, the plan's hash, one SHA-256 per part of the plan, and the change shown (`first`, `changed` with the version before and the parts, or `unchanged`). The same plan at the same pass, a `continue`, reuses the record whole; the same plan at a later pass keeps its version, `unchanged`; another plan is the next version. A design run's new plan keeps the record, the approval binding the plan's hash as before. A document just made is kept in the ref before the comment, so a comment that fails leaves one document; a document deleted by a person is made again. Past the cap, the review log leaves out its oldest passes, said in a line. The approval question and the round summaries say "the plan the PLAN comment shows", true of both comments. Event: `tracker_write` with `action` `document`, `document`, `made` and `version`.
  - *Accepted.* A create Linear took whose answer was lost, or a ref that could not be written right after it, leaves an extra "Plan · OWL-n" that later runs do not update; `owlshift forget` drops `plan.json`, so a forgotten ticket's next plan gets a document of its own; a document made again after a person deleted it leaves the links of older PLAN comments pointing to the deleted one.
- **R · Folding superseded comments.** (OWL-353) On the 2026-10-18 drain, OWL-160 had 13 comments and OWL-336 had 14, most of them earlier PLAN, QUESTIONS and RESUME comments still expanded. The rules are architecture section 5, "Folded when superseded".
  - *Checked live.* On 2026-10-19 through the app's client credentials token (scopes `read,write`, `viewer.app` true, workspace `owlshift` checked first), by two one-off scripts printing outcomes only, each on a throwaway issue made in Backlog through the personal key and trashed after, the token revoked. On OWL-362, with top-level comments A, B, C and a reply R to C posted by the app: `commentResolve(id: A, resolvingCommentId: B)` answered HTTP 200 with `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`, as did a second try with C, `resolvingCommentId` a reply of another thread (R), and the same call through the personal key: the ticket's `resolvingCommentId` set to the superseding comment does not work. `commentResolve(id: A)` alone answered `success: true`, `resolvedAt` set, `resolvingUser.app` true, `editedAt` still null; `commentUnresolve(id: A)` answered `success: true` and cleared `resolvedAt`; resolving the reply R answered `INPUT_ERROR` "Cannot resolve a child comment"; an unknown id `INPUT_ERROR` "Entity not found: Comment". On OWL-363, with two comments by the app, A resolved alone: the adapter's own comments query (`COMMENTS_QUERY`) still listed A, resolved and unedited, through the key and through the app. So a folded comment reads as before, and Owlshift's rule that a comment it never edited is its own holds. Not tried: resolving a comment already resolved.
  - *As built* (OWL-353, 2026-10-19). `Tracker::resolve_comment(comment)`, an error by default, declared by `Capability::FoldComments` (doctor's "fold superseded comments"), optional. Linear: `commentResolve(id)` with no `resolvingCommentId`, through the app when one is set, as every write; the Markdown tracker declares nothing. The selection is `writer::superseded`, pure: of the comments listed before the new one, those that are Owlshift's (marked, the footer naming this ticket and agreeing with the header, never edited, top-level: the rule of "The brief's thread" above with the ticket and the thread added) and that the new one folds by kind and round, less those an Owlshift comment posted in between already folds; a QUESTIONS part other than its round's last counts for no such comment, since its round's fold runs once the round is posted, so the parts of one round fold together. `Writer::fold` asks nothing of a tracker without the capability; otherwise it reads the comments once and resolves each under the floor's `Action::Comment`. The runner folds after the PLAN comment (whole or pointer, `design.rs`), the QUESTIONS round (its last part), the RESUME and a DELIVERY report newly posted (`Writer::post_repair_report` answers whether it posted, so a report found already there folds nothing again). Each resolve is a `tracker_write` event (`action` `resolve`, `comment`, `by` the new comment); a read or a resolve that fails, or a new comment absent from the read, is a `warning` (`what` `comment_not_folded`, `by`, `comment` when one, `reason`), and the command goes on. No marker, footer, ref, brief or event format changed. Accepted: a fold that failed is not tried again; comments posted before this change are not folded; after `owlshift forget`, a round of the number the new one restarts at stays expanded. Tested in `writer::tests` and on the bench (`superseded_comments_fold_where_the_tracker_resolves_them`, the 2-round ticket of `a_changed_plan_gets_one_more_pass_and_is_asked_again` with a stand-in that resolves, one refusal included), the Markdown tracker's own run folding nothing.
- **S · Minor findings and the narrowed pass.** (OWL-356) On the 2026-10-18 drain, minor Verify findings started two more Build, gate and Verify rounds on OWL-160, and Design review ran up to 7 passes on OWL-339. The rules are architecture section 5's "Minor findings", under Design review and under Verify, and "A pass after a revision".
  - *Core.* `review::settle`: at Verify a standing `minor` is `Fate::LastFix`, unless a `blocker` or `major` of a pass before the last is fixed with it (`Fix`); at Design review a `minor` is always `Note`. `PassOutcome::LastFix`, after `Ask` and `Revise`, gives `Event::LastFix`: Verify to Build, the pass ended and counted, valid at a last pass too. `MAX_REVIEW_PASSES` stays 2.
  - *Contracts.* Brief format 21: `review.since` (`pass`; `diff` at Verify; `parts`, `steps` and `removed` at Design review), `review.earlier` (each finding of the loop's earlier passes with its `pass`, as `review.json` keeps it, at most 192, the latest kept), and findings mode `note`, for build only, answered by no `finding_responses`. `review.json` stays format 1, unreleased, as OWL-347 kept it: a pass's `head` (Verify) and `plan` (Design review: the 9 part hashes and one per step), and the record's `last_fix`, the Verify pass whose last fix is under way.
  - *Runner.* `verify_input` gives the Build after a `LastFix` pass, or after the round of a last pass that asked, the minors to fix. `verify_pass` keeps `last_fix` with a `LastFix` pass and, at Verify with `last_fix` at the state's pass count, delivers with no pass (a `review` = `last_fix` event). `last_fix` is dropped when a Build run asks, takes late comments or follows a reply to a DECISION comment, when a pass is kept, and at delivery. `loop_passes` is the one walker of a loop's passes, for the plan's document, `earlier`, `since` and the report. The DELIVERY report lists "Findings given to the last Build". `design_input` gives the design run what it fixes, never a `minor`; the Build after Design review is given the loop's notes, renumbered, mode `note`.
  - *Roles.* `design_review.md` and `verify.md` review `review.since` with `review.earlier` from pass 2; `build.md` weighs `note` findings.
  - *Tests.* The core's settle and transition tables, the contracts' rules, and on the bench: OWL-160's four confirmed minors end with one Build after the first Verify and the delivery, no second pass, the report naming them (`verify_minors_get_one_last_build_and_no_second_pass`, the ticket's first and fourth scenarios in one); a last fix that asks gets a pass told the diff since and the earlier finding (`a_last_fix_that_asks_is_reviewed_on_what_changed_since`); a Design review pass of minors goes to Build with its notes; a pass after a revision gets the changed step and the earlier findings; S9's last Verify pass now asks a `blocker`, proposes a `major` and gives a `minor` on a criterion to the Build after the round.
- **T · Findings to the role that owns the work.** (OWL-377) On the 2026-10-18 and 19 drains, findings two passes left became question rounds through the resolver, and "apply the fix" restarted Design or Build and a new pass: up to 8 design review passes for OWL-339's plan, 3 Verify passes, 28 of 29 replies "ok". The rules are architecture section 5's "A finding goes to its author", designed with S above (OWL-356): S bounds how often a review runs, T sets who owns a finding.
  - *Core.* `review::settle` has no fate that asks or proposes: `Fate::Ask`, `Fate::Propose` and `PassOutcome::Ask` go. At the last pass a standing `blocker` or `major` is `Fate::LastFix` at both stages, a Verify `minor` too, a Design review `minor` stays `Note`; `PassOutcome::LastFix` is valid at Design review, its event `Event::LastFix`, which the state machine now takes at Design review to Design, the pass counted, as at Verify to Build.
  - *Contracts.* `ResponseOutcome` and `FindingOutcome` gain `disagreed` and `deferred`. `result.json` format 13: a run whose brief gives findings to `fix` answers each in `finding_responses` (`fixed`, `disagreed`, or `deferred` for a `minor` with no criterion), in `answer` mode `fixed` or `rejected` as before; a `done` result that leaves one out, defers a `blocker`, a `major` or a finding on a criterion, or uses an outcome of the other mode is refused. Brief format 22: those outcomes reach `review.earlier`. `review.json` stays format 1, unreleased: a confirmed ruling may carry the author's response, its outcome the response's; a Design review pass may name the design run of the last revision (`revision`), with no reviewer, no finding and no plan's print; `last_fix` may name a Design review pass. The `asked` and `proposed` outcomes stay readable.
  - *Runner.* `mark_answered` keeps every response in `fix` mode too. `design_input` gives a last pass's findings to the last revision; `designed()` keeps the plan of a last revision, while the `last_fix` mark names the state's pass, with the reviewer-less pass that names its run, in the same write; `review_pass` on that pass runs no seat, posts the PLAN comment and goes to Build or the approval round, the design run as the round's run; a design run that asks drops the mark. `verify_input` gives a last pass's blockers and majors to the last fix with its minors. The residue rounds go (`residue_questions`, `residue_split`, the `not_asked` lists, `PlanNext::Findings`); the `residue` event keeps `asked`, empty, and lists `noted` and `last_fix`. The PLAN comment and the plan's document list the findings a revision disagreed with and those the last revision answered, with the author's reasons; the DELIVERY report lists the last Build's answers and every finding of the loop disagreed with or deferred. A later pass's `since` starts at the last pass of the loop that kept a plan's print. OWL-345's reversal of residue decisions and the resolver's `resolve[].finding` stay for refs kept before.
  - *Roles.* `design.md` and `build.md` answer the findings to `fix` and ask only a choice a finding uncovers; `design_review.md` and `verify.md` read `disagreed` and `deferred` in `review.earlier`; `resolver.md` gets no finding as a question.
  - *Tests.* The core's settle and transition tables; the contracts' response rules and the review record's; the runner's renderings (a disagreement shows the author's reason, never the ruling's) and inputs, the `since` of the pass after a last revision starting at the last print. On the bench: `two_majors_left_after_two_passes_go_to_the_last_revision_with_no_round` (OWL-377's first acceptance: one fixed, one disagreed with its reason, the plan to Build with no QUESTIONS round, the `residue` event's `asked` empty) and `the_last_revision_goes_to_its_approval_round` (under `always`); `a_finding_the_design_turns_into_a_scope_question_reaches_the_decider` (the second: filed under a word of its own, labelled `scope` by the resolver, refused at `discoverable`, asked of the decider, then the finding answered after the round); `every_verify_finding_goes_to_its_build_and_the_report_lists_what_it_kept` (pass 1's major disagreed with, read in pass 2's `earlier`, pass 2's blocker fixed and its minor deferred by the last Build, no third pass, nothing filed, the DELIVERY report listing both); `scenario_s9_findings_and_review_passes` replayed with no round. The three reversal scenarios of residue decisions seed one in the ref, as a ref kept before OWL-377 holds it.
  - *What remains.* A question filed `scope` at a level that keeps scope goes to the decider with no resolver run (section 4); only a question filed otherwise, which the resolver labels `scope`, reaches it first. The resolver's finding plumbing (`resolve[].finding`, `Asked.findings`) is unused by new rounds, kept for refs written before.

## P5 tasks & exit gate

The tasks are the parts above, OWL-297 to OWL-311 and OWL-347, OWL-350, OWL-353 and OWL-356, in the Linear project "P5 · Quality pipeline", with OWL-32 (after D), OWL-131 and OWL-132 (after B), and the tickets filed in that project since 2026-10-15, OWL-339 among them.

**Exit gate.** Five pull requests judged at least as good as `/parallel` output by the maintainer; first Locary tickets delivered. Run by the maintainer, as P4's was, once M passes:

1. *Before.* Codex logged in and `owlshift doctor` clean on both projects; OWL-131 and OWL-132 merged; Locary adopted (part L).
2. *The tickets.* Five real tickets at least, two of them Locary's, drained by `owlshift watch --drain`, each through the stages of its variant. Among them: one `standard` with a design review, one `risky` whose plan approval was asked and given, one where a finding was confirmed and fixed, and one the maintainer's review on its pull request sent back to Build. A case the backlog does not bring is staged, as P2 staged its round 2.
3. *Judged.* For each pull request, the maintainer compares it with `/parallel`'s output on tickets of like size, on five points: it does what the ticket asks and nothing more; the plan's approach holds; the tests cover the criteria; every finding was fixed or rejected for a stated reason; the review it took the maintainer (comments, fixes after delivery). The verdict, at least as good or not, with one line why, is recorded below. Five at least as good pass the gate; one that is not is recorded, and its cause filed as a ticket.
   - *What is compared* (settled with the maintainer on 2026-10-17). One pull request against one, not the batch. `/parallel`'s throughput, several tickets at once, is P6's and left out, with the checks it owes to running in parallel (a trial merge of sibling branches, breaks between them). Its orchestrator's double check of a pull request is in, since Verify stands for it. Its triage of questions is the resolver's, which this gate does not judge: the questions are recorded (item 4) so that the gap is measured without holding the gate.
4. *Recorded here,* as P4's runs are: per ticket, its variant and routing, runs per stage, minutes and cost; the reviewers that ran, with their models; findings raised, confirmed and rejected, and the review passes; approvals asked and given; the review loops; the questions that reached the maintainer, each with the maintainer's word on whether `/parallel`'s orchestrator would have settled it alone; the follow-ups proposed; and that there was no guardrail breach (agents committed locally only, the runner wrote every comment and push, nothing was merged by Owlshift).

- [x] **Exit gate.** Passed on 2026-10-20 (OWL-311): the ten pull requests below, two of them Locary's, judged at least as good as `/parallel`'s by the maintainer; the review loop staged on OWL-395. Their cost and time are not yet at `/parallel`'s level, which this gate records rather than judges; the causes found are filed (below).

**Runs, 2026-10-16 to 20**, on the maintainer's machine (macOS), Claude Code 2.1.300 for LOC-1407 and 2.1.302 for every other ticket, one `owlshift watch --drain` per project. Every Claude Code run was on Sonnet 5.5, but those below: from 2026-10-19 16:06 UTC, `[models]` named Opus 5.5 for the deep tier, so Design and Build of deep-routed tickets ran on Opus, and a Verify reviewer on Opus where the author ran on Sonnet (OWL-338); rebases and OWL-395's runs ran on Haiku 5.5 (the fast tier). The Codex critic sat in design review and Verify until 2026-10-18 12:57 (LOC-1406's last Verify), when OWL-347 unwired it; Claude reviewers took its seat after that. The maintainer merged each of the ten pull requests. Runs, minutes and cost are summed over each ticket's agent runs, intake, resolver and answer checks included; minutes are `run_ended`'s `wall_ms` (OWL-262), cost the harness's `cost_usd`, which the subscription does not bill.

The cases the gate names: a `standard` ticket with a design review, OWL-341, OWL-345, OWL-340, OWL-338, OWL-330 and LOC-1407; a `risky` ticket whose plan approval was asked and given, OWL-339, OWL-336, OWL-344 and LOC-1406; a finding confirmed and fixed, on every ticket (on OWL-339, Verify pass 5's F1, fixed by the last fix before delivery); a pull request the maintainer's review sent back to Build, OWL-395, staged since the drains did not bring the case (Owlshift's `owlshift.toml` had no `[forge.reviewers]` until #347).

Intake, then a Build of 8.2 min whose gate passed, a Verify pass with no finding, and [#348](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/348) delivered at 08:52 UTC. The maintainer's review, submitted at 08:58:40 ("Use each pull request's title as the link text instead of its URL, and group the list under two subheadings, Locary then Owlshift"), was read at watch's next pass as the decider's instruction: the loop started at 08:59:07, its Build applied both points in 3.8 min, its Verify pass raised one finding against the grouping the review had asked for, which the arbiter rejected, and the change was pushed to #348 at 09:03:51 with a DELIVERY report naming the review. A first comment of the maintainer's on the pull request's conversation was not read, as the design has it: only a submitted review counts. 6 runs, 13.5 min, 0.46 USD. The pull request was closed unmerged once this record was written.
- OWL-160 (resume a ticket a usage limit stopped, at any stage), not delivered: its fifth Build, on 2026-10-18, amended a commit and rebased its branch with `--signoff` over the earlier runs' commits; the runner's isolation check saw the branch no longer descend from the run's start, quarantined the run and pushed nothing. OWL-369 has since stopped a Build from rewriting its branch's history. The maintainer closed OWL-160 on 2026-10-19 with [#323](https://github.com/pitchopp/owlshift/pull/323) (OWL-360). Design review 6 passes, Verify 4; 6 questions to the maintainer in 3 rounds, 10 decided by the resolver; 51 runs, 280 min, 42.32 USD.
- In all, eleven tickets: 567 runs, 1,698 min, 331.57 USD. 92 questions reached the maintainer in 49 rounds; the resolver decided 77, each time on the asking run's recommendation.

**Judged.** The maintainer's verdict, the same for each of the ten delivered pull requests: at least as good as `/parallel`'s output on tickets of like size, "the quality looks good". Ten pass, so the gate passes. What fell short was the cost and the time it took, which the gate's five points do not judge: OWL-330, OWL-339 and OWL-336 took 154 to 275 run minutes and 45 to 81 USD each, and 47 to 58 hours from dispatch to merge.

**The questions.** The maintainer's word, given for the 92 as a whole rather than one by one: `/parallel`'s orchestrator would definitely have settled most of them alone.

**No guardrail breach.** The agents committed locally only; the runner pushed every branch, opened the pull requests and wrote every comment, stage move, decision and proposal; nothing was merged by Owlshift. The one isolation violation, OWL-160's rewritten branch, was caught and contained by the runner, as above. The two `unquarantined` events of 2026-10-19 and 20 cleared markers left by runs a stop of watch cut short, not breaches.

What the runs showed, each with its ticket:

- A question restarts the stage from zero. Of the 1,010 minutes and 172 USD of Design and Build runs measured on 2026-10-20, 532 minutes and 85 USD went to 93 runs that only restarted a stage after a question round, 40 of them after a resolver decision that came within minutes, and 78 of the 98 design review passes followed such a restart: resuming the asking run's session instead (OWL-394).
- The review loop ran up to 14 design review passes (OWL-330) and 5 Verify passes, each pass's residue asked as questions: every design review pass of the gate ran before OWL-377 merged (2026-10-19 22:30 UTC); after it and OWL-356, Verify ended in one or two passes on OWL-330, OWL-336 and OWL-344.
- The arbiter confirmed 498 findings and rejected 18 (3.5%), while the author answers each finding since OWL-377: the author's triage as the default, the arbiter an opt-in (OWL-391).
- At the resolver's bound, delegation `all` sent every question to the maintainer, who took every recommendation in 28 of 29 replies: the recommendation taken at the bound (OWL-375).
- A Build rewrote its branch's history (OWL-160): refused since OWL-369.
- A review loop's Verify reviewer and arbiter are not given the decider's review the loop answers, only its Build is, so OWL-395's Verify raised a finding against the very change the review asked for, and the arbiter rejected it on other grounds: OWL-396, proposed in Triage.
- A ticket ref kept a finding with both a ruling and a response, which watch could not read for OWL-330 and OWL-344 on 2026-10-20 at 00:14 UTC (`watch_unreadable`): a watch still running a binary older than OWL-377, which first allowed a finding both, refused refs a newer binary had written, and read them once restarted on the new binary at 07:00 UTC. Nothing to file: upgrading `owlshift` means restarting watch.

## GitHub App sign-in (OWL-336)

Designed on 2026-10-20 (OWL-336), on the live checks C9 to C17 run on 2026-10-19 ([Results](#results)). The rules are [architecture](architecture.md) D13, section 6's "GitHub credentials" and section 8's "The GitHub App's tokens", and, above, the forge adapter's "API and credential", "`init`" ("The GitHub sign-in") and "What `doctor` checks" ("The GitHub App"). The token lifetime is branch R of C13: user tokens expire, and the Writer refreshes them with the client ID alone, under the account lock. The design's pull request merges before any issue below starts. Six implementation issues, in order, described here only:

- **GA1 · Add the device-flow client and the `github-app` keychain account.** After OWL-336.
  - *Scope.* In `owlshift-adapters`, a device-flow client on the client ID alone: the device code, the polls (`authorization_pending`, `slow_down`, `access_denied`, `expired_token`) and the refresh grant. The client-ID and slug constants of D13, with the personal file's `github_app_client_id` and `github_app_slug` overrides. In `owlshift_runner::secrets`, the project's `github-app` account beside `github`: `ForgeAccount` and `forge()` extended, no second resolver, `ForgeSecret` saying which kind it found, the value the access token, refresh token and expiry as one entry, in the order of "Which credential a command uses" ("`init`"). `shared_notice`, `forget` and `--replace-secrets` take the account set.
  - *Input.* The production app's client ID and slug: the app `owl-shift`, client ID `Iv23li5EhBqiuljIPbiK`, registered by the maintainer on 2026-10-19 with the settings the checks proved (D13), user-token expiration turned on again before GA1 starts, since C14 ran with it off. GA1's Build asks for them if its ticket does not carry them.
  - *Acceptance.* Unit tests on a scripted transport give C9, C10 and C13's answers and the client handles each; the secrets tests resolve the order on an in-memory keychain; the overrides are read from the personal file.
  - *Estimate.* M.
  - *As built* (OWL-382, 2026-10-20). Input: the maintainer confirmed on 2026-10-20 that user-token expiration is on again for `owl-shift`. `owlshift_adapters::forge::github_app`: `CLIENT_ID`, `SLUG`, `install_link`, and `DeviceFlow` over a `Login` seam (`HttpLogin` in production), one request at a time: `request_code`, `poll` (pending, `slow_down` raising the interval to the answer's if larger, else by 5 s, `access_denied`, `expired_token`) and `refresh`, where any `error` GitHub answers but `temporarily_unavailable` is `AppError::Refused`, the refresh-failure rule's "sign in again"; the wait loop, its display and its deadline (`DeviceCode::expires_at`) are GA2's. `expired_token` and the 5 s step are scripted only, never observed (C10-expired; C10 showed `interval` 10). No error or `Debug` output repeats a token, the device code or an answer's body: only a known error code. `UserToken::to_entry` is the keychain value, compact JSON with a version and expiries in Unix seconds. The overrides are top-level keys of the personal file, `github_app_client_id` (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`) and `github_app_slug` (lowercase letters, digits, inner `-`, which keeps the install link on the app's page), read by `forge::GitHubApp::from_personal`. `secrets::forge` reads `github-app`, then the project's `github`, then the shared `github` (`ForgeKind`, `ForgeSecret::kind`). `shared_notice` is unchanged, and `--replace-secrets` reads the order through `wanted_secrets`: an app token is the project's own, so no shared one is named. The `forget` of the scope sentence has nothing to take: `owlshift forget` opens no keychain (`forget.rs`), so the removal it stood for is GA2's, `--github-token` removing the `github-app` entry. Until GA3, `forge::github` sends the stored access token unrefreshed, and one past its stored expiry is not sent: the command stops naming `owlshift init`, never falling back to a personal token; so GA2 merged before GA3 means a sign-in every 8 hours.
- **GA2 · Sign in to GitHub in `owlshift init`.** After GA1.
  - *Scope.* "`init`", "The GitHub sign-in": the device code shown and polled, C12's installation lookup, the install link and its wait, the pending-request message, `--github-token` for the personal token (`init.rs`'s `GITHUB_TOKEN` spec and `forge.rs`'s `GITHUB_TOKEN_HELP` become the expert option's), `--replace-secrets` and `--skip-secrets`.
  - *Acceptance.* CLI tests on a scripted GitHub: signed in and stored; Cancel; an expired code; the app missing, then installed; the app still missing past the wait, nothing stored; a personal token with `--github-token`, and over a stored app token, which it removes; a stored app token kept while it works, and replaced by a new sign-in once its refresh is refused; an existing install kept.
  - *Estimate.* M.
- **GA3 · Read and write GitHub with the app's token, refreshed under the account lock.** After GA1.
  - *Scope.* `forge::github` builds the adapter from the app's token. Its token source follows `AppTransport` (`tracker/linear/app.rs`): the token behind a lock held across a send and its one retry, refreshed on a stored expiry passed or a 401. The refresh is the Writer's one function, under the account lock: `github-app.lock` beside the project's `lock`, through one lock-file helper drawn out of `Project::lock` (its open and `try_lock`, with a bounded wait as a loop over `try_lock`, longer than the fork window of its note), which `Project::lock` calls too. Under it the keychain is read again, and the new value written before it is used. The refresh-failure rule is a typed forge error naming `owlshift init`. `do` refuses without a GitHub credential.
  - *Acceptance.* Tests on a scripted transport: an expired token refreshed once and the read made again; a 401 with a refresh token stored; two processes refreshing at once, one refresh sent and the other using the stored pair; a refused refresh and a lost rotation each stopping with `owlshift init`; no fallback to a personal token.
  - *Estimate.* L.
- **GA4 · Check the app, its token and its permissions in `owlshift doctor`.** After GA2 and GA3.
  - *Scope.* "What `doctor` checks", "The GitHub App": the `github app`, `github token` and per-permission lines, extending `forge::default_branch` and `forge_access_check` rather than beside them; the `secrets` line naming which credential the project uses; the personal-token warning, always given on a personal token, its list of refused features from the probes, and its word for a token kept because the app cannot be installed.
  - *Acceptance.* Doctor unit tests through `System`, faked: each line's ok, warning and failure, each with its why and fix; a personal token that reads every probe still warned, naming `owlshift init` where the app can be installed and saying that where it cannot, the warning is information that stays; a fine-grained token warned with watch's reading of checks listed.
  - *Estimate.* M.
- **GA5 · Record the GitHub App fixtures and an ignored live test.** After GA3.
  - *Scope.* The answers of the device flow, the refresh and the installation lookups recorded under `crates/owlshift-adapters/tests/fixtures/github/`, secrets scrubbed, and replayed by the adapter's tests. An ignored live test beside `github_live.rs` that signs in at a browser and makes C9 to C15's calls through the adapter. `scripts/github-app-checks.sh` removed.
  - *Acceptance.* The fixtures replay in CI on the three platforms; the live test, run once by the maintainer, passes, and its date is written in C9 to C15's Results; the script is gone.
  - *Estimate.* M.
- **GA6 · Run OWL-336's acceptance on the production app.** After GA4 and GA5.
  - *Scope.* OWL-336's own acceptance, run by the maintainer on the production app of D13, which GA6 only verifies (its settings as D13 states them, the install link from its slug): `owlshift init` on a fresh repository, `owlshift doctor` clean, and watch reading the CI of a pull request Owlshift delivered. It records the two points C11 left unverified: a rollup holding check runs read with the app's token, and the delivered pull request's author, the signed-in user.
  - *Acceptance.* The three runs pass, and their dated results are written in C11's Results and here.
  - *Estimate.* S.

The runner files at most three follow-ups per ticket (`MAX_PER_PARENT`, `proposals.rs`), so OWL-336's Build proposes GA1 to GA3, which land in Triage in OWL-336's Linear project, each blocked by OWL-336. A person files GA4 to GA6 from this list once GA3 merges, with the blockers above as `blocks` relations, and relates OWL-336 to GA6, which carries its acceptance.
